# M7: close the canvas gaps in the CLI (metadata + intelligent sync)

Generated 2026-09-08 18:09 UTC from the verified batch list the implementation queue reads (`routed-batches.json`, 33 batches, 151 rows placed once each), the workflow critic's output, and the blank-map routing (section 10). Baseline: `ryanliu-terum/terum-skills` main at f8557c4 (v0.1.6). Sections 1 and 2 are the workflow synthesizer's text, kept verbatim. Its own batch table was discarded: it re-lettered the verified batches twice (S7b for the ls payload, S7d for status), so it disagreed with the routing, the verifier corrections and the queue, all of which use the ids below. Every batch paragraph in section 4 is the verified design text plus its verifiers' corrections, unparaphrased. Local by design: `.planning/` never leaves this machine.

## 0. The answer in five lines

1. **Teddy's premise, judged by the critic: confirmed for the bulk, contradicted for the greyed controls.** 166 of 196 rows are SIMPLE and 53 of the 62 SIMPLE CLI-side rows need no new field anywhere (Result fields, exports, flags, verb payloads). But "more intelligent sync" is not the lever: a committed field already propagates on the next sync with zero sync code (`safeWrite` re-fetches, every people-file writer re-serialises the parsed document), and ten of the twelve `FRAME_FEATURES` flags (`frames.ts:39-42`) are decisions, permissions or absent mechanisms, not metadata. The full verdict is quoted below.
2. **What M7 is now:** 33 batches, S7a to S7ag. 30 run through the queue, 3 are skipped (S7z MOOT, S7a MOOT, S7ac DONE), 2 open as draft pull requests (S7t, S7ab). 151 rows: 62 of the 62 SIMPLE CLI-side rows, the twelve BM rows from the desktop blank map, 26 AD rows (the adapter and shell halves), the MEDIUM rows Teddy chose to build, and the app-only rows.
3. **Connected surfaces (Teddy, 2026-09-08, binding):** the real app shows only real data. A batch is done when its surface shows real data end to end: CLI half, adapter mapping, mock, and the served-surface flip, in one pull request. A surface whose read model is still a gap stays hidden; the Inbox is the first case. Section 10.8.
4. **Who builds and merges:** Codex (gpt-6-astra) writes every line in a worktree per batch; Claude writes the spec, runs the gates, and merges the PR itself after the checks are green (Teddy, 2026-09-08: "You can merge"). Ryan and Ajay are informed by the PR bodies: 91 rows carry Ryan as the named approver and 20 carry Ajay. Two batches stay drafts: S7t (PF-02 needs Teddy's written yes) and S7ab (Ryan's closed team guard).
5. **From Teddy still:** 60 design-side string rows (his standing call), PF-02 in writing, and the design-walk items in section 6.

**Critic's verdict, verbatim (2026-09-08, read against f8557c4):**

> QUALIFIED - confirmed for reads and for the bulk, contradicted for writes and for precisely the gaps he wants closed. CONFIRMED HALF: the metadata mechanism really is as cheap as he says, and I verified it end to end. safeWrite fetches and hard-resets to origin/main before re-running its mutation (teamRepo.ts:15, :125-126), refreshClone runs per team (sync.ts:147), and every people-file writer re-serializes the PARSED document (install.ts:139, uninstall.ts:106, sync.ts:392, :404, team.ts:408/:418), so a committed field propagates to every other machine on its next sync with zero sync code. personSchema (schema.ts:64), teamSchema.projects (:48), configSchema (:105) and the receipt (receipt.ts, passthrough at every level, schema_version z.literal(1) at :51) are all passthrough, so additions are free and a version bump is never the mechanism. Concretely: 166 of 196 rows are SIMPLE, and 53 of the 62 SIMPLE CLI-side rows need NO new field anywhere - they are Result fields, exports, flags, verb payloads and library reads. That is genuinely 'low cost by updating the metadata'. CONTRADICTED HALF, three ways. (1) 'More intelligent sync' is the wrong lever, and the document's own section 2 says so: sync has no writer for anything new, no names payload and no notion of a machine. Only seven rows spend a new-field allowance at all, and of the three config.json ones, two are refused on the MERITS rather than on price - CP-D1 ('it should not be built at all': team.json already carries `name`, written from the same string at team.ts:525 and published as the README heading) and MC-01 (no field and no flag recommended; the app sets the child's cwd, which lib.rs already does). (2) The gaps that actually block 'full canvas functionality' are not metadata gaps. Of the twelve FRAME_FEATURES flags (frames.ts:39-42), metadata plus sync buys at most `roles` and `projectMembers`. favorites/follow need a ledger sentence retiring D38; perCase/liftOnCards/runEvalInApp need Ajay to amend a standing §12 prohibition; disablePerMachine needs a mechanism that does not exist, because placement IS loading; `progress` has no emitter and the source says so in as many words (frames.ts:22-23, 'Reserved: no verb emits progress yet'); lastSeen/installScope/inviteScoping are refused or deferred. Ten of twelve are decisions, permissions or absent mechanisms - not one is a metadata field. (3) 'Monorepo structure' is not the constraint he thinks it is. The team-repo schema is closed by CODE, not by layout: guard.ts:50 routes every team.json change through guardTeam, which at guard.ts:150-156 admits exactly three shapes and throws. No restructuring and no smarter sync moves that line; it takes Ryan reopening a table the spec calls closed, which is why AC-07, CP-14, TJ-02 and TJ-12 are all refused and why S7ab has to be gated. NET: 'nearly everything is low cost' is true of the bulk and false of the residual he is actually asking about - the greyed controls on the canvas. The M7 document is honest about this in section 0.1 and section 6.1, which are its strongest passages; the premise it is answering is not. The one correction I would make to the document's own framing: it should state plainly that Teddy's gap-cost reading is right about the 166 SIMPLE rows and wrong about the causal claim, rather than opening with 'Not on its own', which reads as a scoping quibble instead of the substantive disagreement it is.

## 1. Definition row and exit criterion

|**M7: close the canvas gaps in the CLI (metadata + intelligent sync)**|every SIMPLE `cli`/`both` row shipped (payload, flag, verb or spec delta merged on `ryanliu-terum/terum-skills`) and its board surface real: from `desktop/src/backend/tauri` where the adapter can reach it, from `mock/` where the row is app-side; every MEDIUM, HARD, CLOSED, DEFERRED and design-side row has a recorded disposition; `npm run check` and `check:design` green, 87/87 locked|

**Exit criterion, amended for connected surfaces (10.8) and for the critic's measurability finding.** M7 is complete when every non-skipped batch in section 4 is merged on `main`; every SIMPLE CLI-side row's field is served by the real adapter and its surface is un-hidden; the mock still passes 87 of 87 locked boards; and each batch's run record carries the real-data proof: the batch's verbs replayed over `--frames` against the offline fixture team (`review/2026-09-08-desktop-blank-map/fixture.sh`) and the recorded frames fed through the adapter's read model in a vitest that asserts the served DTO carries the batch's fields and no design constant. The fidelity oracle proves the mock did not move; the replay proves the real adapter serves the data. S7ab (TJ-02) is outside "M7 complete": it is gated on Ryan reopening a closed table.

## 2. What "metadata + monorepo + intelligent sync" concretely means

Right for reads, wrong for writes. `safeWrite` fetches and hard-resets to `origin/main` before re-running its mutation, and `refreshClone` runs per team (sync.ts:147), so **a new committed field is read by every other machine on its next sync with zero sync code**. What sync lacks is a writer for anything new, a names payload, and any notion of a machine.

**Where sync writes.** People files at install.ts:131-140, uninstall.ts:92-107, sync.ts:385-394 (adopt) and :398-405 (decline), all four rewriting only `installed`/`declined`, plus two writers a verifier found: team.ts:407-418 (join, an object literal) and team.ts:524 (a bare `writeFile` outside safeWrite).

**Per field.** `role`, `bio`, `display_name` and `projects` are owner-written through one verb under guard row b (guard.ts:49 is a whole-file allow with no field check, which is why they cost no guard code on an existing action); sync must never refresh them, or a rename made elsewhere is reverted on the hook. `declined` is append-only and id-keyed. `favorites`/`following` ride the same passthrough but must be `.optional()`: the one additive config field that shipped is `app: appChoiceSchema.optional()` (schema.ts:91-94), and a `.default` materialises the key into every people file on the next unrelated write. team.json fields are refused (`guardTeam` admits three shapes and throws, guard.ts:150-156); TJ-10 works only because `projects[]` is passthrough. Receipts are passthrough with `schema_version: z.literal(1)`, so additions are free and a version bump is never the mechanism.

**Conflict rule.** Mutations are re-applied against a freshly reset tree, so a writer reads its pre-image inside the mutation (sync.ts:387-389); capturing it outside turns field-level last-writer-wins into whole-document.

**Four derivations refused.** Install counts stay derived at read time (readme.ts:40). No inbox event log: the change signal is `run/<team>.stamp` (writeStamp sync.ts:414, called :348), already modelled as `ChangeSource 'stamp'`. No machine registry: `hostname()` appears once, in an uncommitted lock. No lastSeen heartbeat: it would push on every session start, so PF-05 becomes a `git log` derivation.

**The stamp invariant.** A team is stamped only when `!incomplete.has(team) && pendingLeft === 0` (sync.ts:344-348), so undone work no later run can clear withholds the stamp forever and refetches at every session start. Hence CP-S3's absent clone is a SKIP, and `sync --team` is a skip-set, not a filter: `reconcileOrphans` excludes only via `skipTeams` (:373) and otherwise prompts "Adopt orphaned placement at <path>?" (:382), writing a permanent team-readable decline on a no (:403).

**D20.** Three surfaces render app-local state as team truth: the role chip (ShareScreen.tsx:45), Follow, the heart. Each batch landing a committed field deletes its pref in the same PR.

**One widening beyond the costing:** sync returns counts where the seam wants names, so `Backend.sync()` returns empty lists (a dead ternary, tauri/index.ts:78). The names exist one line before the projection to counts. Ryan approves that as a widening, not as RM-31.

**Critic corrections to this section (binding on any spec that cites `sync.ts`, `guard.ts`, `schema.ts` or `team.ts`):**

- METHOD NOTE FIRST: the task asks me to refute from cli-clone, but the document pins its anchors at f8557c4 (cli-main). I checked both. src/commands/sync.ts is BYTE-IDENTICAL between cli-clone (c554ebe) and cli-main (515 lines, `diff` clean), and src/lib/guard.ts is likewise identical (208 lines), so every sync.ts and guard.ts claim below is checkable in either tree. src/lib/schema.ts differs by exactly three lines - the ones adding `app` - so the schema.ts:92/:94 claim is verifiable only on cli-main and would be falsely 'refuted' from cli-clone.
- REFUTED - 'S7d's own change is three members on StatusResult (status.ts:16-23)'. Wrong interface. status.ts:23 is `export interface StatusResult { version: string | null; teams: TeamStatus[]; }` - it has exactly two members and neither is per-team. The three costed members (RM-41 `pending`, RM-48 `syncedAt`, RM-S1 `policy`) are all PER-TEAM and belong on `TeamStatus` at status.ts:17-22. Line 16 is `StatusArgs`. Consequence: a spec written from this sentence hangs per-team values off the top level, where they cannot be per-team, and RM-41/RM-48's shared OD-4 paragraph (which says 'one TypeScript member on the existing TeamStatus interface') would be contradicted by its own implementation.
- REFUTED - 'team.ts:524 (a bare `writeFile` outside safeWrite)'. Line 524 is `await git('config', 'user.email', identity.email);`. The bare `writeFile` calls are at team.ts:527 (team.json) and :529 (people/<handle>.json), and both sit inside `team create`'s STAGING-DIRECTORY scaffold, before the repository's first commit exists - there is no safeWrite for them to be outside of. Listing this among 'two writers a verifier found' inflates the people-file write surface a new field must be threaded through: the real people-file writers are install.ts:131-140, uninstall.ts:92-107, sync.ts:385-394, sync.ts:398-405 and team.ts:407-418, and all five re-serialize the PARSED document, which is exactly why a passthrough field survives.
- REFUTED - 'sync must never refresh them [role, bio, display_name, projects], or a rename made elsewhere is reverted on the hook.' Nothing in sync.ts refreshes profile fields; sync's two people-file writers (sync.ts:385-394, :398-405) touch only `installed` and `declined`. The actual revert hazard is `team join`, at team.ts:410-411, which overwrites `display_name` and `email` from config identity on every join and reclaim - and it is scoped to those two fields only: `bio` is preserved at team.ts:414 (`existing?.bio ?? ''`) and any new passthrough field survives via the `...(existing ?? {})` spread at team.ts:408. The doc names the wrong verb and three fields too many, which mis-locates the very risk PF-07's costed delta was written to close.
- REFUTED (over-priced) - S7e's '`--team` and the absent clone each need a new `skipped` reason.' The `TeamOutcome` skipped arm at sync.ts:37 already carries `'unreachable' | 'locked' | 'busy' | 'error' | 'fresh'`, and CP-S3's own costed spec delta says an absent clone should be 'classed with the unreachable teams', printing `Skipping <team>: <clone> is missing` - i.e. reuse `unreachable`, add nothing. Only `--team` (a deliberate exclusion, not a failure) genuinely lacks a reason. CP-S3's PREMISE is confirmed, though: sync.ts:153 rethrows anything that is neither RemoteAccessError nor CloneBusy, so an absent clone does abort the whole run today.
- CORRECTED (line numbers, claims stand) - 'an unreadable clone returns failure(..., {version, teams}) (status.ts:99)': line 99 is a closing brace; the two failure-with-value returns are status.ts:100 and :101. 'a failing result frame drops its value (run.ts:72-79)': the else-branch is run.ts:72-75 (`finish({ ok: false, error }, …)` never passes `frame.value`); :76-77 close the case. 'every result goes through one schema.parse (tauri/index.ts:44)': there are TWO parse sites, `run` at :44 and `read` at :48. All three substantive claims hold.
- UNVERIFIED AND LOAD-BEARING - section 0.1's headline. The 12 FRAME_FEATURES keys and the doc's 12-way accounting are exact (frames.ts:39-42: favorites, follow, roles, lastSeen, installScope, inviteScoping, disablePerMachine, projectMembers, liftOnCards, runEvalInApp, perCase, progress). But nothing in frames.ts says whether `roles` means PF-03's self-declared discipline chip or TJ-02's Admin/Member permission role. If it is TJ-02, M7 flips ONE flag, not two, and the entire five-line answer rests on S7ab - the one batch whose route the bar refuses. Resolve this before the doc goes to Teddy; the whole 'not on its own' framing depends on it.
- CONFIRMED, DO NOT RE-CHECK - these verified exact at f8557c4 (and, where noted, identically in cli-clone): guard.ts:49 is a whole-path row-b allow with no field inspection, and :50 routes every team.json change to guardTeam; guard.ts:150-156 admits exactly rows c/d/e then throws; guard.ts:10 GuardAction and :36 PEOPLE_ACTIONS=['join','install','uninstall','sync']; schema.ts:21 `skillIdSchema = z.uuid()` (so S7t's 'favorites cannot hold favorite-project:<key>' is right); schema.ts:48 projects passthrough (so TJ-10's route is real); schema.ts:63 `declined` id-keyed; schema.ts:92/:94 `app: appChoiceSchema.optional()` is the one additive config field shipped; teamRepo.ts:15 and :125-126 fetch then `reset --hard origin/main`; sync.ts:147 refreshClone per team; sync.ts:161-186 pending replay; sync.ts:344 stamp predicate and :348 the call; sync.ts:414 writeStamp definition; sync.ts:373 skipTeams, :382 the exact 'Adopt orphaned placement at <path>?' string, :403 the permanent decline; sync.ts:387-389 pre-image read inside the mutation; sync.ts:34-39 TeamOutcome, shipped in 55ef7f0 (confirmed by `git log -S TeamOutcome`); readme.ts:40 installCounts derived at read time; receipt.ts:51 `schema_version: z.literal(1)` with passthrough at every level; hook.ts:190 the single `hostname()` inside acquireTeamLock's uncommitted lock record; install.ts:241 the POSIX-suffix `placementHome` bug with its only call site at :106; update.ts:12 `Result<void>`, :28 the exact else-if MC-12 targets, :35 `success(undefined)`; tauri/index.ts:29 `cliValidate` is the only closed zod object, :61-69 the nine gap()s, :72 the install ternary that falls through to the single-skill form, :78 a genuinely dead ternary (both arms `[]`), :85 the update gap; frames.ts:22-23 'no verb emits progress yet' in the source itself, :44-47 isDecline.
- CONFIRMED AND STRONGER THAN THE DOC SAYS - S7ac's residual. app.ts:88 creates `~/.terum/skills/app/` and downloads the release bundle into `app/<version>/`, while uninstallMachine.ts:135 iterates exactly `['run','cache','teams','quarantine']` plus store.root. So `terum-skills uninstall` leaves the entire downloaded desktop bundle on disk after telling the user it removed terum-skills from this machine. Two refinements for the fix: the loop uses `rmdir`, which only removes EMPTY directories (ENOTEMPTY prints 'Kept …'), so adding 'app' to the list is necessary but not sufficient - the bundle directory will never be empty; and the app-state file itself (`run/app.json`, app.ts:133) already IS swept, so the leak is the payload, not the pointer.

## 3. Where M7 sits now that M5 and M6 have shipped

The M6 prerequisite is not a library entry. `package.json` declares only `bin`, `files` and `version` (no `main`, no `exports`, verified by the critic), so the app can only drive the CLI as a child process: it spawns `node <entry> --frames <verb>` (`desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:42-48`), finds the CLI through `~/.terum/skills/run/app.json`, and reads the verb's result frame `value` (`src/lib/frames.ts:24`, `:171-175`). RM-11 (the exports map) is off every path and S7a is retired. Every widened Result member reaches the app the moment its batch merges, provided the adapter maps it, which is why every batch now carries its app half.

Ten read models still return a hard-coded gap on the real adapter (`desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:61-69`, `:85`). Each returns in the batch that first serves it from real data, and the surface is un-hidden in that same PR (10.8):

| gap() read model | served by | fields filled later by |
|---|---|---|
| `status` | S7k (AD-20) | S7k rows (pending, syncedAt, policy, clone path, join block, ledger, identity, tools); TJ-12 categories |
| `settings` | S7k (AD-24) | S7g (placements, AD-23), S7l (hook), S7s (storage), S7e (update advice), S7c (account write half) |
| `library` | S7f (AD-21) | S7g (health, tracked), S7f rows (description, grants, installers, last-changed, projects) |
| `skill` | S7f (AD-22) | S7n (receipt), S7m (validate findings), S7f (SKILL.md body, RM-01) |
| `receipts` | S7n (EV-13 `receipt` verb) | S7m (EV-08, EV-11), S7u (per_case, EV-03) |
| `inbox` | S7r (IB-01, the feed; app-only) | derivable rows first: S7f (BM-04 declined), S7n (IB-02), S7o (IB-06), S7m (IB-05); the Inbox stays hidden until S7r serves it |
| `catalog` | S7b (AD-26) | S7f (BM-01 projects), S7h (host reads) |
| `roster` | S7b (AD-25) | S7h (admin column, invitations), S7b rows (role, projects) |
| `onboarding` | S7r (first-run routing rule and a screen that calls `backend.setup`) | S7ad (join target, BM-12), S7ae (progress frames) |
| `update` | S7e (RM-08 returns a report) | none |

Two adapter facts define "real", and both are S7af's first items: a failing result frame drops its `value` (`run.ts:72-75`), so any payload that rides a failure (status with one unreadable team, RM-19's partial installs) reaches the app only after AD-01; and one malformed row fails a whole call, since every result goes through `schema.parse` at two sites (`tauri/index.ts:44` and `:48`), so every new key is declared on the closed zod objects rather than left to passthrough.

**First end-to-end run.** Only three platforms have a build (`aarch64.app.tar.gz`, `x64.app.tar.gz`, `arm64-setup.exe`; `platform.ts:15-33`), produced by `release.yml`'s `desktop` matrix. The first real run is on the Windows ARM host after `gh auth login` there: `npx -y terum-skills@latest app` installs the NSIS build and launches it, and the app spawns `--frames status`. Today that renders the gap board; after S7af it renders a sidebar with every gap surface hidden, and after S7f and S7k the Library, Skill detail, status footer and Settings appear from real data. Proof on this box is the headless replay described in section 1; macOS is proven by the CI runner plus the checksum-verified install (`app.ts:96-126`). A release without Ryan: bump `package.json` on `main` and dispatch `release.yml` with `dry_run: true`; the desktop bundles and the npm tarball land as workflow artifacts. A public `npx` release still needs the `npm` environment's only reviewer, Ryan.

Cite that as the precedent rather than search.test.ts alone, because it is the same file and the same author.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** THREE CORRECTIONS; the rest of the appHalf stands. (1) DELETE the CP-20 clause entirely - CP-20 is a SIMPLE·app row already placed in S7k's appHalf, and carrying it here too is the plan's only double-placement. (2) RM-51's author→person join predicate is at src/commands/ls.ts:68, not :71 - verified this session as skills.filter((skill) => normalizeAuthor(skill.author) === normalizeAuthor(`${member.display_name} <${member.email}>`)); ls.ts:71 is the 'Installed:' print line. (3) The staleness sentence the deleted CP-20 clause cited as ls.ts:44 is at ls.ts:150 ('Team status is from local clones and may be stale; open endorsement requests are not checked.'); ls.ts:44 is io.print(`Local skills: …`). CONNECTED (10.8): AD-21 and AD-22 land in this PR; the Library and Skill detail surfaces flip to served; undelivered fields are null, never a design constant.
- AD-21: report step 3: implement `backend.library({scope})` from `ls`, `ls project <name>`, `ls --local` (`installed`) and `status` (`origin`) with the pixel-neutral screen half; honest defaults (`desc:''` until RM-30, `size:'-'`, `tokensK:0`, `wlt:null`, `summary:null`, `favorite:false`); flips the Library surface
- AD-22: report step 4: implement `backend.skill({ref})` from `ls` + `ls --local` + `status` + `validate` (bare pass/fail); never default `favorites`, `lines`, `files`, `grants`, per-check hygiene rows or any synthesized receipt; `receipt:null` lands the evals tab on the drawn 'No receipt yet' board; flips the Skill detail surface

**Sync rule.** All five are read-only. `updated` is taken from committed history and is identical on every clone at the same commit, so it needs no propagation rule; it is deliberately NOT `installed[].since` (per-person) or `placements[].placed_at` (per-machine), neither of which answers "when did this skill last change".

**Tests.** `ls.test.ts`: `LsSkill` carries the SKILL.md description verbatim and the printed line is unchanged; carries normalized grants and their sha256, with `null` grants for a malformed `allowed-tools` that does not fail the listing; one malformed folder costs one row and one reported line, never the roster, the other skills, or the exit code (the `ls` twin of `search.test.ts:155`). `readme.test.ts`: `latestChange` returns the committer date of the last commit touching a skill folder and degrades to an em dash; `installersById` lists two scopes for one person while `installCounts` still counts that person once. `search.test.ts`: the three twins.

**Rationale and risk.** The first of three ls batches, split by the STRUCTURE each touches so no two of them run concurrently on `src/commands/ls.ts`: S7f owns `LsSkill`/`SearchHit`/`LsResult`, S7g owns `LocalSection['rows']`, S7h owns the `--host` flag. Splitting by structure rather than by row keeps each batch reviewable and keeps the file single-owner at every moment. RM-49 is a BEHAVIOUR change on repositories that are healthy today, and both halves are deliberate: a folder whose frontmatter `name` disagrees with its directory is now reported and SKIPPED by `ls`, `ls member` and `ls project` - `skillRecords`, the README reader and `findSkill` all already refuse it, so `ls` was advertising skills `install` would not resolve - and a skipped people file no longer hides the roster, though its member's install counts become ABSENT rather than zero and must be reported as such. The README generator keeps its fail-closed read (§9): a derived artifact that silently drops a skill is worse than one that refuses to regenerate.

CORRECTION 6 - RM-49's people-file half is under-specified and, done naively, breaks the very §9 fail-closed read the risk section promises to keep. `ls` reads the roster through `readPeople` (src/commands/ls.ts:34), which is `Promise.all(files.map(... parseJson(personSchema, ...)))` at src/lib/readme.ts:142-144 - one malformed people file rejects the whole call, so `ls` fails whole today. But `readReadmeData` calls the SAME `readPeople` at readme.ts:128, and that reader is the fail-closed one (it throws on a folder/frontmatter name mismatch at readme.ts:135). Tolerance must therefore be added AT THE `ls` CALL SITE, never inside `readPeople`. The precedent is again in-family and file-local: search.ts:37 already does per-file `readPerson(...).catch(...)` + print + `.filter(person => person !== undefined)` under the comment "one unreadable member file is reported and skipped, never fatal for the team". Cite that, not just ls.ts:59.

CORRECTION 7 - RM-49's skills half has a cheaper, already-written implementation the cliChanges does not name. `ls` hand-rolls its enumeration (readdir ls.ts:50 + parseSkillFrontmatter :53 + throw :54), whereas `skillRecords` (src/lib/skills.ts:20-39) already does BOTH halves of RM-49: it skips a bad folder through `options.onProblem` (:34-36) AND enforces the folder-name/frontmatter-name match the risk section describes - `if (parsed.data.name !== name) throw new Error(\`Skill folder ${name} does not match frontmatter name ${parsed.data.name}.\`)` (skills.ts:32) - which is also exactly why `findSkill` refuses such a folder (skills.ts:47-48). Routing `listSkills` through `skillRecords(clone, team, { onProblem: ... })` - the same call search.ts:35 makes - delivers RM-49's skills half and RM-30's `description` (SkillRecord carries `frontmatter`) in one edit, instead of duplicating the check inside ls.ts.

ONE CONSTRAINT TO ADD - both consumers must stay prompt-free. The adapter's `read()` cancels the process and fails on ANY ask: "terum-skills asked \"<question>\" during a read-only call; the desktop app never answers questions on your behalf" (desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:46-53). `ls` is not yet in the adapter at all (only search and validate are real reads, index.ts:70 and :83), so when the app starts driving `ls`, note that `selectTeam(..., args.team, args.form)` at ls.ts:29 can prompt on a multi-team config - the app must always pass `--team`, or `ls` must be driven through `run()` rather than `read()`.
- Lens 2 (corrections apply): S7f's five rows are real, read-only, and D20-clean, and every re-anchoring in `cliChanges` is correct - but the batch is not shippable as written. Six material corrections, three anchor fixes.

MATERIAL

1. `dependsOn: ["S7a"]` is a dead edge - CUT IT. S7a is MOOT per the delta: RM-11's exports map never shipped (`/tmp/.../cli-main/package.json:14-22` declares only `type`, `bin`, `files` - no `main`, `exports` or `types`) and the app reaches the CLI as a child process over frames, not as an import. S7f needs no library entry: its payload arrives as a verb's `result.value` on the wire (frames.ts:24 `ResultFrame{verb,ok,exitCode,error?,declined?,value?}`). Re-point to `dependsOn: []`. Nothing else in S7f depends on S7a's surviving half (CP-19 is a design-side printed-command gate that S7f does not touch).

2. The appHalf's RM-51 clause is UNIMPLEMENTABLE as costed and must move to `cliChanges`. Costing §3 (line 1269) specifies the author→person join as "written once in the Backend adapter … enumerate with skillRecords … read every people/<handle>.json". The shipped adapter cannot read files: `desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:137` registers exactly five commands - `cli_spawn, cli_write, cli_kill, read_app_state, host_platform` - and `read_app_state` reads only `~/.terum/skills/run/app.json` (lib.rs:113-122). There is no generic file read, and AGENTS.md invariant 1 forbids fs access outside `src/backend/` anyway. So the join must ship as a CLI-side payload on the `ls` result (per-skill author identity, or the buckets themselves), exactly as the delta forced on S7d's three "exported reader" rows. Correcting RM-51's line number while leaving it in the appHalf preserves a plan that cannot be built.

3. RM-38 has no seam DTO home, and "Recently updated" is not a sort. `SkillCard` (desktop/src/backend/types.ts:14) carries no last-changed field; neither does the fixture's `SKILLS` (src/fixtures/schema.ts:19) nor `CATALOG` (:21) - only `PROJECTS` has `updated` (:26). And the drawn control is a static default label, not a comparator: `SearchRow({…, sort='Recently updated', …})` in desktop/src/components/domain/Primitives.tsx:30, with no sort implementation anywhere in src/screens. So RM-38's app half is three things the batch does not scope - a new `SkillCard` field, a mock value for it on every fixture-derived card (AGENTS.md invariant 2: the mock renders every board as drawn), and an actual sort. Either state all three in the appHalf or split RM-38 into its own batch; the CLI half alone changes no pixel.

4. The `risk` paragraph describes a check `ls` does not have. `listSkills` reads each folder and refuses only on `!parsed.ok` (src/commands/ls.ts:54); it NEVER compares frontmatter `name` to the directory. The mismatch refusal lives in `skillRecords` (src/lib/skills.ts:32) and the README reader (src/lib/readme.ts:135). So RM-49 in `ls` ADDS a check that does not exist today - it does not merely change an existing failure mode. Rewrite the risk sentence accordingly; as phrased ("is now reported and SKIPPED") a Codex run would look for a check to soften and find none.

5. The "skipped people file" half of RM-49 is unspecified and touches the README path. `readPeople` (src/lib/readme.ts:142-144) is `Promise.all(files.map(… parseJson(personSchema, …)))` - one malformed file rejects the whole call, which ls.ts:33 turns into a whole-verb failure via the outer catch (ls.ts:46). `readPeople` has exactly two non-test callers: ls.ts:33 and src/lib/readme.ts:128. ### S7g. ls --local rows: health, tracked, and the ledger references

- **Rows:** RM-39, RM-40, RM-44, AD-23
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7f
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** M / Ryan
- **Decisions applied (Teddy, 2026-09-08):** D-BM-6 = A: RM-44 ships the subset (placement {id, team, version}, shared {id, team}[]); the sharedState producer / reconcileShared refactor is a later Ryan PR only if the Sharing state chip must light.

**CLI change.** `src/commands/ls.ts` - `LocalSection['rows']` (:19, today EXACTLY `{name, path, state, problem?}`) gains: `health: 'ok'|'update-available'|'local-changed'|'both'|'placement-missing'|'untracked'|'unknown'` (RM-39 - a pure comparison of three fingerprints, the one on the ledger entry, the one taken from the placed folder, and the one taken from the tree the clone holds NOW; it performs no write, takes no lock and moves nothing, `quarantineDrift` remains the only thing that acts on a local-changed answer, and it is a genuinely NEW comparison because for a placement pinned to a version sync compares against the MATERIALIZED PINNED TREE and therefore never asks whether the team's current tree moved; a clone the row cannot read costs that row's health alone); `tracked: boolean` (RM-40 - the boolean the command already computes at ls.ts:131 to decide which folders it lists, exposed as a value so a caller does not have to match the English sentence "untracked locally", which exists to be read by a person); and the ledger references it already resolved - `shared: {id, team}[]`, `placement?: {id, team, version}`, plus `sharedState: 'in-sync'|'local-edit'|'repo-ahead'|'diverged'|'not-connected'` (RM-44). `src/lib/skills.ts` - `sharedState` is factored out of §5.3's reconciler as a read-only helper digesting with the existing `canonicalDigest` (skills.ts:87), and `src/commands/connect.ts`'s reconciler CALLS it, so the two cannot drift and §5.3's decision procedure is unchanged by the relocation. THE PRINTED OUTPUT IS UNCHANGED in all three rows: the state sentences remain the §6 contract, and the structured fields exist so a host need not parse them. `ls --local` remains no git, no network, no prompt, no write - it reads two folder trees per connected row, which is filesystem work only.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** RM-05, RM-06, IB-10, plus the batch's own rows. RM-05: read `~/.terum/skills/quarantine/<timestamp>/<name>` - the directory name reverses `moveToQuarantine`'s `[:.]`→`-` substitution (src/lib/placer.ts:170-171), with the folder mtime as a cross-check - per-machine recovery data that is never committed, so there is no team-wide truth to fork, and only `sync --prune` deletes there (sync.ts:499-515). RM-06: one row per `config.placements` key, `version === null` → "tracking" and otherwise a fixed-width prefix, footer counts derived by `scope.kind` over the ledger itself. IB-10: the app computes its own three extra flag keys with no CLI change and no write - DUPLICATE (two `config.placements` keys sharing one id; the hover names both paths and points at `uninstall-skill`), COLLISION (the honest name for "shadowed": a folder name present in BOTH `LsResult.local` sections), and BROKEN (IB-05's HYG7 finding, once S7m lands). ONE rendering rule is load-bearing: `repo-ahead` must render as a PENDING DESTRUCTIVE ACTION, not as a quieter `in-sync`, because the next sync overwrites the local source and moves the previous one to quarantine; `diverged` is advisory until the user runs `connect --keep-source <id>` or `connect --keep-repo <id>`, which remain the only resolutions. Delete GAPS.md:43's clause.
- AD-23: report step 10's adapter half: map `placement {id,team,version}`, `shared {id,team}`, `tracked`, `health` from `ls --local` rows into Settings, This machine and into `installed`/`version` for steps 3 and 4 (no regex over prose)

**Sync rule.** Read-only by construction, and that is the point of RM-39: the app can ask what state a placement is in WITHOUT running the sync that would change it. §6's local-changed / blocked vocabulary is unchanged - this only gives it a read-only reading.

**Tests.** `ls.test.ts`: `--local` reports `ok` when ledger, placed copy and clone tree agree, and `update-available` when only the clone moved; marks a folder with no shared ref and no placement `tracked: false` and one with a connected source `true`; carries the SharedRef team and the PlacementRef version while the printed sentence is byte-identical. `skills.test.ts`: `sharedState` agrees with the reconciler on all five arms.

**Rationale and risk.** Second ls batch. The dependency on S7f is SERIALIZATION ONLY - same file, different interface - so the two must not run concurrently. Placed after S7f because the local section is the smaller surface and the skill payload is what the Library and Marketplace need first. `sharedState`'s relocation touches `src/commands/connect.ts`, the only file in this batch that also carries a decline site RM-09 (S7d) tags - S7d lands earlier, so the two edits are sequential rather than concurrent, but the reviewer should read connect.ts as touched by both.

The value must distinguish "unpinned, sync will update" from "pinned, the team's tree moved". As drawn this is app-visible state the tool will not act on.

CORRECTIONS, most material first.

(1) THE APP HALF'S RM-05 AND RM-06 ARE NOT IMPLEMENTABLE IN THE SHIPPED SHELL. The batch says the app "reads ~/.terum/skills/quarantine/<timestamp>/<name>" and renders "one row per config.placements key". The Tauri surface exposes exactly five commands - cli_spawn, cli_write, cli_kill, read_app_state, host_platform (desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:137) - and four plugins: opener, store, clipboard-manager, window-state (lib.rs:134-140). There is NO fs plugin and no fs permission (desktop/src-tauri/capabilities/default.json lists core:default, opener, store, clipboard-manager, window-state and nothing else). read_app_state reads exactly one path, $HOME/.terum/skills/run/app.json (lib.rs:113-122). So the app cannot readdir the quarantine root, cannot stat it for sizes, and cannot read config.json. Their costed mechanism - "through the CLI's ConfigStore under the sidecar, or the file directly while mocked" (costing:1259) - is MOOT for the same reason S7d's exported readers are: package.json is still bin-only, no main/exports/types (cli-main/package.json:14-22). Both rows must be re-specced as a verb payload (placements and quarantine rows riding an ls/status Result) or as a new native command. Either is work this batch does not carry, and the verb-payload route adds a fourth CLI-side row to a three-row batch.

The costing names DUPLICATE, COLLISION and **OVERSIZED** - the HYG6 size warning read off `assessment.warnings` membership - with four explicit must-nots (do not key on ValidateResult.warnings>0; do not key on code==='HYG6', which names both the size WARNING at hygiene.ts:95 and the empty-description ERROR at :97; do not call assessHygiene bare because it throws HygieneRefused; do not sync to freshen the clone, D-R4) (costing:1251). HYG7 DOES NOT EXIST: `export type HygieneCode = 'HYG1' | 'HYG2' | 'HYG3' | 'HYG4' | 'HYG5' | 'HYG6';` (src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts:6, identical at 85cc276 and f8557c4). That converts a zero-dependency, zero-CLI-change, no-approver app row into one gated on an Ajay-approved §9 eval-engine delta living in S7m - an undeclared cross-batch and cross-approver dependency (dependsOn is ["S7f"] only; the batch's approver is "Ryan"). - **Critic and follow-up notes (binding):** Critic (refuted the synthesis): RM-41 `pending`, RM-48 `syncedAt` and RM-S1 `policy` are PER-TEAM members and belong on `TeamStatus` (`status.ts:17-22`), never on `StatusResult` (`:23`, two members: `version`, `teams`). The two failure-with-value returns are `status.ts:100` and `:101`. **CLI change.** APPEND a fourth member beside pending/syncedAt/policy, from the same readTeam(clone) the archived-membership check already performs: categories: string[] | null (TJ-12). teamSchema.categories is already required at src/lib/schema.ts:46, so there is no schema line and no additional read, and null follows the same rule as policy for an absent, foreign or incomplete clone. This is what makes the drawn Team-policy Categories row render team.json's own array instead of the catalogue's frontmatter-derived list - the two lists overlap in only four keys, and the tool writes an eighth default, misc, into skills that declare none. The printed block stays unchanged line for line.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** CP-20's VALUE (the last-sync timestamp the three drawn surfaces print) and Settings ▸ Machine's mid-operation state. THREE reading rules the app must implement, not infer. (1) `syncedAt` is rendered VERBATIM, including a stamp dated in the future - the skew rule that makes such a stamp not fresh belongs to `stale` and must not silently rewrite the timestamp. (2) `null` does NOT mean "never synced": `team leave` deletes the stamp with the rest of the team's run artifacts (`removeRunArtifacts`, hook.ts:256), so a rejoined team reads null while being perfectly current - render it as a freshness DISCLOSURE, never a success receipt. (3) A non-empty `pending` beside an old `syncedAt` is the honest signal that the last run left work undone, because a team with pending work is not stamped (sync.ts:344-347); its only remedy is `sync`, and it is not an error and not a failure of the last command. Team policy renders read-only on the Team card, changed by PR - the app never offers to write it, because `team.json` is reachable only through guard rows c/d/e. Delete GAPS.md's staleness clause. CONNECTED (10.8): AD-20 and AD-24 land in this PR; status and Settings flip to served.
- AD-20: report step 1: implement `backend.status()` from `status` (`teams[0].{team,handle,repository,members[].displayName,memberCount,sharedSkills}`, `version`), `ls --local` (`counts.Global`) and `host_platform()` (`machine.os`); omit `counts.Pushes/Updates/Alerts` and every project key until real; `gh_login`/`me.email`/`clone`/`last_sync`/`stamp` empty until their rows land; flips the status surface
- AD-24: report step 12: implement `backend.settings()` from `ls --local` + `status` + board constants with the honest defaults (`PLACEMENTS[].state`/`placed` '-', `APPROVALS []`, `QUARANTINE []`, `CLI_LATEST '-'`, `HOOK.installed false`); stop folding `catalog()` into settings; flips the Settings surface

**Sync rule.** Read-only. `syncedAt` means "this team's last FULLY SUCCESSFUL sync" because the stamp is written per team only after that team's run completed with no deferred work and no remaining pending entry - it is not a claim that anything changed.

**Tests.** `status.test.ts`: reports a team's pending entries on the Result even when its clone is absent; reports no pending entries for a team that has none; returns the team policy block and adds NO printed line; leaves `policy` null for an absent, foreign and incomplete clone. `src/lib/__tests__/hook.test.ts`: `stampedAt` returns the stamp mtime as ISO and null when the stamp is absent, and reports a future-dated stamp verbatim while `stale` is unchanged.

**Rationale and risk.** A parallel lane owning `src/commands/status.ts` alone. It is the batch that gives the app every machine-local fact it currently invents, and it is where the app's `StatusResult` DTO (today `{machine, me, teams, counts}` against the CLI's `{version, teams}`) starts converging on the real shape. `src/lib/hook.ts` is shared with S7l, which is why S7l depends on this batch. Nothing else in the plan touches status.ts.

**3. Either add a fourth registration (which pushes the flag count to four and makes the MEDIUM label unavoidable) or - better, and what the delta actually implies - drop the flag entirely for the app and run one `ls --local` process per root with the child's cwd set per spawn (lib.rs:44-46). That is an ADAPTER change: today `cwd` is one global preference read once (`backend.prefs.get<string>('workspace','')`, index.ts:41) and passed to every run() and read(); per-root reads need a cwd argument on read().

**4. **11. `login --set` names keys inconsistently with itself and with the schema.** cliChanges says it accepts "exactly `name`/`email`/`default-handle`"; the tests field says "`--set display_name` writes only that key". The config keys are `display_name`, `email`, `default_handle` (schema.ts:96-98). Pick the surface spelling and state the mapping explicitly, or Codex will implement one and test the other.

**12. The printed consequence is overstated.** cliChanges/syncRule: `login --set` "Changes `metadata.author` on every skill you author at the next sync". Author IS derived from identity (`const author = \`${config.display_name} <${config.email}>\``, connect.ts:179, :247, :352), so the direction is right - but the managed-field refresh runs only over skills tracked in `config.shared` on THIS machine, through reconcileShared (connect.ts:340-369, called from sync.ts:166-173) under the narrow guard exception (guard.ts:127,134). A skill you authored that is not connected on this machine keeps the recorded author. Since the batch requires the verb to PRINT this consequence, print the true one.

**13. `--label` on `team join` collides with the existing `--as <name>`.** cli.ts:71-72 already registers `--as <name>` ("local team name (default: the repository name)") - i.e. the KEY. Adding `--label` puts two name-ish flags on one subcommand. That belongs in the risk field alongside the two-registration point, and in the sentence Ryan is asked to approve: `--as` sets the identifier, `--label` sets the display string, absence of `--label` shows the key.

**14. D20 is not violated, but the adjacent hazard should be recorded, and the sizing is optimistic.** D20 forbids app-local state shown as team-wide; `label` is CLI-machine-local, written by a CLI verb, and the appHalf's persisted team selection lives in the app's own prefs (S7r) - neither is team state, so no violation. What IS worth one recorded sentence: a machine-local `label` is drawn where the boards show the workspace's name, so two teammates see different names for one repo while the generated README heading keeps the committed `team.json.name` (readme.ts:71). Record WHY the committed field is unavailable - guardTeam permits only rows c/d/e and `name` is untouchable after create (guard.ts:150-157), which is also why the costing marks the team.json owner section REFUSED - and have the UI present it as a local alias. Finally, size: as written this is ~5 CLI source + ~8 CLI test files PLUS ~10 app files across seam DTOs, adapter, mock and three screens, spanning two packages with separate CI. That is not one Codex run and one PR. Split: PR 1 = CLI (`label` + `login --set`), PR 2 = app half, gated on the status/settings batch. Related: CP-19 (the design-side printed-command gate orphaned by S7a's mootness) is the same subject matter as CP-43 and is the natural row to re-place here once the app half is real - decide that explicitly rather than leaving it unhomed.

- **Rows:** PF-03, PF-04, PF-07, PF-09, AD-25, AD-26, CP-19

- **Decisions applied (Teddy, 2026-09-08):** A8 (Teddy 2026-09-08): CP-19's three refused strings stay design-side; the hello-frame verbs/features inventory gate ships here (CP-19 moved from the retired S7a). Connected: AD-25/AD-26 in this PR.
- **Critic and follow-up notes (binding):** Critic: the revert hazard for the new people-file fields is not sync (its two writers at `sync.ts:385-394` and `:398-405` touch only `installed` and `declined`) but `team join` at `team.ts:410-411`, which rewrites `display_name` and `email` from config identity on every join and reclaim while preserving `bio` at `:414`; thread `role` and `projects` through the same preservation. The bare `writeFile` calls at `team.ts:527` and `:529` sit inside `team create`'s staging scaffold before the first commit, so they are not a second write surface. Critic: `FRAME_FEATURES.roles` (`frames.ts:39-42`) is ambiguous between PF-03's discipline label and TJ-02's permission role; flip it here only if the seam's `roles` capability means PF-03, otherwise leave it false and say so in the PR body.

**CLI change.** `src/lib/schema.ts` - `personSchema` (:54-64, `.passthrough()`) gains `role: z.string().max(32).default('')` after `bio` (:61) and `projects: z.array(z.string().min(1)).default([])` after `declined` (:63). `src/lib/guard.ts` - ONE change of the blessed shape, paid once for three rows: `GuardAction` (:10, today 8 members) gains `'profile'` and `PEOPLE_ACTIONS` (:36, today `['join','install','uninstall','sync']`) gains it, after which row (b) at guard.ts:49 (`path === \`people/${context.handle}.json\` && PEOPLE_ACTIONS.includes(context.action)`) admits it UNCHANGED - the guard authorizes the PATH, never the field set, which is the whole reason a new person field is cheap. New `src/commands/profile.ts`: `profile [--name] [--bio] [--role <role>] [--project <name>]` writing ONLY self-describing fields - `email`, `github` and `handle` are refused, because the first two are the §5.4 reclaim evidence a second machine is matched against and the third is the binding identity, immutable once the people file exists; `--project` validates each name against `team.json`'s project registry read from the PRE-IMAGE tree inside the mutation, so the roster can never chip a project that does not exist; `--name` ALSO refreshes `config.display_name`. New `src/commands/decline.ts`: `decline <ref>` records the id in the actor's own `declined` under the EXISTING `'uninstall'` action (zero guard-code change - 'uninstall' is already in PEOPLE_ACTIONS; only the §6.0 row-(b) "Permitted on" spec cell gains `decline`), refuses a ref no automatic path would place, is scope-free, and is idempotent (an id already listed produces no tree change and therefore no commit). It exists because `uninstall-skill <ref>` resolves its targets from `installed` + `config.placements`, so on a team-endorsed never-installed skill it resolves zero targets and is a silent exit-0 no-op. `src/cli.ts` - two verb registrations in the M2 block (after :122). `src/lib-entry.ts` - two sorted one-line appends.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** APPEND two obligations. (1) PF-05 becomes 'Last active' and is DERIVED at sync from the person's people-file last commit - git log -1 --format=%cI -- people/<handle>.json, the same one-pass shape as PF-06's joined derivation and on the same full unshallow clone - with NO new field, no heartbeat and no committed timestamp (Teddy's decision (c)). Absent history renders an em dash, never 'never'. (2) D20 retirement is a per-PR obligation, not a count: this batch DELETES prefs.get('role:'+m.handle, …) at desktop/src/screens/share/ShareScreen.tsx:45 in the same PR that lands the field, and S7l deletes sync:hook at SettingsContent.tsx:22 in its own. A real field shipped beside a surviving localStorage key leaves two sources of truth for one control.
- AD-25: report step 13: implement `backend.roster()` from `status` members (+ PF-03 role, PF-04 projects when this batch lands); `joined`/`lastSeen` '-' until PF-06 exists; flips the Share surface
- AD-26: report step 13: implement `backend.catalog()` from `ls` + `ls member <handle>` + `ls --local` + `status` (Top rated, People, Categories; `projects:[]` until BM-01, `bulkInstall:{}`); flips the Marketplace surface

**Sync rule.** None needed - and this batch is the PROOF of that, which is the load-bearing correction to Teddy's premise. `personSchema` is `.passthrough()` (schema.ts:64); sync parses the pre-image with `parseJson(personSchema, treeText(raw), personPath)` (src/commands/sync.ts:388 and :402) and re-serializes the whole parsed object (`JSON.stringify(fresh, null, 2)`, sync.ts:392 and :406) while only appending to `installed` / `declined`. So `role` and `projects` written on one machine survive sync's own adopt/decline writes untouched and reach every other machine at its next `refreshClone` hard-reset (sync.ts:147) with ZERO sync code.

**Tests.** APPEND the propagation regression, which is the acceptance criterion for the whole sync thesis: a people file carrying role and projects survives a FULL cycle - an install, an orphan adopt and an orphan decline - with both fields byte-identical. Plus rule R1 as a test: a writer that captures the document OUTSIDE the mutation must fail a test in which two concurrent profile writes (one --role, one --project) both land, because safeWrite re-applies on conflict and a capturing writer turns field-level last-writer-wins into whole-document last-writer-wins.

**Rationale and risk.** Dependency-first: this is the owner file the largest block of drawn affordances needs, it is the only batch that pays the guard change, and PF-03/PF-04/PF-07 explicitly SHARE it ("whichever lands first pays it"), so bundling them is the cost-optimal move rather than three separate payments. PF-09 joins because it is the same owner file, the same spec section, and needs no guard code at all. `profile --name` MUST also write `config.display_name`, or a people-file-only rename is silently reverted the next time its owner joins from a second laptop (`team join` rewrites the committed display_name from config identity on every join and reclaim). PF-04's registry read must come from the pre-image tree INSIDE the mutation, or a concurrent `publish` (which may change `projects[].skills` under guard row c) makes the validation stale.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** AC-17. The app reads its OWN version from its own bundle at build time and ships Settings ▸ Updates' "The app" group exactly as drawn - caption `<version> · no update channel yet.`, value `-` - with no updater, no channel, no check, no background request. Record MC-06's closure as the reason no opt-out is needed for a packaged app: a GUI-spawned child has no controlling terminal, so `process.stderr.isTTY` is false and `afterVerb` is undefined (src/index.ts:38-40), and `createExecute` only calls `afterVerb` for verbs registered `notices: true` (src/lib/execute.ts:33-35) - the release notice is never emitted to an app-spawned child, with or without an env var. The stated exception: a `tauri dev` launch from a terminal with inherited stdio CAN print it, so the claim is "packaged app", never blanket.

**Sync rule.** None.

**Tests.** `update.test.ts`: `advice` returns the documented lines for each of the five launch kinds including the `--save-dev` local sub-case and the unknown case; the byte-for-byte templates gain `Latest advertised release: …` in the npx/source/unknown modes. `uninstallMachine.test.ts`: the Kept line names the evals tree when one exists, alongside quarantine and backups, and the run leaves it on disk. `install.test.ts` / `uninstall.test.ts`: a zero-team run names `setup`, not `team join`. `src/lib/__tests__/community.test.ts`: `COMMUNITY_URL` is exported from exactly one module. `src/__tests__/cli.test.ts`: README names every non-hidden command path as a code span and no hidden one.

**Rationale and risk.** UNCHANGED in substance, with one ordering instruction added: S7e is the FIRST Ryan PR to send. ### S7h. ls --host: the admin column and pending invitations

- **Rows:** TJ-04, TJ-05
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7b, S7c, S7g
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** S / Ryan

**CLI change.** `src/cli.ts` - ONE new flag SHARED by both rows (which is the only reason TJ-05 is SIMPLE): `--host` on the `ls` chain (:93), refused with `--local`. `src/commands/ls.ts` - `LsArgs` (:17) gains `host?: boolean`; the roster entries gain `hostRole?: 'admin'|'member'|'unknown'` and `hostCheckedAt?: string`, and `LsResult` gains `invitations?: {login, invitedBy: string|null, invitedAt: string|null}[]` - in-process RETURN TYPES, never serialized, because admin is a host capability (§6.0) and the host is the only thing that enforces it, so the CLI reads it and forgets it. `--host` adds exactly two live GitHub reads: `repos/{owner}/{repo}/collaborators?permission=admin` for the roles, marking each roster handle whose `people/<handle>.json` `github` login appears in it Admin and every other active member with a non-empty `github` Member; and `repos/{owner}/{repo}/invitations` for the pending list - the same call `team remove` already makes to cancel them, with the `.filter(...)` that narrows to one login dropped (team.ts:115-117) - printing each invitee's login with the inviter and date only when GitHub supplies them and an em dash when it does not. A member whose `github` is `''` (schema.ts:60 permits it for a generic-git member) is `unknown`, NEVER Member: the CLI does not infer a role from a missing identity. Invitees are listed as INVITATIONS, never as members, and never counted in the roster, because `invite` writes nothing to the team repository (D9) so an invitee has no people file until they join. On a generic-git remote, without a logged-in `gh`, or on any refusal or failure - including a 403 to a non-admin caller, which GitHub and not the CLI decides - `--host` prints the §6.0 host message and the roster WITHOUT the column, `ls` still exits 0, and it never prints a remembered or inferred role. `ls` performs no `safeWrite` (ls.ts has no write path), so NO guard change; nothing about `ls` without `--host` changes.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** The Share roster's Status column becomes host truth read at a stated moment, and the Admin/Member chip is finally SEPARATED from S7b's `role` discipline label - today `desktop/src/screens/share/ShareScreen.tsx:45` overrides `m.status` from localStorage while :47 renders `m.role`, two different meanings of "role" on one row backed by a per-viewer store. Invited rows render blank Joined / Teams / Last-seen, exactly as drawn, because an invitee has no people file. The role chip gains a fourth state, `unknown`, for offline / no-gh / generic-git / empty github, and loses its chevron: `role_menu`'s two checkable role items have no verb (TJ-02 is design-side), while "Remove from team" does (`team remove`, team.ts:71). Delete GAPS.md:38's admin and pending-invitation clauses.

**Sync rule.** None - `ls --host` is read-only and its values are never stored, so nothing propagates and nothing can go stale between runs.

**Tests.** `ls.test.ts`: `--host` marks admins from `collaborators?permission=admin` and everyone else Member; a member whose `github` is `''` is marked `unknown`; `--host` lists pending invitations with inviter and date and renders `invited by -` when the payload has neither; `--host` with `--local` is refused; a 403 omits the column and still exits 0.

**Rationale and risk.** Third and last ls batch, deliberately last because it is the only read verb in M7 that touches the network, so it should land on a payload that is already stable. Depends on S7g for `src/commands/ls.ts` and on S7c for `src/commands/team.ts` (the invitations read is lifted out of team.ts:115-117 into a shared reader here, and S7o consumes that reader for the DELETE). `--host` must never write, never remember an invitation between runs, and never make `ls` fail - a refusal OMITS the section rather than failing the verb. The two GitHub reads are the only network calls M7 adds anywhere; every other new read is filesystem or git-local.

**Verifier corrections (two adversarial lenses at 85cc276 and f8557c4; authoritative over the batch text above).**

- Lens 1 (corrections apply): S7h is NOT DONE/MOOT (nothing in the 18-commit delta touches ls.ts, and invite.ts changed only its 404 wording), but it is CHANGED by frame mode and has eight material defects. Correct these before it is buildable.

1. SEAM CONTRADICTION - "in-process RETURN TYPES, never serialized" is false under frame mode. The app cannot import a reader; the only channel is the result frame's `value` (frames.ts:24 `ResultFrame{verb,ok,exitCode,error?,declined?,value?}`; run.ts drops everything else). If `hostRole`/`hostCheckedAt`/`invitations` are "never serialized", the appHalf cannot exist. Reword to: these are never PERSISTED to any file (no config.json, no people file, no team.json - admin is host capability per §6.0); they ride `result.value` over frames and are additive against the adapter's passthrough zod schemas (index.ts:17-30, pinned "as of 0.1.5").

2. dependsOn IS WRONG - it names the two batches that do not unblock it and omits the one that does. `roster()` is a hard-coded gap today (`roster: async () => gap('The roster')`, desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:69) and there is no CLI verb behind it. S7g is RM-39/RM-40/RM-44 on `ls --local` → `library()`; S7c is config.json label + identity flags. Neither produces the `Member` DTO (`Design['ROSTER'][number] & {status;projects;lastSeen;lastPublish}`, types.ts:25) that MemberRow renders. Without the batch that supplies role/projects/joined/lastSeen (S7b + the PF-05/PF-06 derivations), S7h's Status column has no row to attach to. Add S7b as a dependency (or the batch that maps a CLI verb onto `roster()`); keep S7g/S7c only as file-ordering deps and say so.

3. D20 IS MIS-ASSIGNED, AND S7h OWNS IT. The `role:<handle>` preference does NOT back S7b's job-title label - it overrides the Admin/Member CHIP. ShareScreen.tsx:45 `const saved=backend.prefs.get('role:'+m.handle,m.status), status=saved==='admin'||saved==='member'?saved:m.status;` and the menu at :48 writes `action.pref('role:'+m.handle,label.toLowerCase())`. The job title is a different field, rendered at :47 as `{m.handle} · {m.role}`. The SYNC ADDENDUM's line "role:<handle> (ShareScreen.tsx:45) with S7b" is therefore wrong: the per-PR D20 retirement of that key belongs to S7h, the batch that makes the chip host truth. The appHalf must state explicitly that it deletes the :45 read AND both :48 writes in the same PR.

ShareScreen.tsx:45 falls back to `m.status` for any value that is not 'admin'|'member', and :48 renders `{status==='admin'?'Admin':'Member'}` with class `'member-role '+status`. THE INVITED-ROW PAYLOAD DOES NOT SATISFY THE SEAM DTO. `Roster['invited']` is `Design['INVITED']` = `{handle,name,role,initials,invited,by}` (src/fixtures/schema.ts:28) and InvitedRow (ShareScreen.tsx:51) renders all six - `<Avatar initials={m.initials}/>`, `<span>{m.name}</span>`, `{m.handle} · {m.role} · invited {m.invited} by {m.by}`. So either the CLI payload grows `permissions`, or `Roster['invited']` shrinks and the drawn row changes (a design-side call for Teddy, not an implementer's). Unstated either way. Note also that `invited`/`by` are display strings in the fixture while the CLI proposes ISO/login - the em-dash fallback is described only for the CLI's terminal print; the app needs its own.

So for an ordinary team MEMBER - the majority of app users - `--host` omits the Status column AND the invitations section every time. The batch buries this inside a list of failure modes ("including a 403 to a non-admin caller"); it is the common path and belongs in the batch's design consequences as a call for Teddy (what the Share roster looks like for a non-admin: a Status column that is present in the design and absent in practice), not as a footnote in `risk`.

### S7i. install: per-item isolation and the Windows home derivation

- **Rows:** RM-19, RM-21, BM-05, AD-14, AD-16
- **Depends on:** S7af
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** S / Ryan
- **Critic and follow-up notes (binding):** Critic: TJ-10's costed route is a §5.1 documentation delta legitimising a hand-maintained `description` key on `team.json.projects` (alive only because `schema.ts:48` is passthrough); TJ-10 sits in S7p, so S7i carries none of it. RM-19 and RM-21 anchors verified exact at f8557c4 by the critic (`install.ts:47-53`, `:55-62`, `:77`, `:93-95`, `:141`, `:241`, `:106`, `result.ts:6`, `sync.ts:308-320`).

**CLI change.** `src/commands/install.ts` - wrap the `install member` / `install project` loop bodies in try/catch, collect per-item errors, report the failing skill BY NAME, and continue, exactly as sync's endorsed batch already does (sync.ts:308-320); the Result carries every skill that DID place on `failure`'s third slot (src/lib/result.ts:1-6), and it is `ok:false` when any item failed, so the exit code still tells a script the batch was not clean while the successful `InstalledResult`s are reported as having happened - because those placements are already durable in `config.placements` and in the people file. TWO error classes are batch-FATAL and are rethrown rather than isolated: `PromptClosedError` (a channel that is gone is not a per-item failure - the same rule sync applies at sync.ts:181, :225, :317) and a remote-access or `safeWrite`-exhaustion failure, because `safeWrite` retries with backoff to a 30 s deadline before throwing, so isolating it would turn one unreachable remote into one retry-to-deadline loop per remaining item - eight of them behind the drawn "Install 8 skills" button. RM-21 replaces `placementHome`'s body (install.ts:241, today `store.root.endsWith('/.terum/skills') ? dirname(dirname(store.root)) : store.root`) with a `basename`/`dirname` walk that ascends only when the last two segments are literally `.terum` and `skills` - separator-correct on every platform, the same reason `join` is correct in the agent-paths table. The POSIX suffix test can NEVER match on Windows, where the default root is `C:\Users\<name>\.terum\skills`: it fell through, the store root itself became the home, and every global placement landed at `<store root>\.claude\skills`, a directory Claude Code never reads. Nothing is stored and nothing is derived from this process's HOME.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** The two batch-install dialogs render per-item outcomes instead of one all-or-nothing error, naming the skills that landed and the skills that did not. ONE consequence the app must expect and disclose rather than treat as a leak: an isolated batch can leave one `config.pending` entry PER failed item where today a single throw leaves one - correct, because pending is written before placement and cleared only after the people-file write, sync replays pending (sync.ts:160-186), and a team with any pending entry is deliberately left unstamped (sync.ts:344-347) so the next session retries - but it changes what the next `sync --hook` does, and that is intended. The app's mid-operation surface reads those entries through S7k's `TeamStatus.pending`.
- AD-14: report bug 2: key the install argv off `kind` using `ref` (`tauri/index.ts:72`; `MarketplaceScreen.tsx:37` sends `{ref, kind}`) so member and project installs stop building a bare skill install
- AD-16: report bug 10: install / uninstall-skill map `name`, not `item.id`, into the DTO's `name` (`tauri/index.ts:72,73`), and the unit test stops feeding `{id:'deploy-check'}`; BM-05's app half

**Sync rule.** See syncDesign - an isolated batch is the one M7 change that alters what the session-start hook finds, and the stamp rule (a team with pending work is not stamped) is what makes that safe.

**Tests.** `install.test.ts`: `install member` isolates one failing candidate - the others land, the failure names it, and the successes ride the failure Result; `PromptClosedError` and a RemoteAccessError abort the batch rather than isolating; one `config.pending` entry per failed item and the next `sync` replays each. `placementHome` resolves a `<home>/.terum/skills` root to `<home>` and leaves any other root alone, asserted on both basename predicates rather than on a separator literal (so the test itself is platform-neutral).

**Rationale and risk.** A parallel lane owning `src/commands/install.ts` alone, placed before S7j because RM-18's install half needs this file's final shape. RM-21 rides it because it is the same file and because a Windows-broken global placement makes every app-side placement claim wrong on one of the two platforms M5 targets. RM-19 changes what a partially-failed batch leaves behind, and therefore what the next session-start sync does. The tests must pin the pending-entry count, not just the placements.

**Verifier corrections (two adversarial lenses at 85cc276 and f8557c4; authoritative over the batch text above).**

- Lens 1 (corrections apply): VERDICT: S7i is UNAFFECTED on the CLI side (confirmed byte-exact, see below) but its APP HALF is unbuildable as written, its dependsOn edge is dead, and three cited facts are wrong. Six corrections, ordered by severity.

1. HEADLINE - the app half cannot work: the real adapter DISCARDS the failure payload RM-19 exists to deliver. RM-19's entire delivery design is "the Result is ok:false with the successful InstalledResults on the failure value". That value does reach the wire: execute.ts:33 attaches `value: outcome.value` on the failing arm (`sink.result?.({verb, ok:false, error, value: outcome.value, exitCode:1})`, cli-main/src/lib/execute.ts:29-33), and frames.ts emits it (`if (outcome.value !== undefined) frame.value = outcome.value;`, cli-main/src/lib/frames.ts:171-175). But the M6 adapter drops it: on `ok:false` run.ts does `const error = frame.error ?? …; finish({ ok:false, error }, …)` - `options.map` is never called and no `value` is attached (cli-main/desktop/src/backend/tauri/run.ts:67-76; the map runs only in the `if (frame.ok)` arm at :68-71). The seam's own `Result<T>` failure arm carries `value?: T` (desktop/src/backend/types.ts:2) and `install` is already `Run<InstalledResult[]>` (Backend.ts:14), so the shape is there and only the adapter throws it away. CONSEQUENCE: with the CLI change landed and nothing else, the drawn batch-install dialog renders exactly one error string - the all-or-nothing board S7i says it replaces. S7i must own a second edit, in a file it does not currently list: map and attach `value` on run.ts's failure arm (guarding the map, since a partial value may fail schema.parse - fall back to the error alone rather than replacing it). Add `desktop/src/backend/tauri/run.ts` to the batch and a test that a `{t:'result',ok:false,value:[…]}` frame surfaces both error and value.

2. dependsOn ["S7a"] IS DEAD - cut it, do not re-point it. S7a is MOOT: package.json at f8557c4 still declares only type/bin/files with no main/exports/types (cli-main/package.json:14-22), and the app reaches the CLI as a child process over frames, not by import. S7i never needed the exports map anyway - it is a self-contained control-flow change in one verb file. S7i's real (soft, app-side only) ordering dependency is on S7f/RM-30: the adapter fabricates `name: item.id` because the CLI's InstalledResult is `{id,team,path,version}` with no name (cli-clone/src/commands/install.ts:36; adapter map at cli-main/desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:72), so "naming the skills that landed" renders UUIDs until RM-30 lands. Say that, and drop the S7a edge.

3. WRONG CITATION - `sync.ts:225` is not a PromptClosedError rethrow. At 85cc276 the three rethrow sites are sync.ts:181 (pending replay), sync.ts:287 (placement loop), sync.ts:317 (endorsed batch) - verified by grep. Line 225 is the pinned-tree check (`if (entry.version && (await runner.run('git', ['cat-file','-e', …]))…`). Replace :225 with :287.

4. THE PRECEDENT IS OVERSTATED - sync does NOT rethrow the second fatal class. S7i says the two-fatal-class rule is "exactly as sync's endorsed batch already does (sync.ts:308-320)". sync's endorsed batch isolates EVERYTHING except PromptClosedError: the catch is `if (error instanceof PromptClosedError) throw error; defer(team, skill.name); notice(…)` (sync.ts:316-318) - a RemoteAccessError or SafeWriteExhausted there is deferred per candidate, not rethrown. S7i is proposing a STRICTER rule than its own cited precedent. The reasoning is sound (safeWrite retries to DEFAULT_DEADLINE_MS = 30_000 before throwing, so isolating it costs one 30 s loop per remaining item), but the batch must state it as a deliberate divergence for Ryan to approve - and decide whether sync's loop should adopt it too, or the two loops stay deliberately different.

5. THE PENDING TEST ASSERTION IS WRONG FOR ONE FAILURE MODE. "one config.pending entry per failed item" holds only for failures AFTER consent. installOne pushes pending at install.ts:93-95 (before consent) and clears it at :143 (after the people-file write), but a declined consent removes its own entry: `catch (error) { // A declined pre-placement consent is not an interrupted install: nothing observable moved. if (!pendingAlreadyExists) await input.store.update((fresh) => { fresh.pending = fresh.pending.filter(…) }); throw error; }` (install.ts:98-104). So an item that fails by declining leaves ZERO pending entries. The test must drive the per-item failure past consent (a foreign-folder collision without --force at :115, a place() failure, or a safeWrite failure) and should additionally pin the decline case as leaving no entry - otherwise the stated assertion fails on the most obvious way to inject a failure.

6. THE CLI CHANGE IS HARDER THAN "wrap the loop bodies" - neither batch loop has a skill NAME in hand. RM-19 requires reporting the failing skill BY NAME. sync's loop can do that because `candidates` are SkillRecords (`defer(team, skill.name)`, sync.ts:318). install's two batch loops iterate ids only: `for (const item of person.installed)` where installedSchema is `{id,version,scope,since}` (install.ts:48; schema.ts:36-41), and `for (const id of project.skills)` where team.json's projects carry a uuid list (install.ts:60; schema.ts:48). So the id→name resolution is extra work the cliChanges paragraph omits: either a `findSkill(store.teamClone(team), team, id)` per item before the call, or installOne attaching the name to the thrown error. Name it, or the "BY NAME" requirement silently degrades to an 8-char id prefix (what sync's pending replay falls back to at sync.ts:183, :184).
