MINOR: "the Result carries every skill that DID place on `failure`'s third slot" is imprecise - `failure<T>(error, value?)` takes value as its SECOND argument (result.ts:6); it is the third FIELD of the ok:false arm (result.ts:3). Reword to avoid an implementer looking for a third parameter.
- Lens 2 (corrections apply): VERDICT: CHANGED (not DONE/MOOT). Nothing in the 18 commits touched src/commands/install.ts (git diff --stat 85cc276..f8557c4 -- src/commands/install.ts src/commands/sync.ts is EMPTY), so the CLI half stands exactly as costed and every anchor below is current at f8557c4. What changed is the consumer and the delivery path. Eleven material corrections.

1. dependsOn ["S7a"] IS WRONG AND MUST BE CUT. S7a is MOOT: package.json at 0.1.6 still declares only type/bin/files, no main/exports/types (cli-main/package.json:14-22), and the shipped seam is frame mode. S7i never needed a library entry anyway - its CLI half is one command file. Re-point dependsOn to [] for the CLI half, and add the real dependency the batch is missing (see 6c): the app half depends on an InstalledResult that carries a NAME (RM-30 / S7f), because today it can only print UUIDs.

2. WRONG ANCHOR - sync.ts:225. The three PromptClosedError rethrows in sync are :181 (pending replay), :287 (placement loop) and :317 (endorsed batch) - grep -n PromptClosedError src/commands/sync.ts returns exactly 11 (import), 181, 287, 317. Line 222-225 is the blocked-pinned-version branch (`blocked(entry.team, basename(path), 'Blocked ... pinned version ...'); continue;`), not a rethrow. Replace ":225" with ":287". sync.ts is byte-identical 85cc276..f8557c4, so this is an authoring error, not drift. The other two sync anchors are exact: the endorsed batch's isolate-and-continue body is sync.ts:310-320 with its rethrow at :317 (cited range 308-320 covers the comment at 308-309), and install.ts:241 is placementHome verbatim.

3. STAMP PREDICATE OFF BY ONE. "a team with any pending entry is deliberately left unstamped (sync.ts:344-347)" - the predicate is sync.ts:343 `if (incomplete.has(team) || pendingLeft > 0) {`, computed from `const pendingLeft = final.pending.filter(e => e.team === team).length` at :341; :344 is the incomplete push and :347 the writeStamp. Cite 341-348, or :343 for the predicate. The syncRule's claim itself is CORRECT.

4. "ONE config.pending ENTRY PER FAILED ITEM" IS FALSE FOR ONE FAILURE CLASS, AND THE TEST AS WRITTEN WILL FAIL. installOne pushes pending at install.ts:95, but a consent decline is caught at :100-104 and CLEARS its own entry (`// A declined pre-placement consent is not an interrupted install: nothing observable moved.` … `if (!pendingAlreadyExists) await input.store.update(fresh => { fresh.pending = fresh.pending.filter(e => e.started !== pending.started); })`), rethrowing afterwards. So a declined item leaves ZERO pending entries; only failures AFTER consent (foreign target without --force, a busy lockTarget, place() failure, safeWrite) leave one each, cleared at :141. The test "one config.pending entry per failed item and the next sync replays each" must therefore name its failure class - and if it injects the most natural one (a declined confirm, which is exactly what the app's install dialog produces) it asserts a count that the code deliberately does not produce. Split the assertion: post-consent failure → one pending entry, replayed by the next sync; pre-consent decline → no pending entry, nothing to replay. The risk line ("the tests must pin the pending-entry count") is right but under-specified.

5. THE OUTER CATCH MUST CHANGE TOO, WHICH THE cliChanges DOES NOT SAY. install.ts:77 is `} catch (error) { return failure(error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)); }` - no value. Wrapping the loop bodies is not enough: the two batch-FATAL classes the batch correctly wants rethrown (PromptClosedError, RemoteAccessError/SafeWriteExhausted - both exported from teamRepo.ts:53 and :66, neither currently imported by install.ts) will unwind through :77 and DISCARD every successful InstalledResult, which contradicts the batch's own "durable placements must never be reported as not having happened". Also, minor: `failure(error, value?)` takes TWO arguments (result.ts:6); "failure's third slot" only makes sense as the union's third field.

6. THE appHalf IS NOT CONSISTENT WITH THE SHIPPED SEAM - three concrete blockers, none named:
 a. THE REAL ADAPTER DROPS THE FAILURE VALUE. desktop/src/backend/tauri/run.ts:74 finishes a failing result frame as `finish({ ok: false, error }, { t: 'result', ok: false, error })` - `frame.value` is read only on the ok branch (:69-72, where options.map runs). So everything the CLI puts on failure's value is invisible in the shipped app. And on the mock path useWorkflow.perform does the same: `else setError(result.error)` (useWorkflow.ts:35, identical in /home/teniroo/Projects/terum-skills-app and cli-main/desktop). The app half therefore edits run.ts (forward and schema-parse the failure value) AND useWorkflow.ts AND mock/index.ts:55 (which returns a single-element ok([...]) and never a partial failure) - not just dialog copy.
 b. THE FAILED NAMES HAVE NO TYPED CARRIER. Successes ride value as InstalledResult[]; the skills that did NOT land exist only inside the aggregate error string and in print frames - and driveRun consumes only `ask` frames (drive.ts:7-12), dropping every print. "naming the skills that landed and the skills that did not" is only achievable if the CLI also returns a failures list on the value (id/name + reason), or the appHalf must state plainly that failures are one error string.
 c. NO NAME EXISTS TO NAME THEM WITH. CLI InstalledResult is `{ id; team; path; version }` (install.ts:36) and the real adapter maps it as `name: item.id` (desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:72) - a UUID. Until InstalledResult carries a name (RM-30 / S7f), every per-item outcome the app renders under the real adapter is a UUID. Add that edge.
 POSITIVE: the seam DOES already model a list - Backend.install returns Run<InstalledResult[]> in both trees (Backend.ts:14) and Result<T> allows a value on ok:false (types.ts:2), so no DTO widening is needed for the successes themselves.

7. "THE TWO BATCH-INSTALL DIALOGS" - there is exactly ONE. InstallDialog is defined at MarketplaceScreen.tsx:25 and rendered at :40 only for a project (`{q && state.dialog === 'install' && <InstallDialog .../>}`); the member path has NO dialog (`onClick={() => q ? param('dialog','install') : void install()}`) and the Inbox share action installs a single ref (InboxScreen.tsx:48). Reword as "the project install dialog and the dialog-less `Install N skills` member action".

8. RM-19 COLLIDES WITH THE SHIPPED DECLINE HEURISTIC (new since the costing). ResultFrame.declined is derived by `isDecline(error)` = `/\bdeclined\b/i.test(error)` over the error STRING (frames.ts:24, 45-46, 173). An isolated batch whose aggregate error quotes a per-item "Consent was declined for <skill>." will set declined:true on a run that PARTLY SUCCEEDED, so the app renders a partial success as a user decline. Constrain the aggregate message wording (or coordinate with S7d, which owns the decline discriminator).

9. RM-21 IS UNTESTABLE AS DESCRIBED, AND ON THE ONE PLATFORM THAT MATTERS. (i) placementHome is module-private (`function placementHome(store)` at install.ts:241, no export) and is bypassed whenever `home` is passed - `input.home ?? placementHome(input.store)` at install.ts:106 - which every existing test does; the test must drive installOne with `home` OMITTED and a store rooted at `<tmp>/.terum/skills`. (ii) The claim that a basename/dirname walk makes the test "platform-neutral" is wrong: node:path resolves to posix on CI, so `C:\Users\x\.terum\skills` still has basename `C:\Users\x\.terum\skills` and the Windows case cannot be exercised. ci.yml has three jobs, all `runs-on: ubuntu-latest`; there is no Windows runner. To actually prove the fix, export placementHome and take the path flavour as a parameter (path.win32 / path.posix), otherwise state that the Windows leg ships untested - which now matters on a user path, since Windows-on-ARM is a released app target (release.yml:373-378) and the shell spawns the CLI there (desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:39-49).

10. SIZE / ONE-PR CLAIM. The CLI half is genuinely S: 2 files (src/commands/install.ts, 241 lines; src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts, 413 lines - the vitest layout the batch names exists), 0 new verbs, 0 frame-protocol changes (no FRAME_VERBS or FRAME_FEATURES entry needed). The APP half as scoped is not S and is not private to this lane: it edits desktop/src/backend/tauri/run.ts and src/components/domain/useWorkflow.ts, both shared seam files that S7j (sync names) and S7d (typed decline) also need, plus mock/index.ts and MarketplaceScreen.tsx. One PR is still possible now that desktop/ is in the monorepo, but declare the shared-file edits and the ordering, or scope S7i to the CLI half and record the render change as a GAPS.md line.

"A parallel lane owning src/commands/install.ts alone" is true only within M7's own batches; note that syncDesign item 4 (RM-18, in S7j) also adds `notices` to install's Result, so the ordering rationale (S7i before S7j) is load-bearing and should be stated as a hard sequence, not a preference.

NO VIOLATION FOUND (verified, not assumed): D20 is respected - config.pending is machine-local and the batch shows it as machine-local; it adds no localStorage-backed team-wide affordance and retires none, so it carries no per-PR D20 retirement obligation. The seam rule is respected - nothing outside src/backend/ is asked to import @tauri-apps or touch the filesystem, and no new capability flag is implied. The CLI-side test names map cleanly onto src/**/__tests__ and the app-side onto *.test.ts(x) (src/backend/__tests__/run.test.ts and src/screens/marketplace/marketplace.test.tsx already exist and are where 6a and 7 would be asserted).

### S7j. sync propagation: per-team outcome, --team, missing clone, complete Results (RM-18 minus its item (a), built once under RM-31)

- **Rows:** RM-31, RM-32, CP-S3, RM-18
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7f, S7i
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** M / Ryan
- **Critic and follow-up notes (binding):** Critic (over-priced): `TeamOutcome`'s skipped arm (`sync.ts:37`) already carries `unreachable | locked | busy | error | fresh`; CP-S3's absent clone reuses `unreachable` ("Skipping <team>: <clone> is missing") and adds nothing; only `--team` (a deliberate exclusion) needs a new reason. Names, not counts (decision taken under Teddy's "finish it" directive, 2026-09-08): `sync` returns the placed and removed skill NAMES beside the counts, taken from `recordPlacement`'s per-team map before it is reduced; additive, so the adapter's placed/removed lists stop being empty.

**CLI change.** `src/commands/sync.ts` - RM-31 is ALREADY ~90% landed by commit 55ef7f0 and this batch finishes it: `SyncResult` (:39) already carries `teams: TeamOutcome[]` with `complete | incomplete | skipped('unreachable'|'locked'|'busy'|'error'|'fresh') | gone`, `PlacementCounts` (:32) and `SharedCounts` (:33), and it needs NO `stamped` flag because `state === 'complete'` IS the stamp predicate by construction (`writeStamp` runs only on that arm, sync.ts:344-349). The remainder is one line, shared with RM-18's sync half: drop the `args.hook ?` ternary at sync.ts:353 and sync.ts:359 so the payload rides interactive failures too. PROVEN SAFE this session: `createExecute` routes hook notices only when `isHookSync(value)` holds, which requires `hook === true` AND both arrays (src/lib/execute.ts:24, :42-44), and `hook: Boolean(args.hook)` keeps that discriminant accurate. RM-32 adds `--team <team>` (cli.ts:128 + `SyncArgs.team`), implemented as a SKIP-SET and never as a loop filter - see syncRule. CP-S3 adds a fourth classification to the per-team catch (sync.ts:148-157, which today classifies only `RemoteAccessError` and `CloneBusy` and RETHROWS everything else into the outer catch at :359, aborting every remaining team): an absent clone directory becomes `skipped.set(team, {reason:'error', detail})` with `status`'s own restore remedy (`move <clone> aside, then run <invocation> team join <remote>`), so one deleted `teams/<name>` no longer costs the whole run; sync still never CLONES, and exit 1 with the existing `Sync finished with <n> team(s) skipped` line is unchanged. RM-07 adds `export` to `approved(config, id, grants)` (sync.ts:364) so the display and the gate cannot diverge - `config.approvals[<id>]` stores the sha256 of the approved grant string and can be compared but never displayed, so any surface showing what was approved must join the stored entry against the skill's CURRENT normalized `allowed-tools` (S7f's `grantsHash`) and show the grants only when the hashes match; `none` is approved with no stored entry. RM-18's other two halves: `InstalledResult` gains `notices: string[]` (`src/commands/install.ts`, the placer lines it prints today) and `search` returns `{hits, warnings}` (`src/commands/search.ts`, the per-team degradations it currently only prints). The two verbs keep DIFFERENT outcome shapes on purpose: a mixed `search` stays `ok:true` because a partial catalogue is a usable answer, while a partial `install` is `ok:false` with its successes on the value because placements already written are durable state. Printed output is unchanged in all three cases.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** CP-26 and RM-36. CP-26: "Re-place from team" runs plain `sync`, NEVER `install <ref>`, and must disclose two things the drawn report does not - the repair is MACHINE-WIDE (sync re-places every drifted placement in the ledger loop, sync.ts:199-297, not just the one on screen) and an app-initiated sync writes `run/<team>.stamp` (sync.ts:348) and thereby suppresses the SessionStart hook's own sync for the next hour (the `stampIsFresh` gate at sync.ts:130 with `STAMP_FRESH_MS = 60*60_000`, src/lib/hook.ts:149; D-R4). RM-36: two git invocations against the clone the CLI already maintains - `git diff --name-status <treeA> <treeB>` for FILE/CHANGE and `git diff --numstat <treeA> <treeB>` for LINES (the combined form prints only the name-status block, so two calls are required) - with `treeA` the placement's ledger `version` (schema.ts:100) or the incumbent named on the Inbox item, and `treeB` the candidate tree. The app renders `TeamOutcome` per team on Settings ▸ Teams and Settings ▸ Sync instead of one aggregate. Delete GAPS.md:49's per-team clause; progress events stay M6.

**Sync rule.** CORRECTED LINE ANCHORS; the rule is unchanged and remains the load-bearing part. --team MUST add every other configured team to the same skipped map the fresh/locked/busy paths use, not filter the top loop, because reconcileOrphans and the placement loop both iterate config.placements GLOBALLY and exclude a team only via skipTeams.has(placement.team) at src/commands/sync.ts:373 and skipped.has(entry.team) at :204. Filtering only the top loop would leave both walking an excluded team's placements - prompting 'Adopt orphaned placement at <path>?' at sync.ts:382 and, on a no, writing a PERMANENT declined entry for a team the user deliberately excluded. The hook's freshness gate is sync.ts:135, not :130. An excluded team is not a skipped team for the exit count.

**Tests.** `sync.test.ts`: `--team` syncs only the named team and leaves the other team's clone, placements, connected sources, orphaned placements and stamp untouched, and does NOT prompt `Adopt orphaned placement at <path>?` for the excluded team; `--team` is refused with `--hook` and with `--prune`; a team whose clone directory was deleted is skipped with the restore remedy while the other teams still complete; an interactive sync that cannot fetch one team returns `ok:false` CARRYING placed, deferred, notices and teams, and the `--hook` path stays byte-identical; a locked team is reported `locked` with no stamp while the other is `complete` and stamped. `install.test.ts` / `search.test.ts`: the notices and warnings twins.

**Rationale and risk.** The sync batch runs AFTER the read-model and install batches because RM-18 spans three files: sync.ts (this batch's own), search.ts (S7f's) and install.ts (S7i's). Both dependencies are serialization edges created by that one row; RM-31/RM-32/CP-S3/RM-07 are sync-only and would otherwise be free to run early. Keeping RM-18 whole rather than splitting it across three batches is what preserves the one-row-one-batch rule. RM-32's implementation is the load-bearing part and is the OPPOSITE of filtering the loops - see syncRule. RM-31 must be re-verdicted before a spec delta is written: at b5c0507 `SyncResult` was `{placed, deferred, notices, changed, hook}`, at 85cc276 it is that plus `teams: TeamOutcome[]`, so most of the row is CLOSED and the ask to Ryan should be one line, not a paragraph. Secondary staleness: the costing cites `writeStamp` at sync.ts:291; it is now sync.ts:414.

TESTS - describable in the existing layouts, yes for the CLI, incomplete for the app. `src/commands/__tests__/{sync,install,search}.test.ts` all exist, so every named CLI test lands in an existing file. But the app half now needs tests too and the `tests` field names none: the adapter has `desktop/src/backend/tauri/__tests__/run.test.ts` (which already exercises `backend.sync({prune:true, hook:true})` at :127), and desktop otherwise colocates `*.test.ts(x)`. 11. SIZE / ONE-PR - feasible but understated. desktop/ is now inside the CLI repo (898a795), so one PR is possible, which it was not before. But the real file count is ~13-16: sync.ts, cli.ts, install.ts, search.ts + 3 CLI test files + types.ts, mock/index.ts, tauri/index.ts (three zod schemas and three mappings) + adapter tests + new Settings UI + GAPS.md. `size: "M"` should be L, and the cleanest fix is to move RM-18's search half into S7f and its install half into S7i - the batches that already have those files open - which also DISSOLVES both dependsOn edges. dependsOn ["S7f","S7i"] is otherwise correct as stated (both edges exist solely because of RM-18; no edge on the retired S7a, so nothing to re-point there) - but if RM-18 stays whole here, S7f/S7i must not also touch search.ts/install.ts, or the merge order breaks.

No localStorage pref is retired by this batch, correctly, since none of the six (`enabled:`, `favorite:`, `favorite-project:`, `following:`, `role:`, `sync:hook`) is sync-owned.
- Lens 2 (corrections apply): VERDICT: S7j is CHANGED (not DONE, not MOOT). Its three CLI owner files are byte-identical between 85cc276 and f8557c4 - `diff -q` on src/commands/{sync,install,search}.ts across cli-clone and cli-main is silent - so nothing in the 18 commits closed, renamed or moved any row. But one stated code change is WRONG AS WRITTEN, one row is misattributed to the wrong batch, and the delta breaks one of RM-18's two "no ripple" premises. Six corrections, then the anchors that verified clean.

=== 1. BLOCKER - the "one line" change ships a double-print regression ===
The batch says RM-31/RM-18's sync half is: "drop the `args.hook ?` ternary at sync.ts:353 and sync.ts:359 so the payload rides interactive failures too", justified by "`hook: Boolean(args.hook)` keeps that discriminant accurate."

Both failure payloads hard-code `hook: true`, not `Boolean(args.hook)`:
  sync.ts:353 `return failure(\`Sync finished with ${unreachable.length} team(s) skipped: ...\`, args.hook ? { placed, deferred, notices, changed, hook: true, teams } : undefined);`
  sync.ts:359 `return failure(args.hook ? message : \`Sync failed: ${message}\`, args.hook ? { placed, deferred, notices, changed, hook: true, teams } : undefined);`
`hook: Boolean(args.hook)` appears only on the SUCCESS return (sync.ts:356). So deleting only the ternary attaches `{hook:true, deferred:[], notices:[...]}` to an INTERACTIVE failure, `isHookSync` (execute.ts:41-43 @85cc276) matches, and `writeHookNotices` (execute.ts:45-48) re-emits every notice to stderr plus `N skills need review - run \`…sync\``. Those notices were already printed to stdout by `notice()` (sync.ts:98, `if (!args.hook) io.print(line)`). The instruction must be: rewrite BOTH literals to `hook: Boolean(args.hook)` and drop the guard - which is exactly what the costing already specifies at costing:825 ("so both read `failure(msg, { placed, deferred, notices, changed, hook: Boolean(args.hook) })`") and costing:924. The batch is a lossy restatement of those two lines; a spec writer following the batch verbatim ships the bug.

Corollary for `tests`: the batch's test list is missing the one regression test that catches this, which the costing already named at costing:926 - "src/lib/__tests__/execute.test.ts: an interactive failing sync Result with a value writes no hook notices to stderr (isHookSync stays false)". The batch's stated test "the `--hook` path stays byte-identical" passes while the interactive path regresses. Add the execute.test.ts assertion to `tests`.

=== 2. RM-18's search half is NOT additive on the wire and breaks the shipped 0.1.6 adapter ===
`search`'s run returns `Promise<Result<SearchHit[]>>` (search.ts:22) - a top-level ARRAY. RM-18 turns it into `SearchResult {hits, warnings}` (costing:825). The costing cleared this as rippleless because "search's only caller is src/cli.ts:126 @ b5c0507, which ignores the value" (costing:831). That premise is dead: search is now one of only two live frames READ paths, and the real adapter validates the value as an array - `const cliSearch = z.array(z.object({ id, name, author, category, installs, latest, unresolved }).passthrough());` (cli-main desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:30) consumed at index.ts:70. Array→object is a change to an existing field's meaning, which docs/frame-protocol.md:61-63 says is precisely the case that is NOT additive. So the search half must either (a) land with the adapter's cliSearch + mapping changed in the SAME PR (the app half of S7j now targets cli-main desktop/src/backend/tauri, not only mock/), or (b) be re-shaped to stay an array. Record the choice; do not leave it as "printed output is unchanged in all three cases", which is true of stdout and false of the wire.
The install half is fine by contrast: `notices: string[]` on the per-item `InstalledResult` (install.ts:36) is additive against `cliInstalled = z.array(z.object({id, team}).passthrough())` (index.ts:18, mapping :72).

=== 3. Scope error: the "partial install is ok:false with its successes on the value" sentence belongs to RM-19 / S7i, not to S7j ===
The batch's cliChanges asserts "a partial `install` is `ok:false` with its successes on the value because placements already written are durable state". That behaviour is RM-19 "Batch verbs without error isolation (install member / project)" (costing:79, body at costing:835; costed SIMPLE·cli with a MEDIUM refuter verdict), which the batch itself routes to S7i. It also does not exist today and is not a payload edit: install's member and project loops push into `results` and any throw from `installOne` escapes the loop to the single catch at install.ts:77, which returns `failure(message)` with NO value (install.ts:38, :47-52, :59-62, :77). Either move the sentence out of S7j's cliChanges (leaving RM-18's install half as the `notices` field only), or move RM-19 into this batch and re-price it - the costing already logs refuter B's objection that RM-18 and RM-19 pick opposite outcome shapes as "UPHELD as a completeness defect" (costing:831), so the two rows arguing with each other across two batches is a real seam.

=== 4. CP-S3 premise verified, but the stated classifier cannot see an absent clone ===
Verified as claimed: the refreshClone catch at sync.ts:148-157 classifies only `RemoteAccessError` (:149-152) and `CloneBusy` (:153) and rethrows everything else (`if (!(error instanceof CloneBusy)) throw error;` at :153) into the outer catch, whose failure return is sync.ts:359 - one deleted `teams/<name>` costs every remaining team. (Nit: the outer `} catch (error) {` clause is at :357; :359 is the return inside it.)
What the batch does not say: an ABSENT clone directory produces no classifiable git error. `withCloneLock` locks a path BESIDE the clone (`cloneLockPath` = `dirname(root)/.<basename>.safewrite.lock`, teamRepo.ts:491-493, used at :503), so locking succeeds when the directory is gone; the failure then comes from spawning git with a non-existent cwd (`systemRunner`, runner.ts:14-22), which REJECTS rather than returning a non-zero `code`, so refreshClone's message-building branches (teamRepo.ts:473-482) never run and there is no stable string to match. The fourth classification must PROBE, not pattern-match: call `describeClone` (teamRepo.ts:445, returning `CloneState = absent | incomplete | foreign | ok`, teamRepo.ts:427 - the same reader `status` uses) before refreshClone, and map `absent`/`incomplete` to `skipped.set(team, {reason:'error', detail})` with status's restore remedy. Say this in cliChanges or the implementer will write a fragile string match.

=== 5. RM-07's `export` has no app consumer post-frames; name the CLI-side one ===
Verified: `function approved(config, id, grants)` at sync.ts:364 is currently unexported, and its body is `grants.ok && (grants.normalized === 'none' || config.approvals[id]?.grants === grants.hash)` (:365) - the hash-compare-never-display claim is exact. But per the delta, package.json is still bin-only (cli-main package.json:14-22) and the adapter imports nothing from the CLI, so `export` is useful only as an intra-CLI import. Name the CLI-side read model that will carry the joined grants in its result VALUE (the Settings ▸ Approvals payload), the way S7d's exported readers had to be rebuilt as verb payloads. Otherwise this row repeats S7a/S7d's retired mechanism at one-keyword scale.

=== 6. Anchor corrections ===
- execute.ts is WRONG in both places the batch cites it. At 85cc276 the `isHookSync` CALL is execute.ts:25 (`if (isHookSync(outcome.value)) writeHookNotices(outcome.value, sink);`) and the DEFINITION is :41-43, with writeHookNotices at :45-48 - not ":24, :42-44". Worse, execute.ts is one of the files the frames commit touched: at f8557c4 the call is :28 and the definition :49-51, and the sink gained `result?(outcome: ResultOutcome)` which now forwards `outcome.value` on a FAILING result. - The appHalf's placement-loop range "sync.ts:199-297" is off: the loop is `for (const [path, entry] of Object.entries(currentConfig.placements))` at sync.ts:200 and closes at :291-292.

=== 7. Delta consequence the batch should record (strengthens it, no change of substance) ===
S7j is now the load-bearing sync batch for the app precisely because of the frames commit: the payload reaches the app ONLY as `ResultFrame.value`, and cli-main execute.ts calls `sink.result?.({verb, ok:false, error, value: outcome.value, exitCode:1})` on a failing Result - so fix #1 is the only way the app ever sees a partial sync. Today the adapter throws that away twice over: `cliSync = z.object({ placed: z.number(), deferred: z.array(z.string()) }).passthrough()` (index.ts:24) does not read `teams`, and the mapping `(value): SyncResult => ({ placed: value.deferred.length || value.placed ? [] : [], removed: [] })` (index.ts:78) returns `[]` on both ternary branches. The app half must extend cliSync to read `teams` and change the seam's `SyncResult` to carry `TeamOutcome`. Also worth citing in `tests`/`syncRule`: `--hook` never reaches the verb from the app at all - the bin refuses it before commander parses, `channel.result({verb:'sync', ok:false, error:'`sync --hook` is the session hook and is not available over frames; run plain `sync`.', exitCode:1})` at cli-main src/index.ts:59-61.

### S7l. hook [on|off]: the session-hook switch

- **Rows:** MC-10
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7k
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** S / Ryan

**CLI change.** New `src/commands/hook.ts` plus one registration in `src/cli.ts`'s M2 block. With no argument it prints the command actually FOUND in `~/.claude/settings.json` - read from the file, never from `HOOK_COMMAND` (src/lib/hook.ts:12, `npx -y terum-skills@latest sync --hook`) - or reports that no entry is installed, which is how a stale hook command becomes visible. `on` calls `installHook` (hook.ts:89) DIRECTLY rather than `offerHook` (:130): it does not short-circuit on an existing entry, so it is how a stale command is corrected on a machine that already has one, and it asks no y/N because a user who asks for the switch has given the consent. `off` calls `removeHook` (:116), which strips only this tool's commands and leaves a mixed group's other hooks intact, and it joins `team leave` of your last configured team and machine `uninstall` in the removal list. Both take the same parse-before-write, backup-once, atomic-rename contract as every other write to that file. The OFFER rule is UNCHANGED - installed only after a y/N at create/join or at the wizard's step 7, exactly one offer per run, never silent, never two - because an explicit `hook on` is not an offer. `~/.claude/settings.json` is never in a team-repo tree, so there is NO guard change and no `safeWrite`.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** The Settings ▸ Sync / ▸ Advanced session-hook toggle stops being the `sync:hook` localStorage bit (`desktop/src/screens/settings/SettingsContent.tsx:22`) and reads and writes the real entry through the seam - the third D20-adjacent per-viewer bit M7 retires, and the one where the drawn control was actually lying about machine state. The row also renders the command it found, so a machine with a hook installed by an older CLI shows the stale spelling rather than the constant. CP-O2's onboarding string ("the session-start hook is set up by `setup` in the terminal; Settings ▸ Sync shows this machine's answer") stays Teddy's design-side call.

**Sync rule.** None - and that is the constraint. The hook's own behaviour is unchanged: `sync --hook` remains typed to a print-only `NonInteractivePrompter` (sync.ts:41-42, :62-63), stdout carries only the reload directive (sync.ts:327), and a team stamped within the hour is silently skipped (sync.ts:130).

**Tests.** `src/lib/__tests__/hook.test.ts`: reports the command actually present in settings.json, not `HOOK_COMMAND`. New `src/commands/__tests__/hook.test.ts`: `hook on` installs the entry and replaces a stale one without asking; `hook off` strips only this tool's commands from a mixed group; both back up once and rename atomically; neither opens a team repo.

**Rationale and risk.** Serialization edge only: both batches edit `src/lib/hook.ts` (S7k adds `stampedAt`, S7l reads the installed entry). Placed after S7k because the status read model is the more load-bearing of the two. The SIMPLE bar names "a hook write path that commits on every session start" as NOT SIMPLE. `hook on|off` stays inside the bar precisely because it writes only `~/.claude/settings.json` and commits NOTHING to any team repository - the batch must not acquire a repo write, and the test should assert no `safeWrite` on the path.

**Verifier corrections (two adversarial lenses at 85cc276 and f8557c4; authoritative over the batch text above).**

- Lens 1 (corrections apply): S7l's CLI half is accurate and correctly costed; its APP half is not buildable as written, and its size/dependsOn/design-consequence accounting is wrong. Nine corrections, in severity order.

1. SEAM: THERE IS NO BACKEND MEMBER TO WRITE THE HOOK - the appHalf's central claim is unbuildable as stated. `Backend` is 27 members (desktop/src/backend/Backend.ts:2-31: capabilities, status, settings, onboarding, library, skill, receipts, inbox, catalog, roster, search, install, uninstallSkill, uninstallMachine, connect, publish, sync, invite, team, setup, eval, validate, update, openInEditor, copyToClipboard, copyImage, prefs, subscribe). None reads or writes the session hook. The READ half already has a DTO slot - `Settings` is `Pick<Design,…|'HOOK'|…>` (desktop/src/backend/types.ts, Settings alias) and `Design['HOOK']` is `{installed, file, timeout}` (desktop/src/fixtures/schema.ts:69) - but "writes the real entry through the seam" requires a NEW Backend member. Because `installHook`/`removeHook` write `~/.claude/settings.json` and ask nothing, the natural shape is a long verb `hook(args:{on:boolean}): Run<HookResult>` plus a `HookResult` DTO, a mock mapping (desktop/src/backend/mock/index.ts), a real mapping in the ten-long-verb table (desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:72-82), and a ChangeSource declaration - 'config' is wrong (the file is outside ~/.terum), so either a sixth ChangeSource or an explicit "notifies nothing" decision. The batch must name this; it currently reads as if the seam already carries it. SEAM: THE READ MODEL HAS NO `command` FIELD - "the row also renders the command it found" has no DTO slot. `Design['HOOK']` is exactly `{installed:boolean, file:string, timeout:number}` (desktop/src/fixtures/schema.ts:69; the fixture value at desktop/src/fixtures/design.json:4021-4025 is `{"installed":true,"file":"~/.claude/settings.json","timeout":60}`). The costing already ruled on this at 2026-09-07-desktop-app-gap-costing.md:1680: "build.py:3645 must stop composing the command from `cli("sync --hook")` and take it from the machine - the mock gains a `HOOK["command"]` beside the existing `HOOK["file"]`". THE BATCH MISSES THE ONE NEW FACT IN THE DELTA THAT MOST CHANGES MC-10: A SUCCESSFUL APP HAND-OFF SKIPS THE HOOK OFFER ENTIRELY. src/commands/setup.ts:112-115 - on a launched app, setup prints 'Continuing in the app.' and `return success({ role, team: teamName, remote, steps })`, so the wizard never reaches `verbs.offerHook(...)` at setup.ts:241. **CLI change.** `src/commands/validate.ts` - `ValidateResult` (:12, today `{name, findings, warnings}`) gains `assessment: HygieneAssessment`, the value `inspectHygiene` returned, verbatim and in order, BOTH buckets; the counts are retained so existing callers are unaffected, and the exit-code gate, the printed lines and the errors-only fail-closed predicate are unchanged - no caller may re-derive a finding it was not given (EV-07). `--json` on the `validate` chain (cli.ts:114) prints exactly ONE line, `{name, errors, warnings}`, each finding `{code, path, line?, message}`, with the two arrays SEPARATE so no consumer can gate on a combined count, and no human lines on either the passing or the refusing path; `validate` records nothing, because hygiene is a gate and never a stored artifact, so every surface showing hygiene shows a check run NOW against the tree it can currently read and must be worded that way (EV-17). `src/commands/eval.ts` - `EvalResult` (:47-55) gains `summary: Aggregate`, the object `aggregate()` already returned and `renderReport()` already rendered, byte-for-byte, with NO recomputation and no additional field: nothing new is measured, this only stops an already-computed value from being discarded at the process boundary, which is what makes a run without `--commit` legible to a library caller without parsing printed text or re-aggregating raw rows (EV-08). `--judge-escalation-model <model>` is registered on the `eval` chain (cli.ts:116, which today registers k/triggersOnly/executionOnly/case/model/judgeModel/working/commit/gen/save/team) and threaded onto `EvalArgs` - eval-engine §7.5 already froze the sentence and the default, so the CODE is behind the SPEC and NO delta is required, which makes EV-11 the only row in M7 that needs code and no paragraph (EV-11). `src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts` - one enum member, `HYG7`, WARNING tier only: a relative path token in a text file that resolves to no key of the file map, qualifying only when it matches `[A-Za-z0-9_./-]+\.[A-Za-z0-9]{1,5}`, is not absolute, carries no URI scheme, and does not sit inside a fenced block or an inline code span, resolved case-sensitively against the skill folder's own keys, carrying the referencing file's path and 1-based line. Warning tier is load-bearing: an error-tier heuristic path matcher would refuse a `connect`, a `publish` or a `sync` write on a false positive (IB-05). `src/cli.ts` - `eval`'s one-line description must stop claiming the command never writes the team repository: without `--commit` a run writes only its local run tree, and with `--commit` it lands exactly ONE new receipt through `safeWrite` under guard action `eval` (row g at guard.ts:47, whose `permitsReceipt` at :62-74 requires one new path matching `RECEIPT_PATH` with a LOWERCASE 40-char version segment) whenever the run reaches the end - INCLUDING a run whose `execution_status` is `partial`. It is not written on a hygiene error finding, on a receipt that fails validation, or for a `--working` run (CP-02).

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** EV-04, EV-06, EV-18, IB-07. EV-04: five string templates, one per drawn paragraph, each a PURE FUNCTION of one parsed Receipt, every slot printed at the RECEIPT's own precision (`sign_p` with `toFixed(3)` exactly as `summarize()` does) and a template whose field is absent DROPS its clause rather than inventing a number - no arithmetic anywhere. EV-06: the size label is computed from the committed SKILL.md bytes using the spec's own method - `decodeUtf8(skillMd).length`, i.e. JS `String.prototype.length` on the DECODED string, NOT Rust `str::len()` UTF-8 bytes and NOT `chars().count()`, either of which would silently disagree with CI - `tokens ≈ round(units / 4)`, guideline `20_000 / 4 = 5_000`, rendered with an explicit approximation marker and WITHOUT the word "cap". EV-18: render `-` for any absent per-arm value, drop a row absent for every present arm, replace Table 2 entirely with one sentence when all three drop rather than showing an empty table, and SUPPRESS the ROI bar when the cost mean is null, because the bar LENGTH is a pure cost ratio. IB-07: enumerate `evals/**/*.json` in the clone against a per-machine watermark set; the item's stable id is the receipt PATH, immutable by construction because guard row g is append-only, so IB-01's identity hashing is not needed; for an app-invoked run the path comes back directly as `EvalResult.receiptPath` (eval.ts:54); a local run appears only when it committed. **Sync rule.** Receipts are append-only by guard row g, so nothing propagates or conflicts: `RECEIPT_PATH` (guard.ts:41) admits exactly one NEW file per eval mutation and refuses an uppercase version segment, which is the segment no reader would resolve. Hygiene results are never stored at all.

Frame mode retires it by a different route, and this must be written into the batch or the Codex run will not know why the field is reachable: ResultFrame now carries `value` (src/lib/frames.ts:24) and execute.ts gained the `result?(outcome)` hook that hands the verb's value to the frame channel on both the success and the failing-Result arms (src/lib/execute.ts:14-15,24-45). EV-17's own costing block rejected the ValidateResult-widening route on precisely the ground that is now dead - "Rejected alternative B (the coster's): widen ValidateResult with `details` - unreachable to any consumer that runs the published bin, because execute.ts:21-39 never serializes outcome.value" (costing EV-17, §2 body). So: dependsOn on the CLI half is []; the APP half acquires two real dependencies the batch does not declare - S7n (EV-13 receipt payload: EV-04, EV-18 and IB-07 all render from a parsed Receipt the adapter cannot obtain today, index.ts:66 gaps receipts() and index.ts:82 maps eval to `receipt: null`) and S7p (RM-01 SKILL.md payload, needed by EV-06; see §6).

=== 2. CP-02 is HALF DONE and the batch's own requirement is already satisfied ===
The batch says "eval's one-line description must stop claiming the command never writes the team repository". It stopped in commit 95511d4 ("feat(evals): IE5 - dynamic test generation"), which is inside b5c0507..85cc276, i.e. before the 18-commit delta. `git show b5c0507:src/cli.ts` → 'Evaluate a shared skill locally; never writes the team repository'; at 85cc276 and at f8557c4 → 'Evaluate a shared skill locally; generated assets stay local until reviewed' (`grep -c "never writes the team repository" src/cli.ts` = 0). The named test - cli.test.ts "eval help ... never claims it never writes the team repository" - passes today with no diff. What actually remains of CP-02 is the POSITIVE half plus the exception: the help names no team-repo write at all, and the partial-run exception (eval.ts gates only on args.commit) is undocumented and untested. Restate the row that way, or it will be built as a no-op. Note this also corrects the CLI-reality report, which flagged only RM-31 as materially stale in cli.ts.

=== 3. IB-07 as written is UNIMPLEMENTABLE in the shipped app, not merely mis-placed ===
"enumerate `evals/**/*.json` in the clone against a per-machine watermark set" requires filesystem access the app does not have and cannot be granted inside this batch. The Tauri capability set is opener/store/clipboard-manager/window-state only - there is no `fs:` permission (desktop/src-tauri/capabilities/default.json), the Rust half exposes exactly five commands (cli_spawn, cli_write, cli_kill, read_app_state, host_platform; lib.rs:36-37,137-140), and read_app_state reads one fixed path ($HOME/.terum/skills/run/app.json). The seam rule (desktop/AGENTS.md:12-22) additionally confines any such access to src/backend. IB-07 must therefore be respecified as a CLI-side payload - a receipt listing returned as a verb's `result.value` - which is S7n/EV-13's mechanism, or explicitly marked mock-only for this batch. As written it would either be built against the mock alone (leaving inbox() gapped, index.ts:68) or drive an implementer to add an fs permission, which is a new mechanism and outside the SIMPLE bar.

=== 4. IB-07 conflates two different EvalResults ===
"the path comes back directly as `EvalResult.receiptPath` (eval.ts:54)" is the CLI's EvalResult (src/commands/eval.ts:47-55, receiptPath?: string). The SEAM's EvalResult is `{name:string; receipt:Receipt|null}` (desktop/src/backend/types.ts:51) and the adapter maps eval to `{ name: value.name, receipt: null }` with `cliEval = z.object({ name }).passthrough()` (index.ts:23,82). Carrying receiptPath to the app is an undeclared seam DTO change plus an adapter schema/mapper change; neither appears in cliChanges or appHalf.

=== 5. EV-07 and EV-17 land in this batch with NO consumer, so GAPS.md:42 is not earned ===
Three things block the assessment from reaching a pixel: (a) the adapter's `cliValidate = z.object({ name, findings, warnings })` has NO `.passthrough()` (desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:29) - zod strips unknown keys silently, so a new `assessment` field is dropped without error; (b) the seam's `ValidateResult {name;findings;warnings}` (types.ts:53) has no place for it; (c) the Quality tab still renders the fixture triples and a hardcoded head - `<span>Hygiene checks · passed on share, 12 days ago · free, no model calls</span>` over `s.hygiene.map(([name,detail,status])=>…)` (desktop/src/screens/skill/SkillScreen.tsx:26). The costing's EV-17 App-side (render the six rows keyed by (code, clause), header "checked now", "not checked yet"/"could not check") is missing from appHalf entirely. Deleting GAPS.md:42 ("the app shows the fixture's pass|warn|fail as drawn") while nothing makes the app show real hygiene is a false status edit. Either add the three app-side edits to this batch or leave GAPS.md:42 alone.

=== 6. EV-06 has no input in the seam and drops the costing's second rule ===
"decodeUtf8(skillMd).length" has no raw string to run on: the seam carries `skillMd:{frontmatter:string; body:SkillMdBlock[]}` where `SkillMdBlock {kind:'h2'|'p'|'ol'|'code'; content:string|string[]}` (types.ts:16,19) - a structured decomposition, not the file text, so a reassembly will not equal the bytes HYG6 measures (hygiene.ts:92-95 counts String.prototype.length over the WHOLE decoded SKILL.md including frontmatter). Worse, EV-06's own board list is card-level (SkillCard, Library, Marketplace…) and SkillCard carries only the display string `size` plus `tokensK` parsed back out of it (types.ts:14; derive.ts:42-45) - no skillMd at all. So EV-06 needs a numeric size on the card payload (RM-30/S7f) or a raw skillMd string (S7p). Also omitted: the costing's SECOND EV-06 rule, that the Marketplace "Context ≤ 5k tokens" facet must evaluate the exact code-unit count and never a parsed display string, because that facet REMOVES skills from the view (costing EV-06 body). That rule is the D20-relevant half and must not be dropped.

=== 7. EV-18 drops the two rules the costing says make it SIMPLE ===
The batch lists three render rules; the costing has four, and rules (3) and (4) are deletions: the Run-eval dialog keeps the countable half and DELETES "roughly N minutes and about $M of API spend", and the button tooltip loses the same two figures - "Deleting the priced half is what makes the row SIMPLE" (costing EV-18 body). Those live in `eval_estimate_text` and `eval_estimate_tip` (desktop/src/backend/mock/derive.ts:68-69) and in the seam's `evalEstimateText`/`evalEstimateTip` (types.ts:19), i.e. two more files than the batch budgets.

=== 8. EV-04 rewrites text on pixel-locked boards; the risk field does not say so ===
EvaluationReport renders the five prose blocks verbatim from the receipt object - `<RichText text={rc.abstract}/>`, `rc.results`, `rc.trigger_text`, `rc.efficiency_text`, `rc.coverage` (desktop/src/components/domain/EvaluationReport.tsx:16). Replacing them with templates changes rendered characters on every board that shows the report unless the templates reproduce the fixture prose (including its <b> markup) byte-for-byte. That is an oracle re-lock, not a refactor, and it belongs in `risk` and in `size`.

=== 9. GAPS.md instructions are wrong and partly forbidden ===
(a) "the mock clause of GAPS.md:32 that is not per_case" does not exist: line 32 is per_case end to end - "…the receipt has `comparisons`/`arm_scores`/`efficiency` and no `per_case` block. Flag `capabilities.perCaseEvalTables`; the mock's receipts carry `per_case` (an additive ask to Ajay, eval-engine §5.3)" (desktop/GAPS.md:32). The instruction is vacuous and will be guessed at. (b) An implementing agent may not edit GAPS.md at all: "Gaps are recorded by the maintainers in `GAPS.md`; an implementing agent never edits `GAPS.md`…" and "FIDELITY.md · GAPS.md maintainer-owned status files (never edited by an implementing agent)" (desktop/AGENTS.md:26,:102). Move both GAPS edits out of the Codex run into a maintainer step.

=== 10. IB-07's id change ripples further than stated ===
Mock ids are `kind+('-'+sub)+'-'+name` (mock/data.ts:9), the default selection is the literal 'share-secret-scan' (data.ts:10), and `selectedInboxId` feeds URL state (app/url-state.ts:9), with desktop/src/backend/__tests__/inbox-ids.test.ts pinning them. Receipt-path ids give the eval kind a second id scheme and change that board's URL. Name it, or keep the existing id shape and store the receipt path as a field.

=== 11. It is not one Codex run / one PR, and it is not M ===
The rationale itself says "review THREE PRs in ascending difficulty", which contradicts the batch shape. Counted: CLI src 4 (validate.ts, eval.ts, cli.ts, lib/evals/hygiene.ts) + CLI tests 4-5 (commands/__tests__/{validate,eval}.test.ts, __tests__/cli.test.ts, lib/evals/__tests__/hygiene.test.ts, and IB-05's costing also names commands/__tests__/publish.test.ts) + spec deltas in FOUR separate sections of one file (§6.0 eval bullet for EV-08, §6.0 validate bullet for EV-07, §6.0 invariant for CP-02, §9 twice for EV-17 and IB-05) + app: EvaluationReport.tsx, SkillScreen.tsx, derive.ts, mock/data.ts, backend/types.ts, backend/tauri/index.ts, inbox screen/reports, GAPS.md + app tests + a board re-lock. Split at the batch's own three tiers, and re-price L.

=== 13. One implementation constraint to write down ===
`--json` cannot be a raw stdout write: verbs are ESLint-forbidden from touching process.stdout (src/lib/prompt.ts's rule, proven by prompt.test.ts), so it must go through `io.print`, which under `--frames` becomes a PRINT FRAME, not a stdout line (frames.ts:19-25). Write the test against the sink, not stdout, and note that the app never needs `--json` at all - it reads `result.value.assessment` off the result frame; `--json` is terminal/CI-only value.

Desktop layout is colocated *.test.ts(x) plus __tests__ dirs - but the batch names ZERO app-side tests for four app-side rows; add them (receipt-rules.test.tsx, library-skill.test.tsx, inbox.test.tsx, backend/__tests__).

### S7n. The receipt reader and the §12 UI contract (IB-02 keeps its eval half here; its ls input is BM-04 in S7f)

- **Rows:** EV-13, CP-11, CP-15, IB-02
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7m
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** S / Ajay
- **Decisions applied (Teddy, 2026-09-08):** D-BM-7 = A: the receipt verb ships --runs (three lines over the same listing); EV-16 becomes display-permission only.

**CLI change.** New `src/lib/evals/receipts.ts` exporting `latestReceipt(clone, skillId, version)` and the committed run list per version - the fourth reader of the rule `latestReceiptVerdict` already implements privately at src/lib/readme.ts:172-181 (readdir the version directory, take the lexicographically newest `.json`, validate it, ENOENT means absent), factored out so the README column, CI and the UI cannot drift. Plus one new read-only verb, `receipt <ref> [--skill-version <tree>] [--runs]`, registered as a SIBLING of `eval`, never as `eval show`, because `eval <skill>` already binds its positional (cli.ts:116) so a subcommand would be swallowed as the skill name. It reads the local team clone ONLY - never fetches, never writes, never recomputes - and a receipt whose `skill_id` or `version` disagrees with its PATH is treated as absent, exactly as the README column already does. It is deliberately FAIL-OPEN like that column and not fail-closed like the publish receipt check, so a malformed newest receipt makes the UI show no receipt while CI still blocks the publish; where the UI and CI can therefore disagree, CI is authoritative. Read-only means no `safeWrite`, no `GuardAction` and no `PEOPLE_ACTIONS` entry. CP-11, CP-15 and IB-02 carry NO code - they are three eval-engine §12 paragraphs: (a) a viewer-SET filter over a receipt field is permitted and is not a derived statistic, subject to two §5.4 constraints - a lift threshold applies only within one `(provenance.model, provenance.cc_version)` pair, so a surface either scopes itself to one pair or shows provenance beside the facet and EXCLUDES receipts from another pair rather than silently comparing them; and a filtered list is still ordered by install count (D38), never by the facet's own number; (b) every receipt-DERIVED facet ships OFF, the filter control shows a zero badge on first run, and the unfiltered catalog is what a new viewer sees, because a default PASS-only or lift-floor filter is the product ranking on the viewer's behalf using precisely the numbers our own probe shows do not rank (Spearman ρ −0.07 against SkillsBench all-harness, −0.22 against its claude-code column); (c) IB-02's cross-version paragraph ONLY if Teddy keeps `report_regression`'s figure, and then each receipt's own provenance line must sit beside its own numbers with the comparison SUPPRESSED entirely when model or cc_version differ.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** The evals rail and report read through the seam's `receipts({skillId, version})` instead of the fixture, and the Marketplace filter control ships with a zero badge and no active facets on first run. The app must not promote a partial receipt (readme.ts:196-199's rule) and must not merge two receipts of one version into one figure, table or derived number. Delete GAPS.md:40.

**Sync rule.** Read-only over committed files. The version key is the 40-char lowercase tree hash, resolved with `latestTree` (readme.ts:119-122) or `skillVersions` (S7d) - never the 8-char `LsSkill.latest`, which `shortHash` (readme.ts:224) truncates and which is unusable as a directory key.

**Tests.** `src/lib/evals/__tests__/receipts.test.ts`: returns the lexicographically newest schema-valid receipt for a version; treats a receipt whose `skill_id` or `version` disagrees with its path as ABSENT; returns the committed run ids for a version; never writes and never spawns a fetch.

**Rationale and risk.** Serialization edge: both batches edit `src/cli.ts`'s eval region and the `src/lib/evals/` area. Placed second because the reader is the piece the app's whole evals surface hangs on, and it is cleaner to add it once the payload rows (EV-07, EV-08) have settled the types it returns. The verb must be a sibling, not `eval show` - a subcommand under `eval <skill>` is silently swallowed as the skill name, which is a bug commander will not report.

**Verifier corrections (two adversarial lenses at 85cc276 and f8557c4; authoritative over the batch text above).**

- Lens 1 (corrections apply): S7n is CHANGED per the delta (nothing of EV-13/CP-11/CP-15/IB-02 landed in the 18 commits), and the batch already honours the important half of that change by delivering the reader as a `receipt` VERB rather than an exported reader - that is correct and should stand. Nine material problems remain.

1. SEAM DTO BREAK - the batch's central unaddressed gap. `receipts(q:{skillId;version}):Promise<Result<Receipt|null>>` (desktop/src/backend/Backend.ts:9) where `Receipt = NonNullable<Design['DETAIL']['receipt']>` (desktop/src/backend/types.ts:15). That fixture object has 24 keys: abstract, arm, attribution, catalog, cc, coverage, date, eff, efficiency_text, engine, inc_p, incumbent, judge, k, model, per_case, results, run_id, runner, sign_p, timestamp, trigger_text, triggers, when. The CLI's receiptSchema (src/lib/evals/receipt.ts:50-93) has NONE of abstract/results/trigger_text/efficiency_text/coverage/catalog/incumbent/inc_p/eff/per_case/when/date; it nests model/judge_model/cc_version/engine_version/k/runner_handle under `provenance`; and `sign_p` is a NUMBER per arm inside `comparisons`, not a top-level string. So a `latestReceipt()` that returns a schema-valid CLI receipt CANNOT satisfy `receipts()`. The batch names no DTO change, no adapter mapping, no prose-synthesis rule - and its own §12 paragraph (b) forbids the app inventing them. Either the seam's `Receipt` narrows to what the CLI actually stores (a boards consequence for §6), or the batch must state where the five prose blocks and the display triples come from. As written the batch cannot land.

2. `receipts()` HAS ZERO UI CONSUMERS TODAY, so "read through the seam's `receipts()` instead of the fixture" is a rewire, not a swap. grep for `receipts(` over desktop/src excluding src/backend returns nothing. The evals rail and report read `SkillDetail` from `skill()`: SkillScreen.tsx:41 renders `<EvaluationReport name={s.name} version={s.version} receipt={s.receipt} summary={s.summary} incumbentLift={s.incumbentLift} numbers={s.reportNumbers} method={s.method}/>` plus `<HistoryRail history={s.history}/>`, and DetailRail (SkillScreen.tsx:23) reads `rc.when`, `rc.runner`, `rc.model`, `rc.cc`, `rc.k`. summary / history[].summary / reportNumbers / incumbentLift / method are SkillDetail-only derived fields that `receipts()` does not return, and `skill()` is still `gap('Skill detail')` (desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:65). The evals surface therefore cannot become real from S7n alone - undeclared dependency on S7p.

3. THE MARKETPLACE DEFAULT-OFF IS NOT AN APP-SIDE CHANGE, and it breaks an existing test. `catalog.filterDefault` is `design.FILTER_DEFAULT` verbatim (mock/data.ts:25); design.json is written only by `python3 tools/export-design.py` from the design canvas and is never hand-edited (desktop/AGENTS.md invariant 3). Today FILTER_DEFAULT = {verdicts:['PASS'], lift_min:20, tokens_max:5.0, installs_min:3} and DERIVED.filterCount = 6. market-filters.tsx:12 seeds the verdict checkboxes from `catalog.filterDefault.verdicts` and :14 seeds the three Sliders from lift_min/tokens_max/installs_min with a footer button reading `Show {catalog.filterCount} skills`. marketplace.test.tsx:13 asserts `getByRole('checkbox',{name:/PASS/})` IS CHECKED and the button reads `Show ${design.DERIVED.filterCount} skills`. So "ships with a zero badge and no active facets on first run" requires a build.py/design-canvas change (Teddy), a .shots re-lock of MarketplaceFilters at tolerance screen 0.0030, and an update to that test. Approver "Ajay" alone is wrong for this row.

4. "Delete GAPS.md:40" IS THE WRONG LINE AND THE WRONG ACTOR. GAPS.md:40 is MarketplaceFilters - "`search` is skills-only substring over the clone; no facet filtering. Filters are app-side over the catalog." S7n adds no CLI-side facet filtering and paragraph (a) deliberately KEEPS the filter viewer-set and app-side, so the gap that line records is not closed. Separately, desktop/AGENTS.md:23-28 states "an implementing agent never edits `GAPS.md`, `FIDELITY.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `README.md` or `package.json` - describe what you found in the report's `openQuestions`/`deviations` instead", so instructing the Codex run to delete a GAPS line breaks the repo's own rule. For reference GAPS.md carries no receipt-reader bullet at all; the receipt-adjacent lines are :20 (receipt-check as a hidden CI verb, "no seam method, no screen"), :44 (12-char hashes) and :49 (no --json mode). The real maintainer edit here is re-anchoring GAPS.md:1, which still reads "pinned at b5c0507".

5. THREE MISSING CLI/ADAPTER EDITS, all forced by the shipped frame protocol. (a) The new verb must join `FRAME_VERBS` (src/lib/frames.ts:32) or a shell cannot discover it in the hello frame - that list is 20 entries today, not the 21 the delta states (login, setup, team create/join/remove/leave/workflow-update, invite, ls, status, publish, validate, eval, connect, install, uninstall-skill, uninstall, sync, search, update). (b) It needs a `CliVerbs` slot so it is injectable in tests, the pattern `app?` follows at src/cli.ts:34-37. (c) The adapter must replace `receipts: async () => gap('Eval receipts')` (desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:66) with a real mapping plus a `.passthrough()` cliReceipt schema beside the twelve at index.ts:17-30. None of the three is in cliChanges.

6. ZERO-PROMPT IS A HARD CONSTRAINT, NOT AN IMPLICATION. `read()` iterates the frames and on ANY ask cancels the process and fails with 'terum-skills asked "<question>" during a read-only call…' (desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:46-53). "Read-only, never fetches, never writes" does not by itself guarantee prompt-free: the batch must say how `receipt` resolves a multi-team config without asking (the `--team` question other verbs raise), and the tests line should assert it emits no ask frame.

7. THE READER IS THREE FUNCTIONS, SO THE "CANNOT DRIFT" CLAIM IS HALF TRUE. readme.ts has `latestReceiptVerdict` (filesystem, :172-181, called at :137), `latestReceiptVerdictInTree` (MutableTree, :184-190, called at :166 - the safeWrite post-image fallback), and the shared `receiptVerdict` (:192-200) which is where the skill_id/version-vs-path rule and the partial rule (:197-199) actually live. A single `latestReceipt(clone, skillId, version)` signature serves the fs caller only; the in-tree caller reads a post-image and cannot take a clone path. Factor the shared VALIDATOR and keep two enumerators, or the safeWrite path silently keeps its own copy of the rule.

8. dependsOn IS INCOMPLETE (S7m itself is right). S7m is a correct serialization edge - validate/receipt-check/eval sit at src/cli.ts:115/116/117 and both batches work src/lib/evals/. Two edges are missing: (i) syncRule depends on `skillVersions (S7d)` for the 40-char tree-hash key, and S7d is itself CHANGED by the delta (its RM-10/RM-22/CP-35 exported readers are moot as exports and must be rebuilt as verb payloads), so `skillVersions` may not exist in the form cited; (ii) the appHalf depends on S7p per finding 2. No re-pointing is needed for the retired library-entry batch - S7n does not depend on S7a.

9. ANCHOR DRIFT + A NAMING HAZARD. The risk's citation "cli.ts:116" was correct at b5c0507 (eval was 116 there, receipt-check 115) but at cli-main f8557c4 the file shifted: 115 validate, 116 receipt-check, 117 `program.command('eval <skill>')`. The underlying risk claim is sound and worth keeping. Two adjacent hazards it does not mention: the new public verb `receipt` sits one hyphen from the existing hidden `receipt-check` (cli.ts:116), and the proposed `src/lib/evals/receipts.ts` sits one letter from the existing `src/lib/evals/receipt.ts` (with `__tests__/receipt.test.ts` already present) - pick distinct names.

TESTS AND SIZE. `src/lib/evals/__tests__/receipts.test.ts` fits the CLI layout exactly (that directory already holds ten files: agent/checks/execution/generate/hygiene/judge/receipt/results/stats/triggers.test.ts), and the four assertions are all describable there against the injected Runner. The desktop half names no test though both layouts exist: colocated (desktop/src/screens/marketplace/marketplace.test.tsx) and __tests__ (desktop/src/backend/tauri/__tests__/run.test.ts) - add an adapter test at desktop/src/backend/tauri/__tests__/ and update marketplace.test.tsx:13 if finding 3 survives. On "one Codex run + one PR": CLI and app are one repo now (desktop/ inside terum-skills), so one PR is mechanically possible, but the batch spans four owners - CLI code (Ryan), app code (agent), the design canvas FILTER_DEFAULT + a board re-lock (Teddy), and three §12 eval-engine paragraphs (Ajay) whose home is not this repo at all (cli-main has no specs/, only docs/frame-protocol.md). Split the §12 paragraphs and the filter default out; the residue - shared validator, `receipt` verb, FRAME_VERBS/CliVerbs entries, adapter mapping - is genuinely size S.

D20: no violation. A viewer-set marketplace facet is per-viewer by construction and is never rendered as a team-wide fact, and the batch adds no localStorage-backed team-wide claim. The seam rule (AGENTS.md invariant 1) is also respected - the work stays inside src/backend/ and the CLI - except for the GAPS.md edit in finding 4.

Nine corrections, one of them disqualifying.

ANCHORS THAT VERIFY (read this session in cli-clone; all three files are byte-identical at 85cc276 and at today's f8557c4 - `git diff --stat 85cc276..HEAD -- src/lib/readme.ts src/commands/receiptCheck.ts src/lib/version.ts src/lib/evals/receipt.ts src/lib/config.ts` is empty):

1. DISQUALIFYING - "factored out so the README column, CI and the UI cannot drift" inverts the costing's own scope rule and breaks the SIMPLE bar. EV-13's costed body (costing:601, "Verb or flag") says in capitals: "CRITICAL SCOPE RULE: do NOT rewire src/lib/readme.ts or src/commands/receiptCheck.ts onto it in this change - their fail-open vs fail-closed behaviours differ on purpose and re-pointing either one would change the README column or the publish gate, dragging in a second spec section (.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md section 6.1 :289 and section 11 :470-476) and breaking condition (5)." Its "Why SIMPLE" repeats the conditional: "SIMPLE holds only with the scope rule above … the coster's 'extract once and have readme.ts call it' is NOT a free refactor." I verified the divergence in the clone: readme.ts:200 swallows a malformed newest receipt (`catch { return undefined; }`, README prints a dash) while src/commands/receiptCheck.ts:104-107 THROWS (`schema-invalid receipt <path>: invalid JSON.` / `schema-invalid receipt <path>.`), with the reason stated in its docstring at receiptCheck.ts:91 ("A malformed newest file fails closed rather than hiding behind an older run"); receiptCheck.ts:94 also filters `entry.isFile()` where readme.ts:177 does not. The batch's own prose is self-contradictory here - it asserts a single factored reader AND that "the UI shows no receipt while CI still blocks the publish", which the same helper cannot do without a mode parameter and two callers rewired. FIX: state that src/lib/evals/receipts.ts is NEW and ADDITIVE, that readme.ts and receiptCheck.ts are deliberately NOT re-pointed, and add the two regression tests the costing lists and the batch omits - `src/lib/__tests__/readme.test.ts: existing cases stay green` and `src/commands/__tests__/receiptCheck.test.ts: existing cases stay green`. Drop "so the README column, CI and the UI cannot drift"; the honest claim is "so the UI does not add a fifth divergent copy".

2. Reader count is wrong: it is the FIFTH, not the fourth. The costing flags this as "the coster's most materially stale paragraph and both refuters caught it" (costing:600): at b5c0507 there are already four private ones - latestReceiptVerdict (readme.ts:172-181), latestReceiptVerdictInTree (readme.ts:184-190), receiptVerdict (readme.ts:192-201), and receiptCheck.ts's receiptFiles/newestReceiptAt/hasSchemaValidReceipt (:92-97, :100-109, :111-119, all confirmed in the clone) - plus two cross-version walkers, latestReceiptedTree (src/commands/eval.ts:252-267) and validReceiptsOutsideVersion (receiptCheck.ts:121-129). Note also that the costing's own paste-ready §12 paragraph still says "It is a third reader of the same rule" (costing:605); whichever number S7n lands, the spec text and the batch text must agree.

3. POST-DELTA, the batch has no delivery mechanism. The costed verb PRINTS the receipt as JSON via `io.print`. Over frames a print is a `print` frame, and the adapter reads only `result.value` (desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:47-53 `read()`, and the map applied to the parsed value). So EV-13 must put the receipt (and the run-id list) on the verb's Result VALUE, not on stdout - the delta says exactly this for S7n. Additive under protocol 1 (docs/frame-protocol.md:61-63). Add it to cliChanges explicitly; without it the app gets a print frame it is not allowed to parse and `receipts()` stays gapped.

4. POST-DELTA, the new verb must be registered in FRAME_VERBS. src/lib/frames.ts:32 @ f8557c4 lists 20 verbs and deliberately excludes hidden maintenance verbs - which is why `receipt-check` is absent. A `receipt` verb that is not added there is never advertised in the hello frame. Same edit S7l and S7z already carry; S7n's cliChanges omits it. (Aside for the delta record: that list has 20 entries, not the 21 the delta fact `hello-frame-payload` states.)

5. The verb signature drops `--team`, and that is a hard failure on a multi-team machine. The costing's registration includes `.option('--team <team>', 'configured team (required when more than one exists)')` (costing:601); the batch's is `receipt <ref> [--skill-version <tree>] [--runs]`. `selectTeam` (src/lib/config.ts:23-30) THROWS `More than one team is configured; pass --team <name> (…)` and never prompts - good for the adapter's read(), which cancels on any ask (index.ts:50), but it means `receipts()` hard-fails for anyone with two teams, and the adapter passes no `--team` on any call today.

6. Seam key mismatch, and the seam's Receipt is not the CLI's receipt. `receipts(q: { skillId: string; version: string })` (desktop/src/backend/Backend.ts:9) keys on the receipt path's uuid, while the verb takes `<ref>` (a skill name, as install/eval do). Either the verb must accept a uuid ref or the adapter needs a uuid→name map that no verb returns (RM-30/ls territory). Separately, the seam's `Receipt | null` is the fixture-shaped DISPLAY object (abstract, results, trigger_text, efficiency_text, coverage, sign_p as a string, arm/eff triples), none of which exist in receiptSchema (src/lib/evals/receipt.ts:50-93) - so the appHalf's "the evals rail and report read through the seam's receipts() instead of the fixture" is not closed by EV-13 alone. Today the adapter hard-gaps it (`receipts: async () => gap('Eval receipts')`, desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:66) and returns `receipt: null` from eval (:82).

One refinement to the syncRule's own claim: resolveVersion DOES accept a short display prefix and normalises it to the lowercase 40-char tree (version.ts:11-20, rejecting a 39-char truncation explicitly), so the verb may accept the short form even though the DIRECTORY key must be the full 40-char hash.

### S7o. endorsements list and invite --cancel

- **Rows:** IB-06, TJ-11, CP-30
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7h
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** S / Ryan
- **Decisions applied (Teddy, 2026-09-08):** A1 (Teddy 2026-09-08): keep CP-30; --project is a note-composer that chooses what the invitee is TOLD (remote + install line), never what the invitation authorises; TJ-01 stays dropped.

**CLI change.** APPEND CP-30's half: --project <key> on invite, validating the key against team.json's projects registry and composing the Slack-ready join block around it - naming the project's remote and the install project <key> line the invitee will run. It gives the drawn scoping selector a real argument WITHOUT membership: no team.json members field, no §6.0 row, no placement. That distinction is load-bearing, because TJ-01 (invitation actually scoped to a project) is NOT built - so the selector chooses what the invitee is TOLD, never what the invitation authorizes. Under --cancel the flag is refused, as the join block is not printed there. If Teddy's TJ-01 drop takes the whole scoping selector off the canvas, this flag is moot and comes back out.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** The Share screen's pending-invitations list and its Cancel action, and the Inbox review pane's open-endorsement list, both of which were fixture-only. `InviteResult`'s new arms let the app render the outcome from the Result rather than from the printed lines (D21). The invited row's Cancel is the only destructive host action the app gains in M7, so it takes a confirm naming the login. Delete GAPS.md:38's invite clause and the endorsement-PR clause.

**Sync rule.** None - neither row touches the clone, the ledger or any stamp.

**Tests.** `src/commands/__tests__/endorsements.test.ts`: lists every `publish/*` head with its open PR URL and author; drops a branch whose PR is not open; states that PR state was not checked without `gh`; a failed `ls-remote` is an error, not an empty list. `invite.test.ts`: `--cancel` deletes the pending invitation for a GitHub login, including one on page TWO of the paginated list (reuse the page-two invitations fixture); refuses on a non-admin `permissions.admin`; writes nothing to the team repo.

**Rationale and risk.** Depends on S7h because S7h lifts the paginated invitations read out of `src/commands/team.ts:115-117` (dropping the `.filter(...)` that narrows to one login) into a shared reader, and S7o consumes that reader for the DELETE - one owner for one read, rather than two batches each writing their own pagination. Both rows touch the GitHub host. `endorsements` must never turn a failed `ls-remote` into an empty list (a UI showing "no open endorsements" for a network failure is the exact dishonesty the §6 footer rule exists to prevent), and `--cancel` must run the admin gate BEFORE any DELETE.

CORRECTION 1 - CP-30's cliChanges contradicts the string CP-30 itself ships, and CP-30 is a design row.
CP-30's costed verdict is SIMPLE · design (costing:187; listed under "SIMPLE · design" at costing:1879), and its CLI-side note is verbatim: "Recorded, not recommended - the row is fixed by a design string today." Its paste-ready fix is THREE strings (costing:1293, repeated at :1624), the third of which is the docstring `"""Invite scoping (the sketch): the whole team, or one project. A note the inviter composes, not an argument: `invite` takes GitHub logins and no scope, and team.json projects carry remotes + skills, never members."""` Landing `--project <key>` makes the very sentence this row exists to write false. You cannot ship both halves of CP-30 in one batch. Either drop the flag, or rewrite string (3) in the same PR and say so explicitly.

CORRECTION 2 - `--project` builds an argument for a control the costing recommends deleting.
TJ-01's design consequence (costing:1324, :1729) removes `scoping_rows` (build.py:3142-3148) AND its call site in `invite_dialog` (build.py:3164) AND the five ShareStates specimens (build.py:3242-3244). The batch's own hedge - "If Teddy's TJ-01 drop takes the whole scoping selector off the canvas, this flag is moot and comes back out" - is hedging against the RECOMMENDED outcome, not an unlikely one. Gate the flag on Teddy's TJ-01 call before any CLI work, or cut it.

CORRECTION 3 - `--project` collides with a flag the CLI already shipped.
`FRAME_FEATURES` (cli-main src/lib/frames.ts:34-42) declares `inviteScoping: false`, documented at :35-37 as "a drawn control a real shell must hide or grey; flipping one is a product decision". A `--project` that governs only what the invitee is TOLD (never what the invitation authorizes) cannot honestly flip that true - so the app keeps hiding the selector the flag exists to feed, and two surfaces assert opposite things about one control. This is a second, independent reason the append is not free.

CORRECTION 4 - a new verb now costs two edits the cliChanges does not name, and IB-06's `hidden: true` conflicts with one of them.
(a) `FRAME_VERBS` (cli-main src/lib/frames.ts:32) lists 20 public verbs; `endorsements` is not among them, and its comment at :30 says "hidden maintenance verbs and `share` are not listed". IB-06's costed registration is `program.command('endorsements', { hidden: true })` (costing:653). So either endorsements ships NON-hidden (a deviation from the registration Ryan approves) or it ships hidden and never appears in `hello.verbs`. Functionally the app can still spawn it - run.ts:63 returns immediately on the hello frame and reads nothing from it - but the decision must be recorded, not left implicit. (b) `CliVerbs` (cli-main src/cli.ts:33) lists 20 keys and none is `endorsements`; the slot plus its default in `active` (cli.ts:36) is unnamed here, where MC-10's costed body names that same step explicitly for `hook`.

"`InviteResult`'s new arms let the app render the outcome from the Result rather than from the printed lines (D21)" is false on the CLI side and incomplete on the app side.
