- Lens 2 (corrections apply): S7o is NOT DONE/MOOT - no endorsement, cancel or scoping code landed in the 18-commit delta (invite.ts changed only at :38-41, the 404 wording; team.ts only at :500-502). But eight material defects, three of them seam-breaking.

1. cliChanges covers only one of the three rows. The field describes CP-30's `--project` and nothing else. IB-06's new `endorsements` verb and TJ-11's `--cancel` appear only in `tests` and `risk`. A Codex run reading cliChanges builds `--project` and neither of the other two rows. TJ-11's CLI change must be stated: `.option('--cancel', …)` on the `invite` registration; `InviteArgs.cancel?: boolean` (invite.ts:10); `InviteResult` gains `cancelled` and `notPending` (invite.ts:11); the admin probe (team.ts:104-106) and the paginated invitations read (team.ts:115-117) and DELETE (team.ts:132) are LIFTED out of `remove()`'s admin branch; `paginatedItems` is module-private at team.ts:147 and must be exported. IB-06's must state the new command file plus `run(args, io): Promise<Result<{open: OpenEndorsement[]; prsChecked: boolean}>>` reusing one `git ls-remote --heads origin 'refs/heads/publish/*'` plus ONE `gh pr list … --json url,headRefName,author,title` (costing:653), not the N per-branch `--head` lookups publish.ts:200 does today.

2. THE SEAM DTOs DO NOT SUPPORT THE appHalf. `InviteResult` is `{invited:string[]}` and `InviteArgs` is `{logins:string[];scope?:Scope;role?:string}` (desktop/src/backend/types.ts:44-45). There is no `cancel`, no `cancelled`, no `notPending`, no `already`, no `failed`. The claim "`InviteResult`'s new arms let the app render the outcome from the Result (D21)" is therefore not implementable as specified - the batch must name the types.ts change, the mock's invite change, AND the adapter mapping at desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:79, which today constructs `{ invited: [...value.invited] }` and discards everything else the CLI returns. This is the single largest omission.

3. `--project <key>` does not match what the drawn dialog sends. ShareInvite.tsx:15 initialises `project` from `catalog.projects[0]?.name` and :17 calls `backend.invite({logins, scope: scope==='team'?'Global':project, role})` - a display NAME. team.json's registry is keyed by KEY (market-components.tsx:19 prints `team.json · projects.${q.key}`; the PROJECTS fixture carries both `name` and `key`). A flag that validates the key against the registry rejects every value the board sends. Either the app half must switch to `q.key` (an app change the batch does not list) or the CLI must resolve case-insensitively - which is RM-15, owned by S7y, so a new undeclared dependsOn. Separately, the adapter drops `scope` and `role` today (index.ts:79); mapping `scope`→`--project` is unbuilt app work the appHalf never mentions.

4. CP-30 is costed SIMPLE · design and "Recorded, not recommended". The costing's own row says "the row is fixed by a design string today. This is named so HARD is not mistaken for the only CLI-side option" (costing:187, :1293), it is listed under SIMPLE · design at :1879, and its §6 consequence says the scoping control "stays a recorded decision rather than a fix" (:1624) with THREE build.py strings to change (build.py:3143, :3144, :3146-3147). The batch promotes it to a CLI flag and ships none of the three strings, so CP-30 is not actually closed by this batch. It also collides with the delta: FRAME_FEATURES declares `inviteScoping: false` (src/lib/frames.ts:39-42) and the block comment at :36-38 says every false "is a drawn control a real shell must hide or grey; flipping one is a product decision, not a frame-mode change." Adding `--project` requires flipping that key and names no approver for it. Finally the batch's own hedge ("if Teddy's TJ-01 drop takes the whole scoping selector off the canvas, this flag is moot and comes back out") makes CP-30 a GATE on an undecided design call, not a buildable item. Recommendation: split CP-30 out of S7o.

5. The new `endorsements` verb has no frame-mode wiring. Per the delta, a new verb costs a FRAME_VERBS entry, a Backend method and an adapter mapping. `FRAME_VERBS` (frames.ts:32) lists 20 verbs and its header at :31 says "hidden maintenance verbs and `share` are not listed" - while IB-06's costed registration is `program.command('endorsements', { hidden: true })` (costing:653). The batch must decide: register non-hidden and add it to FRAME_VERBS, or accept a verb the hello frame never advertises. It also needs a 28th Backend member plus an adapter mapping through `read()` (index.ts:46-53), which cancels the process on any ask - fine, since endorsements never prompts, but unstated. And IB-06's costed App-side route ("the app shells `gh pr list` itself") is now DEAD: AGENTS.md invariant 1 and desktop/src/backend/__tests__/seam.test.ts block node built-ins and non-backend native imports, and the Rust bridge exposes exactly five commands - cli_spawn, cli_write, cli_kill, read_app_state, host_platform (src-tauri/src/lib.rs:36-37, 137-140) - none of them a generic exec. IB-06 collapses from "both" to cli-only; record it.

6. dependsOn is misattributed, and one dependency is missing. The rationale says S7h "lifts the paginated invitations read … into a shared reader". The line citation is exact (team.ts:115-117 is the `--paginate --slurp` read whose `.filter(…)` narrows to one login, verified on cli-main), but the costing assigns that lift to TJ-11, i.e. to THIS batch: "the listing+DELETE block currently sits inside remove()'s admin-gated branch, so it is lifted, not copied" and "`paginatedItems` is module-private at src/commands/team.ts:147 … and must be exported or lifted to a lib" (costing:1129-1142). S7h's rows are costed as one flag on `ls` (TJ-04 at costing:89, TJ-05 at :90, "one flag, SHARED with TJ-04"), which does not by itself produce an unfiltered DELETE-capable reader. Either amend S7h's record to own the lift explicitly or reverse the edge; as written the lift is unowned - precisely the "two batches each writing their own pagination" the rationale claims to prevent. MISSING dependsOn: the app half's endorsement list lands in the Inbox, and `inbox()` returns the hard-coded GAPS.md failure in the real adapter (index.ts:61-69, alongside roster()). S7h covers roster(); nothing in dependsOn covers inbox().

7. syncRule contradicts the shipped adapter. "None - neither row touches the clone, the ledger or any stamp" is correct about the CLI (invite.ts:13: "It deliberately has no team-repo write path"), but the adapter declares ChangeSource `['clone']` on invite (tauri/index.ts:79), so every successful invite/cancel fires clone subscribers today. Either the syncRule is wrong or that array must be emptied in this batch's app half.

8. Two design-side obligations are unowned, and one GAPS.md instruction points at nothing.
   (a) The Cancel affordance is not on the canvas. `InvitedRow` (ShareScreen.tsx:52) renders five cells - name, "Invited", -, -, - - with no action column. TJ-11's §6 consequence (costing:1738) requires a new "Cancel invitation" hover/overflow control (build.py:3042-3044) AND says "THE ROW'S IDENTITY MUST SHRINK to what a pending invitation actually carries", dropping name/initials/role; `Design['INVITED']` is still `{handle,name,role,initials,invited,by}` (desktop/src/fixtures/schema.ts:28) and `Roster.invited` is typed straight off it (types.ts:26). Neither the fixture shrink nor the board change is in the batch, and the approver list is "Ryan" only - both are Teddy's design calls against pixel-locked boards.
   (b) IB-06's four string moves (costing:1669) are likewise absent: the review pane is a single-item `ReviewReport` (reports.tsx:57), not a list, and it prints "Review request." and "{actor} proposes endorsing" - the addressed-to-you framing the costing proves has no producer, since `gh pr create` (publish.ts:128-134) passes no `--reviewer`. Building the app half without those strings ships an assertion the CLI cannot back.
   (c) "Delete GAPS.md:38's invite clause and the endorsement-PR clause": there IS no endorsement-PR clause - I read all 49 lines / 37 bullets of desktop/GAPS.md; nothing mentions endorsements or open PRs (costing §8 maps IB-06 to GAPS.md:29, the shared inboxEventLog flag line, which S7o cannot delete because IB-01/IB-02/MC-02 also live there). And GAPS.md:38's clauses are "role chip Admin · Member, invite scoping, last seen" - deleting the invite-scoping clause claims a closure that is contingent on CP-30's flag surviving TJ-01, while the role chip (TJ-02, Ryan-GATED) and last seen (PF-05) stay open. Also note AGENTS.md:23-28 forbids an implementing agent from editing GAPS.md at all, so the edit must be flagged as maintainer work in the same PR. Separately, desktop/GAPS.md:1 is still headed "pinned at b5c0507" and needs re-anchoring to f8557c4 (delta).

Tests: the CLI layout is right - src/commands/__tests__/ exists with invite.test.ts, and endorsements.test.ts is a new file in that same directory. The page-two fixture is real but lives in remove.test.ts:29 (`invitations --paginate --slurp` → `[[], [{id:9, invitee:{login:'member-gh'}}]]`), so "reuse" means exporting or copying it out of a different suite. The batch keeps three of the six invite tests the costing names and drops the ones its own text depends on: no `notPending`, no generic-remote refusal, no gh-missing wording, and no "--cancel does not print the Slack join block" - despite cliChanges asserting exactly that. There is also ZERO test for `--project` (key validation, the composed join block, refusal under --cancel), a whole flag with no coverage. Desktop side: the layout is colocated `*.test.ts(x)` plus src/backend/__tests__/, and share.test.tsx, inbox.test.tsx and mock.test.ts already exist as the natural homes - the batch proposes no app test at all while changing ShareScreen, ShareInvite, the mock, the adapter and the fixture schema.

Size: "S" is wrong. Counting: CLI 7 files (new endorsements.ts, cli.ts, frames.ts, invite.ts, team.ts, 2 tests) + app ~10 (types.ts, Backend.ts, mock/index.ts, tauri/index.ts, ShareScreen.tsx, ShareInvite.tsx, reports.tsx, fixtures/schema.ts + fixture data, GAPS.md, plus desktop tests) ≈ 17-19 files, one new verb, one new seam member, two DTO changes, a fixture identity shrink and a canvas change. One PR is at least possible now (desktop/ is inside the monorepo, PR #54), but this is M, and it is one Codex run only if CP-30 is split out.

Nothing here violates D20 (endorsements and invitations are host truth; the batch introduces no localStorage) and nothing violates the seam rule as long as the endorsements read goes through a Backend method rather than the app shelling gh - which point 5 requires anyway.

### S7p. SKILL.md body and the hand-maintained project fields

- **Rows:** TJ-10, RM-13
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7b, S7c, S7f, S7i
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** S / Ryan
- **Decisions applied (Teddy, 2026-09-08):** A4 (Teddy 2026-09-08): RM-13 is derived, the first paragraph of the SKILL.md body with description as fallback; no new frontmatter field, no schema change.
- **Critic and follow-up notes (binding):** Critic: TJ-10 is a documentation delta on §5.1 (the hand-maintained `description` key survives because `schema.ts:48` is passthrough); say so in the spec rather than adding a schema member.

**CLI change.** `src/lib/schema.ts` - `parseSkillFrontmatter`'s success shape gains `body: string`, the bytes after the closing `---`, split at the existing FRONTMATTER match the function ALREADY computes, so the boundary reaches a consumer as a value rather than as a regex a second reader has to reproduce; `SkillRecord` (src/lib/skills.ts:7-14, today `{id, name, team, directory, frontmatter, grants}`) carries it, and `skillAtSource` (install.ts:156) must set it from the source IT parsed, so a record built from a materialized pinned version never carries the clone's prose. No file gains a field, no parse rule changes, CRLF and a missing trailing newline behave as today, a file with no frontmatter still behaves, and the enumerator does no extra I/O because the file is already read in full. This deliberately does NOT unify the three sibling helpers that each re-implement the same boundary with their own literal (`declaredCategory` skills.ts:121, `injectManagedFields` skills.ts:139, `canonicalSkillMd`) - that unification is a separate change and is not proposed. TJ-10 needs NO schema line: `teamSchema`'s project entry is already `.passthrough()` (schema.ts:48, `{remotes: string[], skills: uuid[]}.passthrough()`) and §5 promises unknown fields survive every write, so a hand-maintained `description` parses, survives (each writer re-serializes the parsed document) and is IGNORED by guard row (c)'s `onlySkillListsChanged` comparison (guard.ts:153, :175-183) as long as it does not change, leaving `publish` unaffected. The `projects` object stays hand-maintained on the host through GitHub's own editor - no verb creates or removes a project key, `publish --project` refuses a key that is not already present, `team create` scaffolds `projects: {}`, and a clone push carrying that change is refused by the pre-push guard. The batch adds only the test that pins the passthrough.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** APPEND RM-13, closed by derivation rather than by a field: SkillDetail's paragraph under the title renders the SKILL.md body's FIRST paragraph (from S7p's own body value), with description as the fallback when the body has no prose. That keeps the drawn paragraph in place with an honest source, needs no declared frontmatter key, and needs no authoring path - which matters because every one of the catalogue's sample skills and every real skill lacks such a key today, so the app would need this fallback regardless. The declared-key route is therefore NOT taken; if Teddy wants a curated sentence instead, it is a COPY CONVENTION for description, not a schema change. The paragraph still needs a line clamp: it now holds an unbounded committed string.

**Sync rule.** `team.json`'s passthrough is what makes TJ-10 safe under sync: `publish`'s row-(c) comparison ignores an unchanged descriptive key, so a hand-added description is neither clobbered nor blocked by the one verb permitted to touch that file.

**Tests.** `schema.test.ts`: `parseSkillFrontmatter` returns the body after the closing `---`, with CRLF and with no trailing newline, and a file with no frontmatter still behaves; a project entry keeps a hand-maintained `description` through `teamSchema.parse` and through a re-serialization (this pins spec:149 for team.json). `install.test.ts`: a record built from a materialized pinned version carries THAT tree's body, not the clone's.

**Rationale and risk.** Last of the CLI batches because it is the one that touches the most already-owned files: `src/lib/schema.ts` (S7b's personSchema, S7c's teamConfigSchema) and `src/commands/install.ts` (S7i). All three edges are serialization only. Placing it last costs nothing, because the body is a rendering input rather than a dependency of any other row. `skillAtSource` is the subtle half: if `body` is set from the clone's working copy rather than the materialized pinned tree, a pinned placement's detail page shows prose the placed copy does not have. The test must cover a pinned version explicitly.

**Verifier corrections (two adversarial lenses at 85cc276 and f8557c4; authoritative over the batch text above).**

- Lens 1 (corrections apply): EVERY CODE CLAIM IN S7p IS TRUE AT 85cc276 AND STILL TRUE AT f8557c4 - the batch is neither DONE nor closed by the delta. But it is MOOT AS WRITTEN in the same way S7d is: it delivers `body` onto an in-process record no consumer can ever reach, and its own costed app half (read the clone off disk) is dead in the shipped shell. Corrections, in priority order.

1. VERIFIED, so nothing here needs re-litigating. `SkillRecord` is `{id,name,team,directory,frontmatter,grants}` at src/lib/skills.ts:7-14, built at :33 from the file already read in full at :29. `parseSkillFrontmatter` computes `FRONTMATTER.exec(source)` and throws the boundary away at the return. The three sibling helpers each re-implement the boundary with their own literal: `declaredCategory` skills.ts:121, `injectManagedFields` skills.ts:139 (which uses a CAPTURING `(\r?\n|$)` and reconstructs via `match[2]`, unlike the exported FRONTMATTER's non-capturing form), `canonicalSkillMd` skills.ts:158. `skillAtSource` returns `{ ...expected, directory: source, frontmatter: parsed.data, grants: parsed.grants }` - the spread would satisfy a widened type and silently carry the clone's body, exactly as the batch's risk says. There are exactly TWO SkillRecord construction sites in non-test src (skills.ts:33, install.ts:163), not three. TJ-10: teamSchema's project entry is `.passthrough()` at schema.ts:48; `publish` re-serializes the parsed document (`JSON.stringify(fresh, null, 2)`, publish.ts:176) and refuses an absent key (publish.ts:56, :171); `team create` scaffolds `projects: {}` (team.ts:523); the other two writers re-serialize the parsed document (team.ts:165, :421); row (c)'s `onlySkillListsChanged` (guard.ts:175-183, dispatched at :153) scrubs global/projects then compares each entry MINUS `skills`, so an unchanged `description` is byte-identical on both sides; and the pre-push refusal is real - `teamChangeOpenToRawPush` admits only those same three predicates (guard.ts:103-105), so a description-only push is refused and the edit must be made in GitHub's web editor. Blob hashes for src/lib/skills.ts, src/lib/guard.ts, src/commands/install.ts and src/commands/publish.ts are IDENTICAL at 85cc276 and f8557c4 - none of the 27 (b5c0507..85cc276) or 18 (85cc276..f8557c4) commits closed, renamed or moved anything S7p relies on.

2. LINE DRIFT IN THE BATCH'S OWN ANCHORS. `skillAtSource` is install.ts:159 (signature) / :163 (the `...expected` return), NOT install.ts:156 - install.ts is byte-identical at all three revs, so :156 is simply wrong (it is the start of the costing's cite RANGE `install.ts:156-160`). Under main the schema.ts anchors moved +3, because a3de58a inserted `appChoiceSchema` and the `app` key at schema.ts:91-94, above them: FRONTMATTER 147→150, `parseSkillFrontmatter` 150→153, its return 158→161. `teamSchema.projects` stays at schema.ts:48 (the insert is below it). The costing's caller list for skillAtSource is also stale: at 85cc276/f8557c4 the callers are install.ts:97, sync.ts:175 and sync.ts:227 (costing said install.ts:94, sync.ts:141, :185) - the pinned-version test must pin one of THOSE paths.

3. MATERIAL - S7p HAS NO DELIVERY MECHANISM, AND RM-01's COSTED APP HALF IS DEAD. The costing's RM-01 App side ("Read `<clone>/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` … readdir + stat the folder for the file list, byte size and line count") and its "the floor is genuinely zero for an in-process caller" are both SUPERSEDED at f8557c4. The shipped shell exposes exactly five commands - `cli_spawn, cli_write, cli_kill, read_app_state, host_platform` (desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:137) - and its capability set carries NO filesystem permission at all (desktop/src-tauri/capabilities/default.json: core/opener/store/clipboard-manager/window-state only; Cargo.toml:22-28 lists no tauri-plugin-fs). `read_app_state` reads one fixed path, `~/.terum/skills/run/app.json` (lib.rs:113-122). The app therefore cannot read the clone, a placed folder, or any SKILL.md; the only wire is a verb's `result.value` over frames. And no verb Result carries the body: `LsSkill` is `{id,name,author,category,installs,latest,endorsement}` (ls.ts:18), `LsResult` is `{local?,roster,skills}` (ls.ts:20), and `skill()` is a hard-coded gap in the real adapter (desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:65). So S7p as scoped ("no verb, no flag") lands a field that is unreachable - the precise failure the delta already ruled MOOT for S7d's exported readers. FIX: S7p must add the payload route. The cheapest one costs ZERO new verbs and keeps the SIMPLE bar intact: `ls` is already in FRAME_VERBS (src/lib/frames.ts:32) and never prompts (its own §6 comment at ls.ts:22; `selectTeam` THROWS rather than asking, config.ts:23-29), so it is legal under `read()`, which cancels the process on any ask (index.ts:46-53). Put `body` (and, since the same tab prints them, the file list, line count and byte size) on `LsSkill` or on a `ls --skill <ref>` result - additive against the adapter's `.passthrough()` cliSearch/cliValidate-style schemas and additive under the protocol rule (docs/frame-protocol.md:61-63). Do NOT add a new verb AND a flag: that would break bar condition (2).

4. CONSEQUENCE - S7p IS NOT A TRAILER. "Placing it last costs nothing, because the body is a rendering input rather than a dependency of any other row" is false under the delta. `Backend.skill()` cannot be un-gapped without this payload, so S7p is a PREREQUISITE of (or should merge into) the skill-detail read batch S7p's own rows feed - re-place it before that batch, not after S7b/S7c/S7i. Its dependsOn edges are otherwise clean: S7b, S7c, S7i - none of them is the retired S7a, so no re-pointing is needed; S7i's file (install.ts) is byte-identical at f8557c4, and S7b/S7c's shared file (schema.ts) has already moved once for `app`, so only the merge order matters.

5. TJ-10 HAS THE SAME UNSTATED DELIVERY GAP. Every persistence claim is verified (item 1), so the CLI half really is "the test that pins the passthrough" - but nothing puts `projects[].description` on the wire: `ls` returns no projects (ls.ts:20) and `catalog()` is a hard-coded gap (desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:68). State this in the batch and place TJ-10's payload with whichever batch makes catalog()/roster() real. The batch also drops the costing's second honesty note (costing:1125): the key is UNDECLARED, so it reaches the app as `unknown` and the app must type-check and escape it before rendering - that belongs in the app half, since a hand-edited team.json is the one place a string arrives with no schema behind it.

6. RM-13 IS COSTED design-SIDE AND THE BATCH SILENTLY SUBSTITUTES A DIFFERENT SOURCE. Its final verdict is SIMPLE · design (costing:209; design list at :1879) and its costed fix is a build.py edit: delete `desc_long`, render `s["desc"]` at build.py:1517, remove the three literals at build.py:848-850, :914-915, :920-921, plus the line clamp - whose stated benefit is that the paragraph becomes byte-identical to the `description:` printed in the frontmatter block below it (costing:1316, :1700). The batch instead renders "the SKILL.md body's FIRST paragraph, with description as the fallback", which (a) discards that guarantee, (b) is a change to pixel-locked boards that the batch does not schedule (design-side printed strings are Teddy's call per BRIEF, not Ryan's), and (c) needs a parser the batch never names: the seam types `skillMd` as `{frontmatter:string; body:SkillMdBlock[]}` with kinds `h2|p|ol|code` (app types.ts:16,19) and the mock feeds it a PRE-PARSED fixture tuple list (`design.SKILL_MD_BODY`, mock/data.ts:15) rendered block-by-block at SkillScreen.tsx:25 - so RM-01's `body: string` requires an app-side markdown→block parser before either the SKILL.md tab or "the first paragraph" exists at all. The same tab prints `s.files[0]`, `s.lines`, `s.size`, which mock/data.ts:27-37 fabricates and which no CLI value carries (see item 3). Either keep RM-13 as costed (design-side, render `desc`) or take the substitution back to Teddy; do not carry a design decision inside an app half approved by Ryan.

7. BAR CHECK: the delta does not break it. No team.json schema line, no new guardTeam branch (guard.ts:150-156 untouched), no owner-file field, no §6.0 row, one approver, tests in the existing vitest layout - and with the payload added to the EXISTING `ls` verb, still zero new verbs. Add one test the batch omits: the frame-mode round trip, i.e. `ls` over `--frames` returns the body on `result.value` and asks nothing (read() cancels on any ask, index.ts:46-53).
- Lens 2 (corrections apply): STATUS PER THE DELTA: S7p is UNAFFECTED in CLI code (nothing in the 18 commits touches schema.ts's parseSkillFrontmatter, skills.ts or skillAtSource; the only schema edit is `app` at src/lib/schema.ts:91-94) but its CONSUMPTION HALF IS CHANGED and its delivery mechanism is now missing. It does not depend on the retired S7a, so no re-pointing of dependsOn is needed on that axis - but see (1).

WHAT I VERIFIED AS CORRECT (do not re-open): SkillRecord is exactly `{id,name,team,directory,frontmatter,grants}` at src/lib/skills.ts:7-14, built at :33 and copied at src/commands/install.ts:163 - and grep for `SkillRecord` across src/ and desktop/src finds NO other construction site (not even in tests), so widening the type really is two sites and the "S / one Codex run / one PR" sizing holds on the CLI side (desktop/ is inside the same repo, so one PR can carry both halves). `source.slice(match[0].length)` is a sound boundary: FRONTMATTER at src/lib/schema.ts:150 is `/^---\s*\r?\n([\s\S]*?)\r?\n---(?:\r?\n|$)/`, so CRLF and a missing trailing newline both fall out for free, and a file with no frontmatter still returns `{ok:false}`. The risk note on skillAtSource is real and correctly identified: it returns `{...expected, directory, frontmatter, grants}` so a widened type silently leaks the clone's body to a pinned record. TJ-10's whole mechanism checks out: teamSchema's project entry is `.passthrough()` (schema.ts:48); all four team.json writers re-serialize the PARSED document (publish.ts:176 written at :108, team.ts:165, team.ts:421, and the scaffold at team.ts:527); `team create` scaffolds `projects: {}`; guard row (c) is at guard.ts:153 and onlySkillListsChanged is at guard.ts:175-183 exactly as cited, and it compares each project minus `skills`, so an UNCHANGED description passes and a CHANGED one is refused - which also means guardRawPush (guard.ts:104, same predicate) refuses a clone push carrying the hand edit, confirming the "GitHub web editor only" claim. No D20 violation: nothing app-local is introduced, and both rows render repo truth. Tests are describable in the existing layouts (src/lib/__tests__/schema.test.ts, src/lib/__tests__/skills.test.ts, src/lib/__tests__/guard.test.ts, src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts all exist).

1. BLOCKING - THE CLI HALF ENDS AT AN IN-PROCESS TYPE THAT NO CONSUMER CAN REACH. Every deliverable in cliChanges (`parseSkillFrontmatter`'s return, `SkillRecord.body`, `skillAtSource`) is an in-process reader. package.json is still bin-only (cli-main/package.json:14-22: no main/exports/types), the app drives the CLI as `node <entry> --frames <verb>` over JSON lines (desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:39-49, desktop/src/backend/tauri/run.ts:47-79), and NO verb result carries a body: LsSkill is `{id,name,author,category,installs,latest,endorsement}` (src/commands/ls.ts:18) and SearchHit likewise. The seam method that would consume it, `skill()`, is a hard-coded GAPS.md failure in the real adapter (desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:65). So as written this batch ships a field with zero consumers. It must additionally name its wire carrier: a per-skill read model on an existing verb's `result.value`, or a new read verb - which then costs an entry in FRAME_VERBS (src/lib/frames.ts:32), a Backend/adapter mapping, and must be answerable with ZERO prompts, because read() cancels the process on any ask (index.ts:46-53). Note the costing's own App-side route for RM-01 ("Read `<clone>/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` … readdir + stat the folder for the file list, byte size and line count", costing:709 block) is now IMPOSSIBLE: the Rust shell exposes exactly five commands - cli_spawn, cli_write, cli_kill, read_app_state, host_platform (desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:36-37,137-140) - none of which reads a file, and AGENTS.md invariant 1 bars fs access outside src/backend anyway. The file list / size_bytes / lines that the SkillMd meta line prints (SkillScreen.tsx:25) therefore have no source either and are not covered by any row in this batch.

2. SEAM SHAPE MISMATCH - `body: string` does not satisfy the DTO, and the frontmatter half is missing. The seam declares `skillMd:{frontmatter:string;body:SkillMdBlock[]}` with `SkillMdBlock = {kind:'h2'|'p'|'ol'|'code';content:string|string[]}` (src/backend/types.ts:16,19) and SkillScreen renders the blocks structurally (SkillScreen.tsx:25: `s.skillMd.body.map(block => block.kind==='ol' ? … )`). The mock does not parse markdown at all - it maps the fixture tuple list `design.SKILL_MD_BODY` (mock/data.ts:15). So somebody must segment the raw string into h2/p/ol/code, and neither cliChanges nor appHalf owns that work. Separately, `skillMd.frontmatter` is the VERBATIM block the board prints (`data-testid="frontmatter"`), but parseSkillFrontmatter returns only the PARSED object (schema.ts:161) - a re-serialized YAML would not be byte-identical to the file. Since the same match is in hand, the batch should return the raw frontmatter text (`match[1]`) alongside `body`; it currently does not, so RM-01's own board still has an unsourced half.

3. RM-13's appHalf is a THIRD option the costing did not offer, and it is a design-side pixel change costed as if it were free. The costing's RM-13 (costing:209, :1316, §6 :1700) offers exactly two resolutions: delete `desc_long` and render `s["desc"]`, or keep the fuller paragraph as a COPY CONVENTION for `description`. "Render the SKILL.md body's first paragraph" is neither. Concretely, the app renders `<RichText text={s.desc_long}/>` at SkillScreen.tsx:41, and `desc_long` is a REQUIRED key of the fixture detail schema (src/fixtures/schema.ts:2, populated in design.json) that SkillDetail inherits through `Omit<Design['DETAIL'],…>` (types.ts:19). Re-sourcing that paragraph changes rendered text on the whole SkillDetail* board family (costing §6 index: SkillDetail, SkillDetailLight, SkillDetailFiles, SkillDetailNotInstalled, SkillDetailDisabled, SkillDetailStates, SkillDetailRailClosed, SkillDetailEvals, SkillDetailQuality, SkillDetailActivity, SkillDetailNoReceipt, SkillDetailPartial - costing:1562-1580), i.e. it breaks the pixel oracle until the boards are regenerated. That is Teddy's design-side call plus a board regen, not a size-S app tweak under approver "Ryan" alone. The appHalf also never says WHICH artifact changes: does `SkillDetail.desc_long` survive and get filled from the body, or does SkillScreen.tsx:41 start reading `skillMd.body`? Say which, or the Codex run has to guess.

4. TJ-10's app half is missing entirely - and the row is "description AND icon". The appHalf covers only RM-13. Unaddressed: (a) the ICON, which the costing's own spec delta resolves as a deterministic derivation from the project key ("A project's icon is not a repository field at all", costing:1124; §6 item 3 at :1737 says replace the hand-written `ico=` values); (b) the undrawn EMPTY STATE - a project card and page with no description - which §6 :1737 item 2 requires for MarketplaceProjects / MarketplaceProject / MarketplaceStates because a fresh `team create` scaffolds none; (c) the costing's explicit app-side obligation that an undeclared key "reaches the app as `unknown` and the app must type-check and escape it before rendering" (costing:1125).
