### S7r. App-only: chrome, the preference store, the adapter rules the mock already carries, and the RM-17 consumer

- **Rows:** AC-01, AC-02, AC-03, AC-05, AC-10, AC-12, AC-13, AC-14, AC-15, AC-16, MC-02, MC-03, MC-08, IB-01, IB-04, IB-09, CP-21, CP-25, CP-41, RM-12, RM-17, RM-33, RM-34, RM-35, RM-06
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7k
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** L / none

**CLI change.** NONE. Every row here is SIMPLE·app; none is part of the 62-row cli/both partition, which is exactly S7a–S7q's rows.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** MC-08 is the spine: ONE app-owned preferences record in the OS application-data directory (Tauri's app config dir), never under `~/.terum`, holding only per-machine chrome and nothing that is team-wide truth - theme (AC-01: exactly System/Light/Dark, System live via `prefers-color-scheme`, a synchronous root-attribute stamp before first paint PLUS a `tauri.conf` background colour and a runtime `setBackgroundColor`, because a transparent body borrows the host's theme; FigmaDark is generator-only, not a theme), overview/rail/startup-page/sidebar-counts/footer-machine-name (AC-02, with `tauri-plugin-window-state` for geometry, and an unreadable store yielding the DRAWN defaults), the bell badge and the seven Inbox kind toggles and a per-machine seen set (AC-03 + MC-02 - and `acted` is DERIVED, never stored, because it asserts a repo action), and the machine label (MC-03: read host name and OS from the platform at launch and persist NOTHING; the drawn copy already says "Terum keeps no list of your machines and nothing here is committed"). AC-05: `<html lang="en">` (there is no `lang=` today, a WCAG 3.1.1 Level A failure), ONE relative-time module over `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` taking an injectable clock, and a rule that no user-facing string is assembled from fragments; a `strings.ts` extraction is explicitly NOT the ask. AC-16: that clock is frozen at 2026-09-06 in fixtures, tests and every ported gate, and relative strings are formatted from ISO instants and never stored. AC-10: publish the six navigation decisions - route inventory (specimen sheets excluded), per-window history stack, focus-scoped keymap including the wizard, versioned route-id vocabulary with a fallback, no deep links in phase 1 (D25), and the CMD-R / D-R4 rule. AC-12: NEGATIVE work - do not port the frame-root `user-select:none`; scope it to chrome. AC-13: port the generator's contrast gate as a test over the APP's own token values (the 80 pairs × 2 themes, growing with every invented state), never a duplicated palette - that input choice is the load-bearing part. AC-14/AC-15: bundle the two variable woff2 faces from npm with local `@font-face` and drop the CDN link, and ship the 53 icon paths as one typed constant (6,911 bytes) with a one-file ISC notice - do NOT adopt the Lucide package, which silently redraws 21 glyphs including chrome visible on nearly every board. IB-01/IB-04/IB-09: the Inbox item set is RECOMPUTED from clone + `config.pending` + `config.placements` + `evals/` on open, on clone file-watch, and after a user-initiated sync - never on a timer and never via `sync --hook`, because a fresh stamp makes hook mode a silent no-op (sync.ts:130) and an app-initiated sync suppresses the real hook for an hour; one app-owned file holds `{seen_at, evidence}` per identity id; the activity feed merges `git log --follow` over `skills/<name>`, `git log -p -- people/*.json` at COMMIT precision (not a scan of the current `installed[]` snapshot) and every receipt under `evals/<id>/`; search, filter and sort are pure in-memory operations over that list. CP-21: an install action on a zero-team machine routes to onboarding carrying the requested skill as the flow's completion intent, and the predicate is the CLI's OWN conjunction (zero configured teams AND a ref naming a remote this machine has not joined, install.ts:67 with :203-206), not a team count. CP-25: both divergence ghost buttons open a confirm naming the exact source folder (`config.shared[<id>].source`) and the exact destination (`~/.terum/skills/quarantine/<timestamp>/<name>`, placer.ts:170-171) and stating that nothing is deleted and only `sync --prune` empties it. CP-41: on any refetch that drops the selected Inbox id, keep the detail pane mounted, swap its body for one centred state that states the observation and never a cause, ONE action ("Back to the list"), and NO "Sync now". RM-12: the ⌘K index is built in the adapter from three already-exported readers - `skillRecords` (skills.ts:20), `readPeople`/`readRoster` (readme.ts:142, skills.ts:69) and `readTeam(clone).projects` (skills.ts:63) - with zero CLI change; the `#/search` route today renders a literal "Search is coming" placeholder. RM-33/RM-34/RM-35/RM-17: a single-flight FIFO mutation queue in the adapter over every verb that takes a lock or reaches `safeWrite` (install, uninstall-skill, uninstall, connect, publish, sync, team create/join/remove/leave, eval --commit, and setup, which chains several); the app's own `Prompter` with `interactive: true`, rendering a GENERIC question dialog upgraded to the drawn bespoke ones by a PREFIX/PATTERN recogniser (only four confirms and one select are constant literals), with a deliberate hard-coded `false` to `auth.ts:43`'s gh offer backed by a test, because `auth.ts:44` inherits stdio into a window with no TTY; progress driven by polling three machine-local artifacts for the lifetime of the sync the user started, never a timer. Also rides here: AC-04's D-W1 half (min 1200×720, work-area clamp, window-state persistence, real scroll containers with sticky headers, portalled overlays under one z-index scale), AC-18's five accessibility clauses (roles and names at authoring time, a written focus order, `role="status" aria-live="polite"` on the first-sync card announcing PHASE TRANSITIONS ONLY, one `prefers-reduced-motion` block written with the first motion, and the `user-select` narrowing at both sites), MC-09's two Show-in-Finder buttons, and EV-02's D27 second branch (Run eval opens a terminal running the command already printed in the dialog, so the app never spawns an agent).

**Sync rule.** The one rule that binds every app row: the app must NEVER sync on a timer, because an app-initiated sync writes `run/<team>.stamp` (sync.ts:348) and thereby suppresses the SessionStart hook's own sync for the next hour (sync.ts:130 + hook.ts:149). Refresh is subscribe-push + revalidate-on-visible + a low-frequency poll of the two cheap local reads + an explicit Sync now, with invalidation keyed by source (config, clone:<team>, placed, stamp:<team>) and NO optimistic cache writes for any CLI-backed mutation, because `install` writes its `pending` entry before placement (install.ts:90-92) and a failed install leaves durable state the next read shows.

**Tests.** `theme.test.ts` (five cases), `prefs` round-trip with an unreadable store yielding drawn defaults, the ported contrast gate over the app's tokens, an Inbox recomputation test proving no timer and no `sync --hook` call, a single-flight queue test proving at most one mutating verb in flight, and a Prompter test proving the gh offer is answered `false`. Gate: `npm run check` in `desktop/`.

**Rationale and risk.** The single app-only batch the rules allow. Most of these rows are already SHIPPED by the M1-M4 loop as drawn - the app renders every affordance on the mock and 87/87 boards are pixel-locked - so this batch is mostly writing down the boundary that already holds (which store owns what, which values are per-machine, which derivations are permitted) plus the four adapter rules that can be built against the mock today and handed to M6. `print` and `progress` frames are produced and DISCARDED today: `driveRun` (desktop/src/backend/drive.ts:7-12) iterates `run.frames` and acts ONLY on `frame.t === 'ask'`, and nothing outside `src/backend/` consumes `.frames`. So RM-17's progress work has no consumer until this batch adds one, and the drawn progress phases are currently decorative. Also: none of this may hand-edit the generated fixture or move a pixel.

### S7s. Machine storage and the quarantine record

- **Rows:** MC-S1, CP-S1
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7k
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** M / Ryan (phase-1 §6 status + §4.2 local-state tree - two sections, which is the MEDIUM)

**CLI change.** A `storage` block on `status`: byte totals for ~/.terum/skills/{teams,cache,quarantine,evals} by recursive stat, plus a quarantine inventory of {when,name,reason,bytes}. CP-S1: moveToQuarantine (src/lib/placer.ts:170) is the SINGLE funnel and its directory name is new Date().toISOString().replace(/[:.]/g,'-') (placer.ts:171), so one sidecar quarantine/<timestamp>/reason.json beside the moved folder records why (drift, a connect --keep-repo resolution, a team leave clone) and by which verb. That sidecar is a new state file in the store tree, not a field in people/<handle>.json or config.json: the one SIMPLE condition both rows break. Two sections move together, §6's status bullet and §4.2's local-state tree block, which does not name evals/ (machine uninstall sweeps run/cache/teams/quarantine, not evals/). Machine-local, never committed, no guard action, no safeWrite. The one drawn reason that cannot occur stays a design edit.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** Settings ▸ Machine's storage rows and the quarantine card render real bytes and a real reason instead of the fixture's '42 KB'/'1.1 MB', and the drawn path shape quarantine/<timestamp>/<name> becomes literally what is on disk. RM-05's quarantine read (S7g appHalf) consumes this inventory instead of reversing the [:.]→- substitution itself - removing the plan's only place where the app re-derives a CLI filename rule.

**Sync rule.** Read-only except the sidecar, written at the moment the folder moves and never after. Nothing is committed, so nothing propagates: another machine's quarantine stays invisible, exactly as MC-04's ruling requires.

**Tests.** status.test.ts: totals for a seeded store; 0 with no error for an absent subtree; one unreadable subtree costs that row only. placer.test.ts: moveToQuarantine writes reason.json beside the moved folder and the folder is byte-identical to the source. sync.test.ts: --prune deletes the sidecar with its folder.

**Rationale and risk.** Same MEDIUM shape, same board, same verb. The app-side route is impossible, not merely forbidden: the documented Tauri fs plugin surface has no size or metadata call, so the drawn byte figures cannot be produced app-side at all. Escape hatch to offer in the PR body: drop evals/ and §4.2 need not move, restoring condition (5) at the cost of two drawn rows. The sidecar must never be treated as authoritative when absent - every pre-existing quarantine folder has none, so the UI shows path and date with no reason rather than inventing one. The scan must not follow symlinks and one unreadable subtree must cost its own row, not the verb.

**Verifier corrections (two adversarial lenses at 85cc276 and f8557c4; authoritative over the batch text above).**

- Lens 1 (corrections apply): S7s is CHANGED, not DONE or MOOT: nothing in the 27-commit delta (b5c0507..85cc276) or the 18-commit delta to f8557c4 touches quarantine writing or adds any measurement, and both owner files it names are byte-identical between b5c0507 and 85cc276 (src/lib/placer.ts IDENTICAL, src/commands/leave.ts IDENTICAL, src/commands/status.ts IDENTICAL; only src/commands/uninstallMachine.ts DIFF, via 1f87b52, +18/-3 for the /terum-skills wrapper rows - the sweep list itself unchanged). Six corrections, the first two load-bearing.

(1) FALSE PREMISE, and it is the sentence the whole CP-S1 half rests on. "moveToQuarantine (src/lib/placer.ts:170) is the SINGLE funnel" is wrong at 85cc276 and was wrong at b5c0507. `teardownTeam` builds its quarantine destination inline and never calls the helper: src/commands/leave.ts:99-105 does `const stamp = new Date().toISOString().replace(/[:.]/g, '-'); const destination = join(store.root, 'quarantine', stamp, `teams-${name}`); await mkdir(dirname(destination), { recursive: true, mode: 0o700 }); await moveDirectory(clone, destination);` - a duplicated copy of the timestamp rule calling moveDirectory (placer.ts:186) directly. It is reached from TWO verbs: `leave` (leave.ts:41) and `uninstall` (uninstallMachine.ts:111). The real map is six write sites through two funnels: install.ts:116, placer.ts:106, placer.ts:121, placer.ts:161 (the quarantineDrift path used by sync.ts:266 and uninstall.ts:131 via remove()), connect.ts:421 - all through moveToQuarantine - plus leave.ts:101, which is not. The costing inherited the same error (costing:199 and :1306 both say "all five call sites funnel through one helper, src/lib/placer.ts:170") while its own body at :1306 simultaneously names leave.ts:101's `teams-<name>` entry as a specimen. Consequences for the batch: its own cliChanges lists "a team leave clone" as one of the three reasons the sidecar must record, and that is precisely the path the funnel does not cover; the fix is either a behaviour-preserving refactor of teardownTeam onto moveToQuarantine (same stamp rule, same 0700 mkdir, same moveDirectory, plus a reason argument) or two write sites; and `tests` must gain leave.test.ts (clone-with-unpushed-work quarantined WITH its reason) and uninstallMachine coverage, since teardownTeam is shared. The stated size M assumed one helper edit; it is now placer + leave + status.

(2) CP-S1's COSTED RECOMMENDATION IS THE DESIGN FIX, AND THIS BATCH SILENTLY OVERTURNS IT. The row's cells are `CP-S1 | MEDIUM | SIMPLE (ok) | design | Ryan` (costing:199) - CLI MEDIUM, design SIMPLE, recommended owner design - and its rationale ends "...hence MEDIUM rather than HARD - and it is not needed: the recommended fix is design-side and costs nothing" (costing:199, :1306). §8.1 counts CP-S1 in the 39 `SIMPLE · design` rows (costing:1879). S7s adopts the rejected MEDIUM CLI route without recording that it reverses a costed call. Either state the reversal explicitly in the batch and put it to Ryan as such, or drop CP-S1 back to design-side and let S7s carry MC-S1 alone - which is a strictly stronger version of the batch's own escape hatch, since it removes the new-state-file breach of SIMPLE condition (1) as well as condition (5).

(3) MC-S1's DESIGN-SIDE HALF IS HARD AND IS ABSENT FROM THE BATCH. The row is `MC-S1 | MEDIUM | HARD (ok) | cli | Ryan` (costing:229) - the HARD is the design cost, and §4's body says why (costing:1683): four always-settled rows (build.py:3654 Sync▸Quarantine, :3710 Evals▸Run trees, :3757/:3758 Advanced▸Pinned checkouts and Eval runs) are drawn as finished strings that "a recursive walk cannot fill instantly - the 312 MB eval tree is the slow one", so the design must either add a per-row pending "-" (the vocabulary already exists at build.py:3762) or move sizes behind the Diagnostics Run button. S7s's appHalf says only that the rows "render real bytes", names no latency treatment, and its approver line names two Ryan-owned prose sections while the HARD change is Teddy's build.py. Under the delta this gets worse, not better: every read is one spawned `node <entry> --frames status` process (desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:39-49) driven by read() (desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:47-53), so the walk blocks the whole Settings read with no partial frame - ProgressFrame exists but is emitted by no verb (src/lib/frames.ts:22).

(4) DELTA - the app half must name its seam method, and it is settings(), not status(). Both are gapped by the shipped adapter (desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:61 status, :62 settings), and the drawn Settings ▸ Machine's storage / quarantine card is served by settings(). Putting a `storage` block on the `status` verb payload is fine on the CLI side but the batch must say which Backend method consumes it or the block lands where nothing renders it. Two things do go the batch's way here: src/commands/status.ts contains no io.confirm/io.text/io.select, so it is legal on read()'s prompt-free path (index.ts:46-53 cancels on any ask), and `status` is already in FRAME_VERBS (src/lib/frames.ts:32), so adding a result field is additive with no protocol bump (docs/frame-protocol.md:61-63).

(5) The rationale's impossibility claim is stale and understates itself. "the documented Tauri fs plugin surface has no size or metadata call" - at f8557c4 the shell ships no fs plugin at all: the Rust half is 151 lines exposing exactly five commands, cli_spawn / cli_write / cli_kill / read_app_state / host_platform (desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:36-37, :137-140), plus opener, store, clipboard-manager and window-state. Re-word to "the shell exposes no filesystem command whatsoever", which makes the CLI route the only route rather than merely the preferred one.

(6) Scope of the storage set. The costed mechanism names three paths the CLI already constructs, all verified: cache/ at src/lib/version.ts:36, evals/ at src/commands/eval.ts:111 (costing cites :103 @ b5c0507 - line drift only, the b5c0507 blob has the identical statement at :103), quarantine/ at src/commands/sync.ts:500 (costing cites :361 @ b5c0507 - same drift). S7s adds a fourth, teams/, which is the largest tree on disk (git clones) and is not a drawn Settings row; meanwhile store.root also holds backups/ (uninstallMachine.ts:44) and run/, which neither the batch's set nor §4.2's sweep covers. Drop teams/ or say why it is measured.

Two smaller things to fold in, both new:
(a) Sidecar naming collision. moveToQuarantine's directory is millisecond-stamped and `mkdir(..., {recursive:true})` never fails on an existing dir (placer.ts:171-173), and sync.ts:266 quarantines inside a loop over placements, so two moves in the same millisecond share one `<timestamp>/` directory. A single `reason.json` "beside the moved folder" would then be overwritten by the second mover, attributing the wrong reason to both. Make it per-folder - `<name>.reason.json` - not one per timestamp directory.
(b) The prune test claim holds, with a Windows caveat that now matters. prune reads entries immediately under the quarantine root and does `rm(path, {recursive:true, force:false})` (sync.ts:498-511), so a sidecar inside `<timestamp>/` is deleted with its folder - the batch's sync.test.ts assertion is sound. But the guard on that list is `paths.filter((path) => path.startsWith(`${root}/`))` (sync.ts:504), a POSIX separator against a `resolve()`d path, so on Windows every entry is filtered out and prune reports "Quarantine is empty." That is RM-21-adjacent, but Windows-on-ARM is now a shipped app target (src/lib/platform.ts:29, .github/workflows/release.yml:373) and the new quarantine inventory would reuse the same readdir/resolve shape, so the batch should either fix or explicitly not-inherit it.

Verified and correct as written, for the record: the §4.2 justification (machine uninstall sweeps exactly ['run','cache','teams','quarantine'] at uninstallMachine.ts:135, and evals/ is in neither that list nor the kept list at :61-63, so it silently keeps store.root non-empty at :141); moveToQuarantine's directory name rule at placer.ts:171; "no guard action, no safeWrite" (quarantine lives under store.root, machine-local, never committed, and nothing in guard.ts:43-55 addresses it); the risk that an absent sidecar must never be treated as authoritative (there is no sidecar of any kind today and prune's inventory is bare names); and dependsOn S7k, which the delta strengthens rather than breaks. No dependency on the retired library-entry batch S7a exists in S7s, so no re-pointing is needed.
- Lens 2 (corrections apply): DELTA STATUS: CHANGED (not "UNAFFECTED"). Nothing measurement- or reason-related landed in the 18 commits, but two things moved under the batch. (a) The shipped Tauri shell registers NO fs plugin - Cargo.toml lists only tauri-plugin-{log,opener,store,clipboard-manager,window-state} and capabilities/default.json grants only core/opener/store/clipboard-manager/window-state permissions - so the costing's preferred app-side route for RM-05 is now foreclosed. (b) §4.2's local-state tree is now stale in a second way: `terum-skills app` writes `<config root>/run/app.json` (src/commands/app.ts:57-58) which the tree does not name.

1. THE RATIONALE'S "IMPOSSIBLE" REASON IS FACTUALLY WRONG (fix the reason, keep the conclusion). "the documented Tauri fs plugin surface has no size or metadata call" is false - tauri-plugin-fs v2 exposes stat/lstat/size. The true reason is stronger: the plugin is not a dependency and no fs permission is granted (desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml:17-27; desktop/src-tauri/capabilities/default.json permissions list), and only five commands exist (cli_spawn, cli_write, cli_kill, read_app_state, host_platform, lib.rs:36-37,137-140). Ship the real reason in the PR body; the current one is refutable in one search and would lose the argument with Ryan.

2. moveToQuarantine IS NOT THE SINGLE FUNNEL. src/commands/leave.ts:100-104 builds its own directory name and calls moveDirectory directly: `const stamp = new Date().toISOString().replace(/[:.]/g,'-'); const destination = join(store.root,'quarantine',stamp,`teams-${name}`); await mkdir(dirname(destination),...); await moveDirectory(clone, destination);`. That is precisely one of the three reasons the batch names ("a team leave clone"), so the batch's own scope contradicts its own funnel claim. The costing repeats the same error ("all five call sites funnel through one helper", costing:1306). Correct site list: placer.ts:106 (discardDisplaced), :121 (restoreDisplaced), :161 (quarantineDrift), install.ts:116 (--force), connect.ts:421 (replaceDirectory) - plus leave.ts:100-104 off-funnel. Threading a reason also changes the signatures of remove() (placer.ts:134) and quarantineDrift() (placer.ts:157) and place()'s options (placer.ts:51), rippling into sync.ts:266,270,279 and uninstall.ts:131. That is 8 CLI files, not "one helper".

3. THE SIDECAR IS PLACED AT THE WRONG GRANULARITY. moveToQuarantine does `mkdir(directory,{recursive:true})` (placer.ts:172), so one quarantine/<timestamp>/ directory can hold more than one moved folder - the costing flags exactly this as RM-05's "UNIT MISMATCH" (costing:1695). One `quarantine/<timestamp>/reason.json` therefore cannot record a distinct reason per folder. Use `<name>.reason.json` beside the folder (still inside the timestamp dir, so `--prune` still deletes it with the dir, sync.ts:499-513 readdirs one level and rm -r's each entry), and keep the "byte-identical to the source" test intact by never writing inside the moved folder.

4. THE APP HALF CANNOT LAND: settings() IS ALL-OR-NOTHING. `settings(): Promise<Result<Settings>>` (desktop/src/backend/Backend.ts:5) where Settings is a 24-key Pick (desktop/src/backend/types.ts:59). Every drawn storage/quarantine row reads that one DTO (SettingsContent.tsx:51,54,64,70; SettingsDialogs.tsx:21), and the adapter gaps settings() wholesale (tauri/index.ts:62). So "renders real bytes" is unreachable until all 24 keys have a source - a `storage` block on `status` changes no pixel on its own. Either dependsOn must name the batch that composes settings() (S7k is status, not settings), or S7s must own the settings()-composition seam decision. As written the dependsOn is wrong.

5. THE STORAGE DTO HAS NO `teams` KEY. STORAGE is `{cache:string, cache_n:number, evals:string, evals_n:number, quarantine:string}` (desktop/src/fixtures/schema.ts:73; values "1.1 MB"/2/"312 MB"/14/"60 KB"). Measuring ~/.terum/skills/teams is work with no drawn consumer - drop it, or say which board grows. Note also the DTO values are DISPLAY STRINGS: if the CLI returns bytes, the batch must name who formats (the adapter), which the appHalf does not.

6. THE `when` FORMAT IS A SECOND DESIGN EDIT, ON PIXEL-LOCKED BOARDS. The shipped fixture is `["2026-08-28T09:14:30Z","csv-profiler","a local edit moved aside at sync","42 KB"]` (desktop/src/fixtures/design.json QUARANTINE) - colons, no milliseconds - but the on-disk name is `toISOString().replace(/[:.]/g,'-')` (placer.ts:171), i.e. `2026-08-28T09-14-30-123Z`. The drawn strings `quarantine/{when}/{name}` (SettingsDialogs.tsx:21) and `${why} · ${when}` (SettingsContent.tsx:51) therefore CHANGE when the real value arrives. The batch claims only "the one drawn reason that cannot occur stays a design edit"; the timestamp form is a second design edit, it hits three pixel-locked boards (SettingsMachine, SettingsPrune, SettingsSync), and desktop/src/screens/settings/settings.test.tsx:36 asserts `quarantine/${when}/${name}` and will fail. Add both to the batch or the PR is not one run.

7. THE ESCAPE HATCH DOES NOT WORK. "drop evals/ and §4.2 need not move" is false: §4.2's tree names the quarantine layout explicitly - `quarantine/<ISO8601>/<name>/  folders moved aside by orphan handling, --force, and local-changed` (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:113) - so adding any sidecar file under it moves §4.2 regardless of evals/. There is no SIMPLE-condition-(5) path back; say so instead of offering Ryan an escape that isn't one. (§4.2 also does not name leave's `teams-<name>` cause, and now omits run/app.json - both cheap to fix in the same edit.)

8. THE COSTED ROUTES ARE BEING OVERRIDDEN WITHOUT SAYING SO. CP-S1 is costed SIMPLE · design (costing:199 and the SIMPLE·design roster at :1879), with the explicit note "it is not needed: the recommended fix is design-side and costs nothing" (costing:1306). RM-05 is costed SIMPLE · app, owner "none" (costing:157), with "putting a recursive `du` inside a verb to satisfy a Settings card is worse than the app measuring its own disk" (costing:1258). This batch re-routes BOTH to the CLI. The re-route is defensible now (point 1(a) killed the app route), but the batch must state that it overrides two costed recommendations, or Ryan will read it as MC-S1 alone. MC-S1's own verdict (MEDIUM · cli, costing:229,1881) is quoted correctly.

9. RM-05 IS CROSS-REFERENCED TO THE WRONG BATCH. The appHalf says "RM-05's quarantine read (S7g appHalf)", but S7g carries RM-39/RM-40/RM-44 (ls --local health/tracked/ledger). RM-05's boards are SettingsMachine, SettingsPrune, SettingsSync, SettingsStates (costing:157,1546-1550) - the same boards as S7s. RM-05 belongs IN S7s, not consumed from S7g.

10. THE UNCONDITIONAL SCAN REGRESSES `status` FOR EVERYONE. A recursive stat of evals/ (312 MB / 14 runs in the drawn state) on every `status` run slows the terminal verb and the app's status read alike; MC-S1's own note recommends a per-row "-" or a Diagnostics button precisely because "the 312 MB eval tree is the slow one" (costing:1683). Gate it (`status --storage`, or a separate read) - no FRAME_VERBS change is needed either way since `status` is already listed (src/lib/frames.ts:32), and adding a flag is additive against protocol 1 (docs/frame-protocol.md:61-63).

11. TESTS: layouts are right, coverage is short. status.test.ts, placer.test.ts and sync.test.ts all exist under src/commands/__tests__ and src/lib/__tests__, so all three named tests are describable. Missing: (a) leave.test.ts for the off-funnel site in point 2; (b) placer-remove.test.ts, install.test.ts and connect.test.ts if the reason threading changes those signatures; (c) any desktop test - the app half changes rendering and desktop tests are colocated `*.test.tsx` (desktop/src/screens/settings/settings.test.tsx), which must be updated per point 6; (d) a test that two folders quarantined into one timestamp directory each keep their own reason (point 3).

WHAT IS CORRECT AND SHOULD NOT BE RE-LITIGATED: D20 is not violated - quarantine and storage are machine-local recovery data (spec §4.2:103, "never committed"), drawn under "This machine"/"Local state", and the syncRule's "another machine's quarantine stays invisible" is right. The seam rule is not violated: the work is CLI-side and the adapter stays the only fs-touching module. "moveToQuarantine's directory name is toISOString().replace(/[:.]/g,'-')" is exact (placer.ts:171). "machine uninstall sweeps run/cache/teams/quarantine, not evals/" is exact (uninstallMachine.ts:135). The quarantine inventory shape {when,name,reason,bytes} matches the drawn tuple [when,name,why,size] exactly (settings-data.ts:13). "The one drawn reason that cannot occur" is verified: reconcileOrphans adopts or declines and never moves a folder (sync.ts:382-407), so the fixture's "orphan: no longer in your people file" cannot arise - though note spec:371 asserts orphans ARE moved to quarantine, so this is arguably CLI/spec drift to raise with Ryan, not purely a design edit. "--prune deletes the sidecar with its folder" needs no code (sync.ts:500-511 rm -r's each timestamp entry). SIZE: still one PR, but "M" is optimistic - ~8 CLI files plus a design/fixture edit plus a board re-baseline; call it M/L and say the reason threading, not the measurement, is the cost.

### S7t. Favorites and following: the two people-file social fields

- **Rows:** PF-01, PF-02
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7b
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** M / Ryan for the §5.2 fields; Teddy for the two rulings (see gated)
- **Status:** GATED: PF-02 needs Teddy's written yes (A5, 2026-09-08 = hold); open as a draft PR that says so in its first line
- **Decisions applied (Teddy, 2026-09-08):** A5: PF-02 stays GATED; the Follow tooltip's Inbox promise is held until IB-01 exists.
- **Critic and follow-up notes (binding):** Project heart (decision taken 2026-09-08 under Teddy's "finish it" directive): `favorites` stays `skillId[]` (`schema.ts:21` is `z.uuid()`), so the project heart is a DERIVED chip (any favourite skill in the project), no widened field; the `favorite-project:` preference is deleted. PF-02 stays gated on Teddy's written yes (draft PR).

**CLI change.** src/lib/schema.ts - personSchema (:54-64, .passthrough()) gains favorites: z.array(skillIdSchema).default([]) and following: z.array(handleSchema).default([]). NO new guard code: both ride S7b's 'profile' action through row (b) at src/lib/guard.ts:49, which authorizes the PATH and never the field set - the second payoff of the one action string S7b pays for. Two flag pairs on the existing profile verb (--favorite/--unfavorite <ref>, --follow/--unfollow <handle>), each idempotent (an entry already present produces no tree change and therefore no commit) and each validating its operand against the clone read from the PRE-IMAGE tree inside the mutation. Counts are DERIVED, never stored: a group-by over people/*.json, the same shape as installCounts (src/lib/readme.ts:40). Neither field is read by any placement or authorization path.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** Deletes three live D20 violations in the same PR: favorite:<name> (SkillCard.tsx:16), favorite-project:<key> (MarketplaceScreen.tsx:32) and following:<handle> (market-components.tsx:26-29). The follower chips and the Follow control become team truth. One string must NOT ship until IB-01 exists: 'their new skills land in your Inbox as they publish' promises an Inbox source the CLI does not have - until then the control says only what it does.

**Sync rule.** Both ride personSchema's passthrough exactly as role and projects do: sync re-serializes the whole parsed object and appends only to installed/declined, so neither field is clobbered and neither needs sync code. Rule R1 in the sync addendum binds both writers.

**Tests.** profile.test.ts: favorite/unfavorite and follow/unfollow are idempotent, write only the actor's own file, refuse an unknown skill id and an unknown handle, and leave installed/declined/role/projects byte-identical. readme.test.ts: favorite counts group by id across all people files and never count one person twice.

**Rationale and risk.** Teddy's 2026-09-08 call moves PF-02 out of the design walk, and PF-01 was already the closest MEDIUM to S7b. Same owner file, same guard action, same approver, same D20 retirement - one review instead of two. Separated from S7b only so S7b is never blocked by a ruling neither field can proceed without. Shipping either field without deleting its localStorage key leaves two sources of truth for one control, which is the exact failure D20 names. Shipping PF-02 before IB-01 makes the Follow tooltip a promise nothing keeps.

### S7u. Receipt additions and eval run-tree pruning

- **Rows:** EV-03, CP-23, MC-09
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7m
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** M / Ajay (eval-engine §5.3 for the two receipt fields; §4.2 + §6.0 for prune)

**CLI change.** EV-03: a per_case block on the receipt. Verified additive this session - receiptSchema (src/lib/evals/receipt.ts:50) is .passthrough() at the top (:86) and on provenance (:74-85), carries comparisons/arm_scores/efficiency and has NO per-case key, while aggregate() already holds every row with its case field. One more grouping over data in hand: no new I/O, no prompt, no guard change. CP-23: the same receipt gains the INCUMBENT's identity - the 40-char tree the incumbent arm was materialized from - because the receipt names only the candidate's version (receipt.ts:54) and nothing anywhere identifies the incumbent, which is exactly why the drawn caption cannot name it. It must be null on the origin/main^ fallback, which materializes a tree with no receipt. MC-09: a root-level prune-evals verb, NOT a flag on eval <skill> (whose operand is required and whose run refreshes the clone and fails on an unknown ref, so a per-skill flag can never reach the trees the drawn row is about). Retention policy stays out of scope.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** The per-case tables on SkillDetailEvals / SkillDetailEvalsReport / InboxEval stop being fixture-only, so capabilities.perCaseEvalTables finally has something behind it. The Inbox update report's 'vs installed' becomes 'vs incumbent' backed by a real identity, so the caption names the tree without asserting it is the copy on your machine - which is what CP-23 was drawn to claim. Settings ▸ Evals gains MC-09's two Show-in-Finder buttons beside the prune action.

**Sync rule.** Receipts stay append-only under guard row g (RECEIPT_PATH, src/lib/guard.ts:41), so a new receipt FIELD propagates with its file and nothing merges. Prune is machine-local and never touches a committed receipt.

**Tests.** receipt.test.ts: a receipt with per_case and with the incumbent tree round-trips, and one WITHOUT either still parses (forward-compat is why it is passthrough). eval.test.ts: per-case rows equal the aggregate's own grouping with no recomputation; the incumbent tree is null on the origin/main^ fallback. prune.test.ts: refuses a path outside the evals root, deletes only after a confirm, touches no clone.

**Rationale and risk.** One approver, one message, one file region (src/lib/evals/). App-side is FORBIDDEN for EV-03 and CP-23 under D20 - the app would re-aggregate per-case numbers from run.jsonl and invent an incumbent - so there is no legal alternative route: build them or the boards stay fixture-backed forever. MC-09 breaks two conditions (a §4.2 decision line and a §6.0 signature inside the 'evals never mutate anything but their own surfaces' invariant); EV-03 and CP-23 break one each (the owner is the receipt). The prune verb must not reach anything but ~/.terum/skills/evals - a path escape here deletes a team clone.

### S7v. The one Ajay §12 paragraph: what a display surface may do

- **Rows:** EV-01, EV-02, EV-09, EV-16, EV-19
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7n
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** S / Ajay - one message, one paragraph, five rows

**CLI change.** ZERO CODE in all five. One §12 paragraph, written as an ENUMERATION of what is permitted rather than a relaxation of 'The receipt JSON is the API. The UI never runs evals and never derives new statistics' - a broad relaxation licenses the app to invent statistics generally, which is the thing that sentence exists to prevent. Permit exactly: (a) EV-01/EV-16 - a surface may render a receipt's own comparisons[*].net_lift at card altitude and may list more than one receipt per version, provided each receipt's provenance sits beside its own numbers and no two are merged into one figure; (b) EV-02 - a viewer may TRIGGER a run through a terminal hand-off, never an in-app agent spawn (D27's second branch); (c) EV-09 - a per-machine forecast from ONE receipt's own per-arm means, suppressed when the configured arm model differs from receipt.provenance.model (receipt.ts:74-85); (d) EV-19 - a coverage/verdict tally over the latest receipt at each skill's current tree with a provenance stamp. EV-16 additionally needs §5.4 and §12 amended TOGETHER, since both carry the identical latest-receipt-per-version sentence.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** EV-19's app half flips FORBIDDEN → SIMPLE the moment the paragraph lands, and is one walk of the clone: skills/*/SKILL.md for the denominator, git rev-parse HEAD:skills/<name> per key, then the newest schema-valid receipt under evals/<id>/<tree>/. EV-01 moves the lift figure onto the card. EV-09 renders the forecast labelled as pricing arm runs only, and renders nothing on the no-receipt state. EV-16's rail lists a version's committed runs and opens one at a time, never two side by side in one number.

**Sync rule.** None - every input is a committed receipt read at the tree the clone currently holds.

**Tests.** No CLI tests. App-side: a fixture invariant asserting every rendered figure is a receipt field printed at the receipt's own precision (toFixed(3) for sign_p), and a test proving a receipt from a different (provenance.model, cc_version) pair is EXCLUDED rather than compared.

**Rationale and risk.** The highest-leverage sentence in M7: one paragraph unblocks five rows, four of them MEDIUM, and turns the app's whole evals surface from fixture-backed to receipt-backed. It costs no code, which is why it should be asked for first even though it is reviewed late. Teddy's call moves EV-01 here from the design walk, so the lift figure stays on the card as drawn. Written as a relaxation instead of an enumeration it becomes a licence to compute; written without the §5.4 twin it makes §12 contradict §5.4. Both failure modes live in the wording, not the build.

### S7w. App-only seam mechanics: change notification and driving setup

- **Rows:** RM-23, RM-24
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7r
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** L / none

**CLI change.** NONE, and for RM-24 that is a RULE rather than an omission: §6.1 makes setup a sequencer that cannot skip, pre-answer or batch a delegated verb's consent, with a §12 test proving it, so an option to suppress a prompt must not be asked for. setup already records only reached steps (src/commands/setup.ts:47-48, steps: Partial<Record<Step, StepOutcome>>) and returns them inside the FAILURE value (:71-72), so an interrupted run already hands its caller its stopping point. RM-23's CLI half is likewise closed: stampPath is exported and ConfigStore.read returns the parsed config with its pending array.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** RM-23 is a new adapter mechanism: Backend.subscribe plus invalidation keyed by source (config, clone:<team>, placed, stamp:<team>), revalidate-on-visible, a low-frequency poll of the two cheap local reads, an explicit Sync now, a ≥250 ms debounce ignoring .lock directories and config.json.<pid>.<uuid>.tmp files, and NO optimistic cache writes. Two forcing facts: config.ts writes by atomic rename so the watched file is REPLACED and a path watch alone misses every change; and a clean no-op sync's only trace is the stamp write. RM-24 is four pieces: the Prompter rendering the drawn wizard dialog, a hand-written 9→6 mapping from SetupResult.steps onto the drawn step map, print-only steps as screen copy, and a hard-coded false to the gh offer.

**Sync rule.** Poll the two cheap local reads only; never sync on a timer. The standing rule in the sync addendum binds this batch above all others.

**Tests.** A subscribe test proving a config rename fires exactly one invalidation; a no-op sync fires the stamp invalidation and nothing else; a setup-drive test proving every printed step maps to a drawn step and that the gh offer is answered false.

**Rationale and risk.** Both are app-only MEDIUMs whose CLI halves are CLOSED, so they belong to the adapter and must never become asks. They are a batch rather than a prose disposition because RM-23's subscribe mechanism is what every other app row's freshness depends on and has no other owner. RM-23 is the one place a timer can creep in. An app-initiated sync writes run/<team>.stamp and suppresses the real SessionStart hook for an hour (stampIsFresh, src/commands/sync.ts:135), so a polling refresh that syncs is a product bug, not a performance choice.

### S7x. The deferred app halves: sixteen reflow rules and the axe gate

- **Rows:** AC-04, AC-18
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7r
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** L / none; Teddy owns whether the second baseline is worth authoring
- **Critic and follow-up notes (binding):** Window floor (design call, routed to the design walk): the shipped shell is minWidth 960 / minHeight 600 (`tauri.conf.json:18-19`), none of the three options the costing priced; raising to 1200x720 or drawing the icon-rail sidebar and single-pane Inbox is Teddy's call and not this batch's.

**CLI change.** NONE - both rows are NA on the CLI side.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** The remainder after S7r's appHalf, which already carries AC-04's D-W1 half (min 1200×720, work-area clamp, window-state persistence, real scroll containers with sticky headers, portalled overlays under one z-index scale) and AC-18's five clauses. What is left here: the sixteen reflow rules with their sixteen measured binding widths, sixteen Playwright assertions at sixteen widths - the fidelity gate is pinned at 1440×900, so a rule without its own assertion at its own width exists only in prose - and a SECOND app-authored baseline for the three boards no design PNG can validate. AC-18's remainder is the @axe-core/playwright gate over every route in both themes, which is a CI JOB and is exactly why the row is MEDIUM.

**Sync rule.** None.

**Tests.** The sixteen width assertions, and the axe gate as its own CI job - not folded into npm run check, which must stay the portable gate.

**Rationale and risk.** Kept as a batch so the deferred half has an owner and a size instead of a prose line that quietly never happens. Split from S7r because every S7r row is buildable today against the mock, while these two are gated on measurements and a CI job that do not exist yet. A second app-authored baseline is a second oracle. If it drifts from .shots nobody can say which is right, so it must be scoped to the three boards the design PNGs cannot cover and to nothing else.

### S7y. Case-insensitive project-key resolution

- **Rows:** RM-15
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7h, S7j
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** S / Ryan (phase-1 §5.1, one paragraph: keys match case-insensitively and resolve to the declared spelling)
- **Decisions applied (Teddy, 2026-09-08):** A2 (Teddy 2026-09-08): proceed, but confirm against a real team.json that project keys are capitalised before the PR is sent; if the live keys are lowercase, S7y is moot.

**CLI change.** ONE helper resolving a supplied project key against team.json.projects - a z.record(z.string(), …) at src/lib/schema.ts:48, so the key is any string, including Terum - replacing the five Object.hasOwn sites verified this session: src/commands/install.ts:57, publish.ts:56, uninstall.ts:40, search.ts:41 and ls.ts:76. The resolver returns the CANONICAL key, so every message, every people-file scope and every placement keeps team.json's own spelling and never the user's, and it REFUSES by name when two declared keys case-fold to one - the new refusal case the row honestly costs. --project on publish and search resolves the same way. Nothing writes team.json, no guard action changes, and the resolver must also apply on READ of a pre-existing ledger written with a user's lowercase spelling rather than rewriting that ledger.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** MarketplaceProject, MarketplaceProjects, Settings ▸ Teams and the sidebar render PROJECTS[].key verbatim - the key IS the project's only name (schema.ts:48) - so the drawn labels Terum / SSM / MRF stay, the invented name field comes out of the sample data, and the drawn command boxes keep their lowercase operand.

**Sync rule.** Placement scopes already store the canonical key, so config.placements and people/*.json keep their shape and sync's sameScope comparison is untouched. Resolve on read; never rewrite a ledger to migrate it.

**Tests.** install/publish/uninstall/search/ls tests: `terum` resolves the Terum key and every printed line and recorded scope carries Terum; an unknown key still refuses with the existing message; a team declaring both Terum and terum refuses by name and writes nothing; a ledger entry recorded as terum still resolves after the change.

**Rationale and risk.** Teddy's decision (a): the boards keep Terum / SSM / MRF as labels, so the CLI resolves the case rather than the design lowercasing three sidebar rows and a Settings caption. It clears every SIMPLE condition; the honest cost is that it changes identifier semantics across five user-facing verbs, which is why it is one batch with one owner rather than five edits scattered into five other batches. Both dependsOn edges are serialization only (S7h is ls.ts's last owner, S7j is sync's). The collision refusal must fire at RESOLUTION, not at write time, or a team declaring both Terum and terum gets a half-applied install.

### S7aa. MC-01: --cwd on three verbs, isolated as a deliberate MEDIUM

- **Rows:** MC-01
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7c, S7j
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** S / Ryan - and it must be presented AS a MEDIUM, not inside a SIMPLE bundle
- **Critic and follow-up notes (binding):** Critic: MC-01's costed route recommends no field and no flag; the app sets the child process cwd, which `lib.rs` already does. At spec time, verify `cli_spawn` sets cwd from the app; if it does, return status skipped with reason MOOT instead of building `--cwd`.

**CLI change.** --cwd <path> on install (src/cli.ts:125), uninstall-skill (:126) and sync (:128). COST CORRECTION verified this session: the args already exist and are already threaded - InstallArgs.cwd, UninstallArgs.cwd, SyncArgs.cwd - and args.cwd is already consumed inside sync's placement loop, where matchingProjectRoot(clone, entry.scope.project, runner, args.cwd) is called at src/commands/sync.ts:206 and the project placement is skipped at :209 when no matching root is found. Only the three commander registrations and the pass-through are missing. NO project→checkout-path map is added: a recorded path is a second answer to a question the worktree already answers, and it goes stale the moment a checkout moves.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** One --cwd <root> call per project root the app knows about, merged with the global-root call under the CLI's own precedence rule, plus the caption stating the nearest-repository narrowing. The root set is derivable from config.placements keys, which are absolute placed paths carrying scope {kind:'project', project}.

**Sync rule.** --cwd changes only which worktree matchingProjectRoot resolves (called at sync.ts:206). The project-placement rule at sync.ts:209 is unchanged, so a project placement outside its checkout stays SKIPPED rather than quarantined.

**Tests.** install/sync/uninstall tests: --cwd resolves the project from the named checkout, not the process cwd; an absent path fails with the path named; --cwd outside any git repository behaves exactly as the same run from that directory would.

**Rationale and risk.** Isolated out of S7c on the judges' finding: this is the one row whose CLI half knowingly breaks the bar's flag-count condition (three flags on three verbs), and it must not ride inside a batch presented as SIMPLE. Alone, the bar-break is the only thing litigated and the cost correction above is the strongest available framing. There is no alternative: spec:343 bans the LIBRARY from calling process.cwd() and one app process cannot chdir per team or per project. Trimming to sync alone removes project-scoped install and uninstall from the app entirely.

### S7ab. TJ-02: permission roles in the team model (Ryan-GATED)

- **Rows:** TJ-02
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7h
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** M / Ryan - the only batch in M7 that asks him to reopen a table his spec closes
- **Decisions applied (Teddy, 2026-09-08):** A6 (Teddy 2026-09-08): interim accepted: a read-only role chip from ls --host with an explicit unknown state and no chevron if Ryan declines the fourth guard arm; the discipline label (PF-03) and the permission role never share a word in the UI.

**CLI change.** src/lib/schema.ts - teamSchema (:43-51) gains roles: z.record(handleSchema, z.enum(['admin','member'])).default({}), a TEAM-MODEL role deliberately distinct from S7b's role discipline label and from S7h's host-derived chip. New verb `team role <handle> <admin|member>`. THE COST THE GATE EXISTS FOR: a FOURTH guardTeam arm and a FOURTH row in the §6.0 permitted-writes table. guardTeam (src/lib/guard.ts:150) today admits exactly three shapes - publish + onlySkillListsChanged (:153), team-remove + archivedAppendedOnly (:154), join + archivedRemovedOnly (:155) - and throws on everything else at :156. The new arm must be a role-change action plus a comparison proving ONLY the roles map changed and only for the named handle; the verb refuses a caller who is not already admin in that map, with the host's own admin check as the bootstrap for the first one.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** The Share roster's role chip gains its chevron back and its two checkable items become a real verb. Until this ships the app shows a READ-ONLY host-derived role from S7h (ls --host), with unknown for offline / no-gh / generic-git / empty github - never a remembered or inferred role, and never an editable control backed by nothing. That read-only state is Teddy's stated interim, not a placeholder to be quietly upgraded.

**Sync rule.** roles is committed team.json, so it propagates with the clone's hard reset and needs no sync code. It must NOT be read by any placement decision - sync authorizes on the people file and the ledger (src/commands/sync.ts:212-213), never on a role - or a role edit silently re-places skills.

**Tests.** guard.test.ts: the new arm admits a roles-only change for the named handle and refuses one that also touches global, projects, categories or archived. team.test.ts: a non-admin caller is refused before any write; the last admin cannot demote themselves; a role change is exactly one commit.

**Rationale and risk.** Teddy's decision (b): the drawn Admin/Member chip asserts a team-model role, so either the model gains one or the chip is host truth forever. This makes the ask explicit and bounded instead of leaving the chip half-real behind a localStorage write. Two roles now exist in one product - the self-declared discipline label (S7b, people file) and the permission role (here, team.json, admin-written) - and the Share row draws both. They must never share a word in the UI. Second: a roles map that disagrees with GitHub's collaborator list is a claim the CLI cannot enforce, so the verb must state that the host remains the only enforcer.

### S7z. The `new` verb: a destination for the drawn New skill action

- **Rows:** RM-37
- **Depends on:** S7af, S7p
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** S / Ryan (one §6 paragraph, one new verb)
- **Status:** MOOT: Teddy relabelled the New skill CTA to Connect (A3, 2026-09-08); RM-37 needs no `new` verb. The relabel is a canvas change for the design walk.
- **Decisions applied (Teddy, 2026-09-08):** A3 = relabel. No CLI work.

**CLI change.** ONE new verb, `new <name>`, scaffolding a local skill folder under the placement root. The name is validated by isSkillName (src/lib/schema.ts:19, /^[a-z0-9]+(?:-[a-z0-9]+)*$/ and length ≤ 64) - the same rule connect enforces - and an existing folder is refused. It writes a SKILL.md carrying name and a non-empty description and NOTHING ELSE: it must not write license, metadata.id, metadata.author or metadata.terum-category, because those four are connect's managed fields and a hand-written id is the one value that can collide with a real skill. It takes the placement-root lock (the correction to the coster's draft: ~/.claude/skills is where install and sync write, so an existing-folder race is real), touches no team repository, needs no guard action and no safeWrite, and prints `connect <path>` as its last line.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** The Library's New skill CTA and the two empty-state pairs keep their drawn label and gain a real destination. The board debt is real and must be paid before it ships: no board draws name / description / scope / what-happens-next, and the second step must say honestly that a new folder is LOCAL, fails validate until connected (src/cli.ts:114), and blocks install <name> for a same-named team skill. The zero-new-board alternative is relabelling the CTA to Connect - connect with no path is already a picker (src/cli.ts:122).

**Sync rule.** A new folder is untracked until connect runs: ls --local reports tracked: false (S7g) and sync never touches it, because sync's placement loop iterates config.placements (src/commands/sync.ts:200) and a scaffolded folder has no entry.

**Tests.** new.test.ts: creates a folder whose SKILL.md fails validate only on the managed fields; refuses an invalid name, an existing folder and a name colliding with a placed skill; writes no team-repo path and takes no safeWrite; prints connect <path> in the caller's invocation form.

**Rationale and risk.** Teddy's decision (a): the New skill CTA stays on the canvas, so the verb has to exist. SIMPLE on every condition - no owner-file field, one verb, no guard change, tests in the existing layout, one paragraph, one approver. Placed after S7p because S7p is the last batch to touch the frontmatter parse this verb writes against. The verb must not write the four managed fields, or a hand-authored skill reaches connect already carrying an id connect is supposed to mint. The name-collision consequence (costing row RM-37, costing:1321) is a product fact the board must state; the verb cannot prevent it.

### S7a. Library entry: the exports map (M6's own deliverable, first)

- **Rows:** RM-11
- **Depends on:** none
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** S / Ryan
- **Status:** MOOT: RM-11 exports map superseded by frame mode (80054f4); CP-19 re-specified as a hello-frame/FRAME_VERBS inventory in the batch the writer names

**CLI change.** APPEND: the barrel also exports commands(), a read-only inventory walking buildProgram(...).commands for each command's name, registered arguments, options and hidden flag (CP-19). It is the CLI's own answer to 'which printed strings are real', so the design repo's gate checks board commands against the package instead of against a committed snapshot that rots. No new registration and no verb: buildProgram is already the injectable factory (src/cli.ts:35-36). It must call that factory itself and NEVER import src/index.ts, which runs .parseAsync() at :49. It is the fifth thing the barrel must expose on creation, beside run, Prompter, Result and execute.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** None. S7a is the only batch with no app half - `desktop/**` is untouched. The programmatic Prompter it must expose already exists: `Prompter` is exactly five members (src/lib/prompt.ts:14-20, no `secret()`), every verb is `run(args, io): Promise<Result<T>>`, and `CliVerbs` (src/cli.ts:33) is already a 20-key injectable verb table that `buildProgram(execute, verbs, context)` defaults key by key (src/cli.ts:35-36).

**Sync rule.** None. S7a touches no read or write path.

**Tests.** `src/__tests__/bin.test.ts`: the built `dist/lib-entry.js` imports without parsing argv and exposes `run` for all 20 verbs; a tarball assertion in both workflows. `npm run build && node -e "import('./dist/lib-entry.js')"` must produce no output and no exit code.

**Rationale and risk.** Strictly first: nothing downstream is observable without it. The costing says so twice - RM-09 "delivers nothing until RM-11 lands" because src/lib/execute.ts:26-29 prints only the string, and the StatusResult row says "a Result member is invisible until the seam exists ... this ships as dead code until RM-11 lands". It is also literally inside M6's exit line (investigation:305, "the CLI's `exports` map"). ALREADY DRAFTED: TEAM-HANDOFF.md:55 assigns exactly these four edits to a Mac teammate as one PR ("All four are non-breaking. Do not merge; Ryan reviews"), so M7 ADOPTS that PR as S7a rather than re-doing it, and RM-10/RM-18/RM-30 must NOT ride it (RM-30 changes a verb payload). Two hazards. (1) `declaration: true` under `strict` + `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` raises TS4023/TS2742 wherever an exported function's inferred type names a non-exported type - `type Git = (args: readonly string[]) => Promise<CommandResult>` at src/lib/teamRepo.ts:84 is exactly that shape and S7d exports a NEW teamRepo function, so widen or export it here. (2) Adding `exports` CLOSES deep imports into `dist/` that are open today (`files: ["dist",…]` with no exports map admits every subpath) - a real removal, nothing known consumes them, and it is why the barrel must be COMPLETE on creation.

**Verifier corrections (two adversarial lenses at 85cc276 and f8557c4; authoritative over the batch text above).**

- Lens 1 (corrections apply): VERDICT: S7a is MOOT for RM-11 and must be retired as a batch; only CP-19 survives and must be re-placed. Nothing in it is DONE - the barrel was never built (package.json:14-22 at v0.1.6 still declares only type/bin/files: no main, no exports, no types; tsconfig.build.json has no `declaration`) - but the deliverable no longer has a consumer, and three of its own load-bearing claims are now false at f8557c4.

1. RM-11 is superseded by frame mode, not merely deferred. The costing's own App-side line for RM-11 priced the alternative that shipped: "Skip packaging entirely - the sidecar either vendors the CLI source into the app build, or drives the published binary over a JSON-lines stdio protocol" (costing:807, the "App side:" line of the RM-11 block at 807-817). That is exactly what 80054f4/898a795 built: the shell spawns `Command::new(&node).arg(&entry).arg("--frames").args(&args)` with piped stdio (desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:39-49), and the adapter reads JSON frames off stdout (desktop/src/backend/tauri/run.ts:61-79). Nothing under desktop/src/backend imports the terum-skills package - a grep for an import naming it returns nothing - so a barrel + exports map would ship with zero importers. The batch's rationale ("Strictly first: nothing downstream is observable without it") is false today: verb payloads reach the app as ResultFrame.value (frames.ts:170-175), and RM-09's decline half already shipped on the wire as ResultFrame.declined, derived by `isDecline()` regex over the error string (src/lib/frames.ts:24, 44-47, 171-175) - i.e. the "cruder app-side variant" the costing priced, landed without RM-11.

2. RM-11 was already routed out of the ask, so it should never have been a batch row. costing:1173: "RM-11 *(**ALREADY ASSIGNED - do not include this row in the ask.** `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:55`: a Mac teammate drafts exactly these four edits as one PR … 'All four are non-breaking. Do not merge; Ryan reviews.')*". The batch's own rationale quotes that note and then still carries RM-11 as a row it "ADOPTS". With the consumer gone, the correct disposition is: tell Ryan the drafted PR no longer has a caller and let him decide to merge it as a public-API choice, not as an M7 prerequisite. It is not M7 work and must not gate anything.

3. CP-19 must be re-placed, and it needs NO packaging change - in fact S7a would HURT it. CP-19 is design-side (costing:182 owner `design`; the gate is `scripts/emit-commands-contract.mjs` walking `buildProgram(...).commands` plus a committed `commands.contract.json` beside build.py, costing:1288/1614). tsconfig.build.json emits every `src/**/*.ts` except `__tests__` into dist/ with `files: ["dist"]`, so `dist/cli.js` ships today and, because the package has NO exports map, a deep import of it resolves right now - the gate script can import buildProgram from a local checkout or an installed tarball with zero CLI edits. Adding the exports map is precisely what would CLOSE that path (the batch's own risk (2), and costing:817 "the coster's justification 'nothing is taken away' is FALSE"). Re-place CP-19 into a design-side batch; if a machine-readable inventory is wanted on the wire instead, the hello frame already carries a verb-name list (`FRAME_VERBS`, src/lib/frames.ts:32, emitted at :170) - 20 entries, names only, no arguments/options/hidden flag - and run.ts drops the hello frame entirely (`case 'hello': return;`, run.ts:63), so consuming it is unbuilt work that belongs with S7q's flag reconciliation, not here.

4. Three stale anchors/counts in the batch text (all re-read this session at f8557c4):
   - "src/index.ts, which runs .parseAsync() at :49" - parseAsync is now at src/index.ts:64 and :67 (two call sites, frames and terminal); :49 is `const execute = createExecute({`.
   - "`CliVerbs` (src/cli.ts:33) is already a 20-key injectable verb table … `buildProgram(execute, verbs, context)` (src/cli.ts:35-36)" - CliVerbs is at src/cli.ts:34 and is now 21 keys (`app?: typeof runApp` first, c487048); buildProgram/`active` are at src/cli.ts:36-37. src/commands/ now holds 21 modules (app.ts added), so the tests line "exposes `run` for all 20 verbs" is also off by one.
   - "`Prompter` is exactly five members (src/lib/prompt.ts:14-20, no `secret()`)" - Prompter is now six members at src/lib/prompt.ts:14-26: it gained `readonly channel?: 'terminal' | 'frames'` at :21 (80054f4/a3de58a), which is the only discriminator for frame mode since frameChannel sets `interactive: true`. Any in-process caller story must account for it.

5. Claims that DO hold, for the record: package.json is bin-only (package.json:14-22); tsconfig.json has `strict: true` (:8) and `noUncheckedIndexedAccess: true` (:9) and tsconfig.build.json sets no `declaration`, so the TS4023/TS2742 hazard is real and `type Git = (args: readonly string[]) => Promise<CommandResult>;` is still at src/lib/teamRepo.ts:84; the named test files exist in the stated layouts (src/__tests__/bin.test.ts, which already builds a scratch dist with `--declarationMap false`; src/__tests__/release-plan.test.ts) and both workflows already assert tarball contents (.github/workflows/ci.yml:59-61, .github/workflows/release.yml:160-162). Note the cross-batch premise "S7d exports a NEW teamRepo function" is itself dead: S7d's three "exported reader" rows (RM-10, RM-22, CP-35) are moot as exports for the same reason and must be rebuilt as verb result payloads.

6. appHalf / seam / D20: "None. desktop/** is untouched" is consistent - S7a writes no seam DTO, adds no read model, and shows no app-local state as team-wide, so it violates neither D20 nor the seam rule (AGENTS.md invariant 1). Two notes for whoever inherits the fragments: the live seam is now desktop/src/backend/types.ts in the CLI monorepo (byte-identical to /home/teniroo/Projects/terum-skills-app/src/backend/types.ts - verified by diff this session), and the standalone terum-skills-app repo is superseded by desktop/ per the 2026-09-08 ledger, so any successor batch must target desktop/src/backend/tauri (real) as well as mock/. Also re-anchor desktop/GAPS.md, whose header still reads "drawn affordances the CLI on `main` (pinned at b5c0507) does not have yet" (GAPS.md:1).

7. dependsOn: `[]` is correct for S7a itself, but its declared role as the universal prerequisite is void - every dependsOn edge pointing at S7a must be cut, and any batch that assumed "an exported reader the app imports" must be rewritten to deliver the same data as a verb's `result.value` (the adapter answers eleven read models with a hard-coded gap string today: desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:32, 61-69, 85). Size/one-run-one-PR is unanswerable as posed: after removing RM-11 the residue is one design-repo gate (CP-19), which is not a CLI Codex run at all.
- Lens 2 (corrections apply): S7a is MOOT as written, its "already drafted, adopt the PR" premise is FALSE, its surviving row (CP-19) needs re-SPECIFYING rather than re-placing, and four of its code-reality anchors are stale at f8557c4. Every claim below was read this session.

1) MOOT - RM-11 has no consumer, and the alternative it priced is what shipped.
- package.json at f8557c4 (v0.1.6) still declares only type/bin/files: no main, no exports, no types (cli-main/package.json:2-22). The exports map was never added.
- The shipped seam is a subprocess over frames, not an import: `--frames` is detected and filtered in the bin (cli-main/src/index.ts:42-43), the wire shapes are cli-main/src/lib/frames.ts:12-32, and docs/frame-protocol.md:5 states "The desktop app's `src/backend/tauri/` speaks this protocol".
- Nothing in desktop/ imports the CLI package. A grep of desktop/src and desktop/package.json for `from 'terum-skills`/`require('terum-skills`/`"terum-skills"` returns exactly one hit, and it is a printed string in a UI card (desktop/src/screens/settings/SettingsContent.tsx:57), not an import.
- RM-11's own App-side line in the costing priced precisely what shipped: "App side: MEDIUM (D20 ok) - Skip packaging entirely - the sidecar either vendors the CLI source into the app build, or drives the published binary over a JSON-lines stdio protocol" (costing:807 block, App side line). So the delta's MOOT verdict is correct on RM-11's own terms, not merely by fiat.

2) FALSE PREMISE - "ALREADY DRAFTED … M7 ADOPTS that PR as S7a". There is no PR and no draft.
- `grep -rn 'lib-entry'` across the whole cli-main tree (excluding node_modules) returns NOTHING - no src/lib-entry.ts, no reference in any .ts/.json/.yml/.md.
- `git branch -a` in cli-main lists 30+ remote branches (feat/frame-mode, feat/app-command, feat/desktop-shell, release/0.1.2…0.1.6, codex/ie1…ie5, …) and none is a packaging/lib-entry branch.
- TEAM-HANDOFF.md:55 does exist and says what is quoted, but the file is NOT in the CLI repo or in ~/Projects/SSM/terum-skills - it is /home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-08-overnight/TEAM-HANDOFF.md:55 (copy at /home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/review/2026-09-08-repo-decision/orig/TEAM-HANDOFF.md:55). Cite the full path; "TEAM-HANDOFF.md:55" alone does not resolve.
- That item is an UNSTARTED task, and its own sibling retired it: item 6 on the same list ("Decide the sidecar runtime for M6: bundle Node … or require a global `terum-skills` install") was answered by the shipped `app` verb + state file + spawn design, which is exactly why item 5's in-process premise is gone.

3) SCOPE - the packaging PR is five files, not four (if anyone revives it). The tarball must-have list is duplicated: .github/workflows/ci.yml:59-60 `for (const must of ["package.json","README.md","LICENSE","NOTICE","dist/index.js"])` and .github/workflows/release.yml:160-161, whose list additionally carries "dist/claude/skills/terum-skills/SKILL.md". Fixing only ci.yml leaves the release path able to publish a .d.ts-less tarball. This correction is already recorded at 2026-09-07-desktop-app-teammate-handoff.md:50 and :155 ("It is a five-file edit, not four"). The batch's `tests` field does say "a tarball assertion in both workflows", so only the "four edits" framing in `rationale` is wrong.

4) CP-19 must be RE-SPECIFIED, not just re-placed into another batch. The batch delivers it as a barrel export `commands()`; that dies with the barrel. Two facts settle the replacement:
- The walk needs no exports map and already exists in-repo. src/__tests__/frames-cli.test.ts:53-57 walks `buildProgram(...).commands` including subcommands (`for (const command of program.commands) { names.add(command.name()); for (const sub of command.commands) names.add(...) }`) and asserts FRAME_VERBS names only registered commands; src/__tests__/cli.test.ts:37,72,156 and cli-hints.test.ts:31 do the same for descriptions and options. So the design repo's contract generator can walk a CLI CHECKOUT - which the costing already conceded it needs ("its regeneration needs the CLI checked out", costing:1288) - or the assertion can live in the CLI beside frames-cli.test.ts.
- The hello frame is NOT a substitute. FRAME_VERBS (src/lib/frames.ts:32) is a hand-maintained `as const` literal of 20 strings carrying no registered arguments, no options and no hidden flag, and it deliberately omits `app` and `share` while `app` IS a registered command (src/cli.ts:34, src/commands/app.ts). A gate over hello.verbs would check a hand-written list, not the program - the exact rot CP-19 exists to prevent.

5) CP-19's design-side anchors have drifted and must be re-resolved. build.py is now 4658 lines: `def terminal_hint` is at build.py:763 (costing:1288 says :752), `def cli_box` at :1060 (costing says :752/cli_box unnumbered), and `check_people([...])` is called at build.py:4646, not :4514 - so "insert check_commands(EMITTED) after check_people(...) at build.py:4514 and before the write loop at :4516" no longer resolves.

6) STALE CODE ANCHORS - every one is exact at 85cc276 (cli-clone) and wrong at f8557c4 (cli-main):
- "CliVerbs (src/cli.ts:33) … 20-key" and "buildProgram … (src/cli.ts:35-36)": at cli-main it is src/cli.ts:34 with 21 keys (`app?: typeof runApp` added first, commit c487048) and buildProgram at src/cli.ts:36-37.
- "expose `run` for all 20 verbs": src/commands now holds 21 verb modules (app.ts). A barrel would have to decide whether to export `app`, which is deliberately excluded from the public frame surface (frames.ts:32).
- "`Prompter` is exactly five members (src/lib/prompt.ts:14-20, no `secret()`)": Prompter gained `readonly channel?: 'terminal' | 'frames'`, so it is SIX members at cli-main/src/lib/prompt.ts:14-26. Still no secret().
- "NEVER import src/index.ts, which runs .parseAsync() at :49": at cli-main the parseAsync calls are src/index.ts:64 (frames branch) and :67, with the flag filter at :42-43 and the `sync --hook` refusal at :60-62. The rule holds and is now stronger - importing index.ts would also wire the frame channel to process stdio.

7) RISK (1) IS CONTINGENT ON A ROW THE DELTA RETIRES, and its premise is not demonstrated today. `type Git = (args: readonly string[]) => Promise<CommandResult>;` is at src/lib/teamRepo.ts:84 at BOTH revs - correct. But it appears only in NON-exported positions: a local const at :91 and the parameters of `assertOrigin` :200, `push` :221, `skillTrees` :285, `requireGitResult` :295. Every exported symbol in teamRepo.ts carries an explicit annotation (e.g. `openTeamRepo(...): TeamRepo` at :86), so `declaration: true` cannot raise TS4023/TS2742 on `Git` as the tree stands. It becomes real only if RM-10 exports `skillTrees(git: Git, …)` - and the delta makes RM-10 moot as an export, so the stated coupling evaporates with it. Also: the batch dropped the costing's second named shape, `ConnectStepError`, which is at src/commands/connect.ts:40 - not :39 as the costing cites - at BOTH 85cc276 and f8557c4, and is likewise never in an exported signature.

8) THE SEQUENCING RATIONALE IS NOW FALSE, so no batch may be held behind S7a. "Strictly first: nothing downstream is observable without it" no longer holds: RM-09's decline already reaches the app with no exports map, as `ResultFrame.declined` derived by `isDecline()` (src/lib/frames.ts:24, 44-47, 171-175), and any Result member becomes observable as the result frame's `value` (frames.ts:24). Cut every dependsOn edge on S7a rather than re-pointing it.

9) KNOCK-ON THE DELTA DID NOT NAME. Retiring the barrel invalidates more than S7d's RM-10/RM-22/CP-35: any row whose app-side plan is "the sidecar imports an already-public reader" is now impossible. Costing:1260 (RM-12, the ⌘K index) states it outright - "Zero CLI change; the three readers are public today and RM-11 makes them importable" (skillRecords, readPeople/readRoster, readTeam) - so RM-12 must be re-planned as a verb payload or as an app-side consumer of `search`'s frame result. Whoever re-places CP-19 should sweep the costing for that phrasing.

10) SIMPLE bar: not broken. S7a touches no owner file, no team.json row, no verb, no flag, no guard (confirmed: the only schema edit in the 18 commits is the `app` key, src/lib/schema.ts:91-94). But its one unverified cost stands and nobody has retired it - no `tsc --declaration` has been run in this repo or this session, and the costing itself booked that as "a NAMED BUILD RISK, not a verified non-breakage". If the batch is retired, that risk retires with it; if any part is revived, run `npx tsc -p tsconfig.build.json --declaration --emitDeclarationOnly --outDir <scratch>` first. Note also that src/__tests__/bin.test.ts still exists with the anchors the costing cites (describe at :24, scratch build at :33 building via tsconfig.build.json with `--declarationMap false`), so the test scaffolding claim in `tests` is accurate.

RECOMMENDED DISPOSITION: retire S7a (RM-11 → MOOT, superseded by frame mode at 80054f4/898a795); move CP-19 into a design-side batch with its mechanism rewritten as "generate commands.contract.json from a CLI checkout by walking buildProgram(...).commands, the way src/__tests__/frames-cli.test.ts:53-57 already does", re-anchored to build.py:763 / :4646; and record the RM-12-class knock-on separately.

### S7ac. Decision: how npx hands off to the desktop app

- **Rows:** (none)
- **Depends on:** none
- **Size / approver for the PR body:** S / Teddy decides the route; Ryan approves whichever one becomes a verb
- **Status:** DONE: the app verb shipped as c487048 and PR #58; BM-12 (S7ad) is its named residue

**CLI change.** NOTHING IS BUILT - three options costed, plus the evidence that no mechanism exists today: src/lib/launch.ts only DESCRIBES the executing entry (describeLaunch at launch.ts:14 over the Launch union at :5-10, kinds npx|local|global|source|unknown); there is no spawn, no download and no install-location convention anywhere in the CLI. OPTION A, release-asset download: a new `app` verb (or a setup step) resolves the platform asset from the GitHub release, verifies a checksum, unpacks to a stated location and prints it - costs a network fetch on a verb that has none, a checksum policy, an unpack path outside §4.2's enumerated tree (so machine uninstall must sweep it), and macOS quarantine on an unsigned artifact. OPTION B, bundled binary in the npm tarball: refused on inspection - per-platform binaries in one tarball, and both the files list and the duplicated CI/release must-have arrays would have to carry them. OPTION C, URL hand-off: setup's epilogue and an `app` verb print or open the releases page. Zero mechanism, honest today, and the only option that ships without spending a phase gate.

**App half (connected surfaces, 10.8; ships in the same PR).** Whichever option wins is what the drawn onboarding means by 'open the app'. Until then the app must not claim the CLI can launch it, and CP-O2's onboarding string stays a design-side call.

**Sync rule.** None.

**Tests.** None until a route is chosen. Option C's whole test is one string assertion in setup.test.ts.

**Rationale and risk.** Teddy's decision (d). This is a real fork with a phase gate behind it: TJ-08 ('setup opens the app') is deferred twice in the phase-1 spec, and the costing's reason is exactly this - the launcher needs an install-location convention, which is a phase-2 mechanism decision. It is in the plan so the decision has an owner and a place, not so anything gets built. Option A quietly makes the CLI a package manager for a second artifact, with a sweep obligation and a signing story attached. Option C is the only one that cannot rot, and it is also the only one that ships before M5 gives the app a stable install location.
