## 6. Decisions

**Answered by Teddy in-thread, 2026-09-08 (ledger: `review/2026-09-08-desktop-blank-map/m7-routing/decision-ledger-2026-09-08.md`):** D-BM-1 = B (S7ae ships the progress-frame producer); D-BM-2 = A (tool presence only, offline); D-BM-3 = A with the rider that the join target comes from the invite's generated wizard command and a joiner never types the team; D-BM-4 = A (`declined` on `ls member`); D-BM-5 = A (one S7k PR); D-BM-6 = A (RM-44 subset); D-BM-7 = A (`--runs`); A1 keep CP-30 as a note-composer; A2 confirm key casing before S7y; A3 relabel New skill to Connect; A4 derive RM-13; A5 hold PF-02 and the Follow tooltip's Inbox promise; A6 accept the TJ-02 interim; A7 nothing to decide; A8 the three refused strings stay design-side. Merging: Claude merges. Real data only: hide gap surfaces, show them when M7 connects them.

**Taken by Claude under Teddy's "finish up the M7 and move to implementation" (reversible, each recorded on its batch):** names beside counts on `sync` (S7j); the project heart as a derived chip, no widened `favorites` (S7t); the window floor goes to the design walk (S7x); CP-D1 closed by merits (S7c); MC-01 checked for mootness first (S7aa).

**Still Teddy's:** PF-02's written yes (S7t stays a draft until then). The design walk list: (1) Relabel the Library's New skill call to action to Connect (A3): canvas change plus re-lock; S7z is retired. (2) The `TZ` avatar and the `Terum / SSM / MRF` sidebar literals (`build.py:247-249`, `:265`) on 75 of the 87 locked boards. (3) CP-19's three refused command strings on the boards (`share <path>`, bare `share`, `uninstall deploy-check`): fix the strings, the CLI keeps its refusals (A8). (4) The window floor (960x600 shipped vs 1200x720 or two new boards), critic finding on S7x. (5) CP-03 (session-start sync prompt) as three honest strings (costing:1278); it breaks two bar conditions as code. (6) The BootError rows and the six error boards' fabricated cause sentences (shell hygiene, pixel-moving parts through the walk).

**Ryan's, stated in the S7ad PR body:** the recorded launch PATH (BM-11) versus resolving a login-shell PATH at spawn time on the Rust side.

## 7. Merging and delivery

One batch, one Codex run, one worktree, one branch `codex/m7-<id>`, one PR into `ryanliu-terum/terum-skills` `main`, merged by the implementer after its own gates and the PR's CI checks pass; a dependent batch stacks on its dependency's branch until that merges. Gated batches open as drafts and are never merged by the queue. No direct push to `main`, no hook bypass, no force push. Gates per batch: the root battery (lint, typecheck, tests), the desktop battery (typecheck, lint, vitest, build, Playwright routes, the 87-board fidelity spec with `TERUM_DESIGN_DIR`), `cargo check` when `src-tauri` changes, and the real-data replay of section 1, all serialised on one lock because two batteries thrash this box. The approver column is the person the PR body addresses, not a merge gate. Delivery to a machine: bump `package.json` on `main`, dispatch `release.yml` with `dry_run: true`, download the desktop artifacts; the public `npx` path waits on Ryan's `npm` environment approval.

## 8. First spec outline: S7af, adapter plumbing and the real-data rule

App-only, no CLI change, first in the queue; every batch with an app half stacks on it. Base f8557c4. Files: `desktop/src/backend/tauri/{run,index,bridge}.ts`, `desktop/src/backend/types.ts`, `desktop/src/backend/mock/index.ts`, `desktop/src/components/domain/Sidebar.tsx`, `desktop/src/app/providers.tsx`, the four screens with `COUNTS` literals, `desktop/GAPS.md`.

1. AD-01: carry `value` beside `error` on an ok:false result frame (`desktop/src/backend/tauri/run.ts:72-74`); `status` fails-with-value when any team is unreadable (`status.ts:100`), so without this every status payload row lights only on healthy clones
1. AD-02: the query lifecycle (report step 2): `providers.tsx:11` policy, `({signal})` on every read, no refetch storm on minimise/restore (measured +1 fetch per key per cycle; Settings, Teams = 10 node processes and 15 git children at N=3); a stale board is a previously true board and must not be presented as current
1. AD-03: the served-surfaces declaration and the real-data rule: the adapter declares which read models it serves (a `surfaces` block beside `Capabilities`, `desktop/src/backend/types.ts:8`); `Sidebar.tsx:14` omits a nav group whose model is a gap (Inbox first) and `Shell.tsx:15` already queries capabilities; the mock declares every surface so the 87 locked boards do not move; delete the four hard-coded sidebar `COUNTS` literals (`InboxScreen.tsx:67`, `ShareScreen.tsx:32`, `LibraryScreen.tsx:18`, `MarketplaceScreen.tsx:66`) so a count comes only from `status` or is omitted (`Sidebar.tsx:8` draws the span only when present)
1. AD-04: report bug 12: `read()` (`tauri/index.ts:47-53`) collects print frames and appends them to a failing `Result.error`, as `run.ts:57` already does for stderr
1. AD-05: report bug 15: one `useEffect` in `providers.tsx` does `backend.subscribe(source => queryClient.invalidateQueries(...))` so `ChangeSource` (`tauri/index.ts:39-44,91`) has a consumer and counts refresh after install, uninstall and sync
1. AD-06: report bug 3 and step 5: pass `--team` on every verb, `ref` becomes `<team>/<name>` from a search hit, optional `team` on `ValidateArgs` / `InviteArgs` / `EvalArgs` (`tauri/index.ts:70,72,75,79,82,83,100`); no CLI change
1. AD-07: report bug 4: `cliConnect.optional()` (`tauri/index.ts:22,75`); bare `connect` legitimately succeeds with no value
1. AD-08: report bug 9: drop publish's `?? 'main'` third arm and carry `changed` (`tauri/index.ts:76`) so a no-op publish never reads as a real one
1. AD-09: report bug 11: `(args.ref || args.cwd)` for validate (`tauri/index.ts:83`); today `{ref:'', cwd}` sends `['validate','']` and emits 47 findings over `.git/**`
1. AD-10: report step 7: widen the `SearchHit` mapping (`team`, `category`, `author`, `installs`, `latest`, `endorsed`, `unresolved`; `description:''` until RM-30) and stop rendering the author's email in the results row
1. Re-anchor `desktop/GAPS.md` to f8557c4 and add the PF-08 line (critic).
1. Tests: `run.test.ts` (value carried on ok:false; events kept after the first settle stay S7ag's), `index.test.ts` (print frames appended to a failing error; `--team` on every argv; `cliConnect.optional()`; publish `changed`), `mock.test.ts` (the strict `toEqual` at `:30` gains `surfaces`), a `Sidebar` test (a gap read model hides its group on the real adapter; the mock shows every group), a `providers` test (a settled run invalidates the queries). Acceptance: desktop battery green, 87 of 87 locked boards unchanged, the replay of `--frames status` through `read()` returns a typed gap for `settings` and the sidebar omits Settings and Inbox while the mock shows both.

- NOT VERIFIED: the seam `Receipt`'s full field list against `src/lib/evals/receipt.ts`; S7n (the receipt verb) and S7u (per_case) both assume a CLI-receipt-to-seam-Receipt mapper nobody has written; S7n writes it.

- NOT VERIFIED that the axe gate and the reflow assertion battery (S7x) fit this box beside the fidelity battery; they run under the same lock.

- NOT VERIFIED on this box: the Rust lifecycle bugs (AD-11 to AD-13) cannot be reproduced here (no packaged app, `cargo check` only); S7ag's NOTES.md says so and the native leg on the Windows host is the proof.

- UNPLACED rows after this routing (7): BUNDLE-AJAY, BUNDLE-RYAN, CP-28, EV-14, RM-04, RM-28, RM-50. Each is an app-side SIMPLE row no batch names; S7r (app-only) owns them by default until a batch claims one.

**Critic gaps, verbatim, with their standing against this document's section 4 (the critic read the synthesizer's re-lettered table):**

- (1) ROW COVERAGE - no SIMPLE cli/both row is missing, but the coverage is fragile in three places. I enumerated all 62 (31 cli + 31 both) against the Appendix index: every one appears. Caveats: (a) S7a's rows cell in the section-4 table lists ONLY 'RM-11 (moot)' - PF-03, PF-04, PF-07, PF-09 appear only in the S7a prose paragraph and the Appendix, so a planner reading the table drops four SIMPLE·cli rows including the `profile` verb and the one shared guard change that S7b, S7i and S7t depend on; (b) the same table gives S7a size 0, CLI change 'none', app half 'none', which directly contradicts S7t's dependsOn cell 'S7a's verb'; (c) MC-01 is placed in both S7n and S7aa with opposite rulings and S7n says 'pick one' - the document never picks.
- (2) DISPOSITIONS - all 13 MEDIUM, 3 HARD, 9 CLOSED and 5 DEFERRED rows have one, and the 57 SIMPLE·design rows in section 5 match the extracted set exactly (I diffed them id by id). But section 5's MEDIUM lists silently mix two different verdict columns: CP-23 and CP-S1 are SIMPLE·design rows and MC-01 is SIMPLE·both - they are listed as MEDIUM because their CLI-SIDE cell is MEDIUM. Under that same reading seven more CLI-side-MEDIUM rows are missing from the list (RM-34, RM-35, MC-07, CP-D1, EV-01, RM-13, PF-02); under the row-verdict reading, three rows are miscategorised. Either way the list is not the set the section-1 exit criterion asks for. Fix by saying which verdict column 'MEDIUM' means.
- (3) TWO OF THE TEN GAP() READ MODELS ARE IN NO BATCH - the hardest completeness hole. Section 3 correctly counts 'ten read models still return a hard-coded gap', but its unblocking table maps only eight method names. `settings: async () => gap('Settings')` (desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:62) and `onboarding: async () => gap('Onboarding data')` (:63) appear in no row of that table and in no batch's app-half column. Under the section-1 exit criterion ('its board surface real from desktop/src/backend/tauri'), every Settings board and every Onboarding board still renders the GAPS.md error board after all 29 batches merge. S7r is app chrome and S7w's app half is the setup step map (RM-24), neither of which is the `settings` or `onboarding` read model.
- (3) SIMPLE-BAR - the bar is a PER-ROW classifier and the document never says so, yet batches bundle rows past it. S7a spends two people/<handle>.json field allowances (PF-03 `role`, PF-04 `projects`) plus two new verbs (`profile`, `decline`); S7t spends two more people-file fields plus two flags. With section 7's 'one batch, one PR' cadence, Ryan reviews a diff no single SIMPLE row priced - four new fields in one owner file across two PRs. Not fatal, but state the per-row/per-PR distinction explicitly or Ryan will read the first PR as a bar violation.
- (3) TEAM.JSON - exactly one batch touches a team.json row: S7ab (TJ-02), and it is a genuine three-condition break, which the document discloses and gates. Verified in code: guard.ts:50 routes every team.json change to `guardTeam`, and guard.ts:150-156 admits exactly rows c/d/e then throws. Two follow-ons the doc misses: section 1's exit criterion should say S7ab is OUTSIDE 'M7 complete' (TJ-02 is SIMPLE·design, not in the required cli/both set), and S7k's stated `categories` widening is ambiguous against TJ-12 - if it means writing team.json categories it reopens the same refused table, if it means reading them onto a Result it is free. The doc does not say which.
- (3) TWO ROWS RIDE A BATCH WITH NO STATED TREATMENT. S7q carries CP-D1, whose CLI route the costing refuses on the MERITS not on price ('AND IT SHOULD NOT BE BUILT AT ALL' - team.json already carries `name`), and S7q's prose never mentions CP-D1 at all. S7i carries TJ-10, whose costed route is a §5.1 DOCUMENTATION delta legitimising a hand-maintained `description` key (which survives only because schema.ts:48 is `.passthrough()` - verified), yet S7i's CLI-change cell reads only 'ls --host; invite --cancel; joined'. Both rows are counted as covered while their actual dispositions are invisible.
- (3) S7k HAS TWO APPROVERS ('Ajay (+Ryan: TJ-12, CP-16)'), against bar condition (6). It is also the largest batch (10 rows) and gates the whole evals surface. Split the Ryan rows out or the PR blocks on two reviewers.
- (5) M6 PREREQUISITE - the retirement is SUBSTANTIVELY RIGHT and I verified it: package.json declares no `main`, no `exports` and no `types` (keys are bin, bugs, dependencies, description, devDependencies, engines, files, homepage, keywords, license, name, repository, scripts, type, version), so the app can only drive the CLI as a subprocess and RM-11 buys nothing. The PLACEMENT is broken three ways: RM-11 sits in two batches (S7a 'Retired: exports map' and S7p 'Retired: packaging') and section 9 records the conflict without resolving it; the retirement collapsed S7a's table row and erased its four live rows (above); and section 7 excludes RM-11 from Ryan's count for a DIFFERENT reason than section 4 does - 'already routed to a Mac teammate' vs 'moot'. Those cannot both stand: if it is moot, the teammate is building something nothing needs and should be told to stop. The dependsOn edges are clean - no batch depends on RM-11 - and CP-19's re-home to S7b is correct (the hello frame really does carry `verbs` and `features`, frames.ts:19, :32, :39-42).
- (5) A CONSEQUENCE OF THE RETIREMENT THE DOC DOES NOT DRAW: CP-33 ('Printed commands say @latest while the app would run a bundled library in-process') rests entirely on the in-process-import premise that frame mode just killed, exactly as RM-11's did. CP-33 sits in section 5's design list marked 'blocked' by BRIEF.md:32, with its dead premise unremarked. Retire or re-premise it in the same breath as RM-11.
- EXIT CRITERION IS NOT MEASURABLE AS WRITTEN. Section 1 conjoins '87/87 locked' with 'its board surface real: from desktop/src/backend/tauri where the adapter can reach it'. The fidelity oracle runs the mock/browser mode - that is what 87/87 measures - so a board rendered from the REAL adapter is not covered by the gate the criterion cites, and a batch can turn a board real while 87/87 stays green against mock pixels. The document needs a second acceptance artefact for adapter-rendered boards, or the criterion must drop the 'real' clause.
- UNOWNED RESIDUALS. Three to-dos have no batch: re-anchoring desktop/GAPS.md (verified still headed 'pinned at b5c0507', now 49 lines in the monorepo, not the 37 of the app repo) - section 9 assigns it to 'the first batch that touches it', which is not an owner; PF-08's missing GAPS.md line (section 5 flags it, nobody owns it); and the Appendix's 'twelve keep a design half' list names PF-05 but omits CP-S4, which is a design row actually sitting in S7m's rows column, and claims PF-05 is 'the app half of S7b' - S7b's rows and app-half cells contain no PF-05.
- MINOR BOOKKEEPING. Section 9 lists 'S7f's dependsOn S7e' as an open risk, but section 4's table already gives S7f dependsOn 'none' - the caveat is stale. And section 0.3's 'across 17 batches' for Ryan is not reproducible from section 4, which marks 16 batches purely Ryan plus three mixed (S7t, S7z, S7ab).
- STRONGEST PART, worth preserving in any rewrite: section 8's spec outline. All eight of its code pins verified exact against f8557c4 (install.ts:47-53 member loop, :55-62 project loop, :77 catch, :93-95 pending written, :141 pending cleared, :241 placementHome, :106 its only call site, result.ts:6, sync.ts:308-320 isolation pattern). ## 10. Desktop blank map routed into M7 (2026-09-08)

Source: the desktop blank-map review of 2026-09-08 (REPORT.md, 29 claims verified two ways). The review folder lives outside this repo, so everything M7 needs from it is copied here: the demand list (its section 5.1) verbatim in Appendix B, the two verified bugs with a CLI half (its section 4, rows 1 and 13) as rows, and the batch order that lights the desktop soonest. Every row below was re-confirmed against `src/commands/*.ts` at f8557c4 (v0.1.6) and against the measured frames before it became a batch row: one confirm agent per row, two adversarial refuters on every new, moved or divergent row, one ordering pass. Where a refuter overturned a claim, only the corrected fact appears. "Before" shapes are the last line of the named frames file, real output of the built 0.1.6 CLI against the fixture team.

Three facts govern every row here and are stated once:

- **Frame mode is the only delivery.** The app reaches the CLI as a child process (`desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:39-49`) and reads the verb's result frame `value` (`src/lib/frames.ts:24`, `:171-175`). An exported function, a `ConfigStore` read or a library import delivers nothing. Every costing verdict of the form "the app reads it itself" is void, which is why several costed rows below gain a reader line and why the nine uncatalogued asks exist at all.
- **The adapter drops `value` on a failing result** (`desktop/src/backend/tauri/run.ts:72-74`), and `status` returns failure-with-value whenever any configured team is unreadable (`src/commands/status.ts:100`). Every `status` payload row in this section therefore reaches the app only while every team's clone is healthy. That one-line adapter change ("carry `value` beside `error` on ok:false") is not an M7 batch (section 10.5) but is the first item of the adapter track, and every S7k PR body must say so.
- **Additive fields keep `hello.protocol` at 1** (`docs/frame-protocol.md:61-63`). A new verb must be added to `FRAME_VERBS` (`src/lib/frames.ts:32`) plus an `INVOCATIONS` entry and a stub in `src/__tests__/frames-cli.test.ts` (:23-26, :51), or the hello frame under-advertises it (as it already does for `app`).

### 10.2 New rows: the uncatalogued asks, costed against the bar

Each row was costed on the six SIMPLE conditions at f8557c4, then attacked by two refuters. The edits are paste-ready for the batch spec; "lights" names what turns on once the adapter reads the verb.

**BM-01, `projects` on `LsResult` (`ls`). SIMPLE, batch S7f, Ryan, §6 `ls` bullet (`spec:345`).** No verb enumerates `team.json.projects`; every reader takes the name as input (`ls.ts:76`, `search.ts:41`, `install.ts:57`, `publish.ts:56`, `uninstall.ts:40`, `sync.ts:421`), and `LsResult` (`ls.ts:20`) has no such key in any measured frame. Edit: `projects?: readonly { name: string; skills: readonly string[]; remotes: readonly string[] }[]` on `LsResult` (`remotes` is REQUIRED by `teamSchema`, `schema.ts:48`, so it is not optional); built right after the parse at `ls.ts:32` with a codepoint sort (`readme.ts:47` is the precedent; `localeCompare` is locale-dependent and would not keep frames byte-stable); spread the parsed entry's passthrough keys so TJ-10's hand-maintained `description` (S7p) is not stripped; threaded into `showMember`/`showProject` (typed as the element array, not `LsResult['projects']`, so the member and project forms never emit an `undefined` key) and returned at `:45/:73/:81`. `projects` is absent exactly on the `--local` path (`ls.ts:28` returns before `team.json` is parsed at `:32`): omit, never `[]`. Multi-team: `selectTeam` throws with no `--team` (`config.ts:29`), so the app passes `--team` per team. Tests in `ls.test.ts`: the registry in name order including a project with no skills; absent on `--local`. Lights: the sidebar project rows (`Sidebar.tsx:13`'s literals), `/library/project/:name`, the Marketplace Projects board; per-project counts are one app-side intersection with `LsSkill.id`, no fan-out. RM-26 stays CLOSED (it assumed this enumeration).

**BM-02, `clonePath` on `TeamStatus` (`status`). SIMPLE, S7k, Ryan, §6 `status` bullet (`spec:347`).** `status.ts:45` computes `const clone = store.teamClone(team)` and drops it. Edit: `clonePath: string | null` on `TeamStatus` (`:17-22`), `null` in the detail literal at `:38-41` (the literal is built before `teamClone` can throw on a bad config key, `config.ts:92`), assigned at `:45-46` BEFORE `describeClone` at `:47` so absent/foreign/incomplete clones carry it too. Never inline `teamClone` into the literal: a throw there escapes the per-team catch and kills every remaining team, contradicting `spec:347`. Map to the design's `TEAMS[].clone` (a string, `fixtures/schema.ts:60`), not the CLI's `clone` state object. Tests: the clone-state block at `status.test.ts:119-133` plus a healthy case; the strict `toEqual` at `:102` is the zero-team case. Lights: the clone-path row in Settings ▸ Teams; there is no per-team Show-in-Finder control at f8557c4 (`SettingsContent.tsx:36` renders the value only), so that button is app/design work (AC-08).

**BM-03, `joinCommand` and `joinBlock` on `TeamStatus` (`status`). SIMPLE, S7k, Ryan, §6 `status` bullet.** The block already exists and is exported: `joinCommand` at `invite.ts:54`, `slackBlock` at `:56-58`, printed only inside the GitHub-gated `invite` (`:45`); `setup.ts:18` already imports it. Edit: split `slackBlock` into an exported `joinLines(ownerRepo): readonly string[]` plus the joining wrapper (byte-preserving, invite's stdout unchanged); `joinCommand: string | null; joinBlock: readonly string[] | null` on `TeamStatus`, null in the literal, set after `detail.repository` at `:46` from `githubOwnerRepo(binding.remote)` (`remote.ts`), null on a generic-git remote. Tests: byte-identical to `slackBlock('acme/team')` for a GitHub team; null for generic git. Lights: ShareInvite's copyable block and the Members screen on the success path; the Onboarding join block only once the adapter carries `value` on ok:false (a missing clone makes `status` fail-with-value).

**BM-04, `declined` on `ls member`'s value (`ls`). SIMPLE, S7f, Ryan, §6 `ls` bullet.** REDUCED from the report's "installed list": `installed` per member is RM-03's `installedBy` inverted (one entry per install record, already in S7f), and shipping both projections of the same people-file rows in one batch is the duplicate the inventory already avoids (rows 12 and 13). What no verb returns is the member's `declined` ids (`schema.ts:63`), read at seven sites and returned nowhere. Edit: `member?: { handle: string; declined: Person['declined'] }` on `LsResult` (`ls.ts:20`; add `Person` as a type-only import at `:15`, there is no exported `Scope` type), returned at `:73` from the `member` already found at `:66`. The printed `Installed:` line at `:72` stays. Tests: the member fixture at `ls.test.ts:60-74` with a `declined` id; unknown handle still fails. Lights: with `status` (my handle) and plain `ls` (global set plus `installedBy`), the Inbox `share` row and Onboarding's "later" list; three spawns. Alternative for Ryan (D-BM-4): `declinedBy?: readonly string[]` on `LsSkill` on the default `ls`, filled beside `installCounts` at `:51`, so the share row costs one spawn.

**BM-05, `name` on `UninstalledResult` (`uninstall-skill`). SIMPLE, S7i, Ryan, §6 `uninstall-skill` bullet (`spec:353`) only.** The install half is CLOSED at f8557c4: `InstalledResult.path` (`install.ts:142`) is `join(root, skill.name)` (`placer.ts:52/:78`), so `basename(path)` IS the name, exactly, and the adapter's `name: item.id` (REPORT §4 row 10) is fixable app-side. `UninstalledResult` (`uninstall.ts:16`) is `{ id, team, removed }` with nothing name-bearing. Edit: `name: string | null`, derived inside `uninstallMany` from the matched placement-ledger keys it already reads at `:83` (each key was written as `join(root, skill.name)`), the last path segment of the first match, `null` when `matching` is empty; the literal at `:88`; zero new I/O, no clone read. Tests: `uninstall.test.ts` (the deleted-upstream fixture at `:94` exercises the null arm). Adapter note: `cliUninstalled` is passthrough but must DECLARE the key as optional and fall back to `item.id`, or an older CLI breaks the read.

**BM-06, a result frame on a commander usage error (the bin). SIMPLE, S7d, Ryan, `docs/frame-protocol.md` (the ordering sentence at `:11` and the `result` row at `:19`).** `src/index.ts:69-72` catches `CommanderError`, sets the exit code and writes no result frame; commander runs in `exitOverride` (`cli.ts:40`) so every usage error throws before any action, and the app reports "exited with code 1 before reporting a result" (measured for 20 argv shapes). Edit: in `frames.ts` beside `isDecline`, `COMMANDER_NON_ERRORS = new Set(['commander.help', 'commander.helpDisplayed', 'commander.version'])` (those already wrote their text; rule 3 forbids them over frames) and `attemptedVerb(operands)`: the longest leading non-flag prefix naming a `FRAME_VERBS` entry (two words for the `team` forms), else the first operand, else `terum-skills`, never empty; in the catch, when `channel` exists and the error is a `CommanderError` whose code is not in the set, `channel.result({ verb: attemptedVerb(argv.slice(2)), ok: false, error: error.message, exitCode: 1 })`. Do NOT guard on `channel.closed` (stdin is already closed when the adapter and the harness run) and prefer a `reported` flag over re-emitting. `isDecline` false-positive: an unknown option literally named `--declined` would be stamped `declined: true`; suppress the stamp for commander errors. Tests: `frames.test.ts` (`attemptedVerb` cases), `bin.test.ts` or `frames-cli.test.ts` (an unknown option under `--frames` ends in exactly one result frame, exit 1; `--help` and `--version` still emit none).

**BM-07, stop the `--frames` and `--hook` scans at the first `--` (the bin). SIMPLE, S7d, Ryan, `docs/frame-protocol.md:7` (the position-independence paragraph gains "before the first `--`").** `index.ts:42-43` scan and filter the whole argv for `FRAMES_FLAG` and `:60` scans it for `--hook`, so `--frames search -- --hook` returns a `verb: "sync"` refusal frame and an operand literally named `--frames` is eaten. Edit: `const separator = process.argv.indexOf('--')`; scan and filter only the prefix; recompute `argv.indexOf('--')` after the filter (the separator shifts left by the removed matches) and slice `argv` to it before the `--hook` test at `:60`. Rule 2 (`:35`) stays literally true. Tests in `bin.test.ts` (an unconfigured HOME makes `search` return `success([])`): `--frames search -- --hook` ends in a `search` result, `search -- --frames` likewise. Coupled to BM-06 in one commit: alone, `sync -- --hook` regresses from a wrong-verb frame to none (`sync` has no `allowExcessArguments`, `cli.ts:129`). Inert in the app today (nothing writes `--`); it is what makes the adapter's argv reorder (REPORT §3 step 5) safe.

**BM-08, bound `ls`'s per-skill git fan-out (`ls`). SIMPLE, S7f, Ryan, §6 `ls` bullet, RECONCILED with RM-10.** `ls.ts:52` runs an unbounded `Promise.all` with one `latestTree` child per skill (`:59`); `search.ts:51-52` batches eight. The report's "142 + 2.46N ms" motive does not hold as a latency claim (followup-2.md:74/104: the slope is per child, peak concurrency with a real git is already 2); the defensible framing is a documented cap matching `search`, bounded process pressure when git is slow. But RM-10 (S7d, "export the batched `skillTrees` reader", costing:793) names the identical defect at the identical lines and its mechanism, ONE `git ls-tree <ref>:skills` for every version (`teamRepo.ts:284-286`), strictly dominates a chunk of eight. Under frame mode RM-10's export has no consumer except this call site. Resolution: RM-10 MOVES from S7d to S7f; `listSkills` consumes `skillVersions()` once (its call-site half is BM-08); the chunk-of-eight is the fallback only if Ryan declines RM-10. Written INSIDE RM-49's rewrite of `ls.ts:49-62` as one commit, and the bound must cover RM-38's second git call per skill (`git log -1`), or peak concurrency is 16, not 8. Tests: a runner spy asserting the peak in flight (the first injected-Runner team-path test in `ls.test.ts`; the three existing injections are `--local` runs that never reach `listSkills`).

**BM-09, the machine ledger on `StatusResult` (`status`). SIMPLE, S7k, Ryan, §6 `status` bullet.** RM-06 was costed CLOSED because "config.placements is readable via ConfigStore", an in-process route frame mode retired; RM-07 (S7j) exports the private `approved()` predicate (`sync.ts:364`), equally unreachable. No measured frame carries `placements`, `approvals` or `shared`. Edit: `ledger: { placements: { path, id, team, version, scope, placed_at }[]; approvals: { id, grants, approved_at }[]; shared: { id, source, team }[] }` on `StatusResult` (`:23`), declared beside `teams` at `:28` so all three returns carry a well-formed value, filled right after the config read at `:32` and BEFORE the `--team` selection at `:33` (machine-wide, never narrowed); `fingerprint` omitted. Distinct from RM-44: `ls --local` is scoped to the discovered roots (global plus the cwd project), the machine-wide count Settings ▸ This machine draws lives only in `config.json`. Consequences: RM-07's CLI half is superseded (the app compares `approvals[id].grants` with RM-02's `grantsHash`), so RM-07 moves to S7k as this row's consumer and S7j loses it; RM-06 flips CLOSED to app-side over BM-09 (S7r). Privacy: `config.json` is machine-local and the reader is the local app.

**BM-10, the identity block on `StatusResult` (`status`), MC-11's read half. SIMPLE, S7k, Ryan, §6 `status` bullet.** `configSchema` declares `default_handle`, `email`, `display_name`, `github` (`schema.ts:95-98`); `setIdentity` (`auth.ts:176-180`) is their only writer; `status` loads the whole config at `:32` and returns none of it. **BM-11, the launch PATH on `app.json` (`app`), bug row 1's CLI half. SIMPLE, S7ad (new), Ryan, spec §4.2 (`:103`).** `AppState` (`app.ts:58`) is exactly `{ schema, node, entry, version, writtenAt }`, written at `:134`; the shell hands the CLI the inherited environment (`lib.rs:47-49`) while `runner.ts` spawns bare `git` and `gh`, and with gh off PATH `invite` returns "GitHub CLI (gh) is not installed" (measured). Edit: `path: string | null` after `entry` (`process.env.PATH ?? null`, precedent `index.ts:37`; Node reads Windows `Path` case-insensitively; the string is `;`-delimited there and must be replayed verbatim), an `AppArgs.path` test knob; `app.json` is machine-local run state, never an owner file, and `app` stays OUT of `FRAME_VERBS` (its seam is `read_app_state`, not a frame). SIMPLE, S7ad, Ryan, §4.2, GATED (decision D-BM-3).** `setup` prints "Continuing in the app. Join `<target>` there." and returns with no team work done (`setup.ts:113-115`); `app.ts:139` opens the app with no argument and `app.json` carries no target. ### 10.5 Excluded from M7, and where each goes instead

- SUPERSEDED BY 10.8 (Teddy, 2026-09-08 17:40 UTC: the adapter halves are M7 rows now). Kept for the record: the adapter work in `desktop/src/backend/tauri` (the review's basic set, steps 1 to 4: implement `status()`, the query lifecycle, `library()` with its pixel-neutral screen half, `skill()`; the adapter halves of steps 5, 6, 7, 12 and 13; and bugs 2 to 12, 14 and 15 of its section 4) goes to the adapter track: a spec of its own under `desktop/` (Codex batches in the desktop tree, gated by the desktop battery, `desktop/AGENTS.md` invariants binding), sequenced after the end-to-end review's CLI leg. Its first three items are the ones every payload row above depends on: carry `value` beside `error` on an ok:false result frame (`run.ts:72-74`), add `path` and `target` to `appStateSchema` (`bridge.ts:13`) with `cli_spawn` setting PATH, and declare the new keys on the closed zod objects (`cliValidate`, `cliInstalled`, `cliUninstalled`) instead of relying on passthrough.
- PARTLY SUPERSEDED BY 10.8 (the four `COUNTS` literals are AD-03; the rest stands). The React screen fixes listed with those steps (the four hard-coded sidebar `COUNTS` literals at `InboxScreen.tsx:67`, `ShareScreen.tsx:32`, `LibraryScreen.tsx:18`, `MarketplaceScreen.tsx:66`; the Skill error board's fabricated crumbs and diagnosis at `SkillScreen.tsx:41` and the `actionError`/load-error split at `:33`; the "passed on share, 12 days ago" caption at `:26`; `Analytics.tsx:10`'s provenance default; the `reports.tsx` literals; `InboxScreen.tsx:21`'s recipient; the `white-space:nowrap` `ErrorLine`; gating the disable Switch on `disablePerMachine`) go to the shell-hygiene track: pixel-neutral edits only, bundled with the design-strings closure (D16, strings first). Anything among them that moves pixels on a locked board goes through the design walk instead (canvas patch, re-render, Codex spec, re-lock), never through an M7 PR.
- The two design calls stay with the design walk: (a) the `TZ` avatar and the `Terum / SSM / MRF` sidebar literals are the canvas's own (`build.py:247-249`, `:265`) on 75 of the 87 locked boards, so removing them is a canvas change plus a re-lock, Teddy's call; (b) the Onboarding `BootError` board's invented rows (`OnboardingScreen.tsx:79,83`) need an absent state that is not drawn (D9/D10 board work with Teddy) before the screen logic can stop inventing them.
- **Teddy's rule, 2026-09-08 (shell-hygiene track, first item, before the native e2e leg): the real app shows only real data; a surface whose read model is a gap on the real adapter is hidden, not stubbed, and the Inbox is the first case.** Today `desktop/src/components/domain/Sidebar.tsx:14` draws the Inbox group (Inbox, Pushes, Updates, Alerts) unconditionally, while every real-adapter read model, `inbox()` included, returns the typed gap at `desktop/src/backend/tauri/index.ts:32` (`ok:false`, "not available from terum-skills yet"). Mechanism: the adapter declares which read models it serves (a `surfaces` block beside `Capabilities`, `types.ts:8`, or a per-model probe), and the shell, which already queries `capabilities` at `Shell.tsx:15`, omits a nav group whose model is a gap; the mock keeps every surface, so the 87 locked boards do not move. Consequence: a nav entry reappears on the real adapter only when the adapter track maps that model, never from a CLI batch alone (S7f lights Library and Skill detail, S7k Settings and the footer, S7n/S7o/S7m the Inbox's derivable rows; the Inbox feed itself, IB-01, stays app-only in S7r). Not an M7 row.
- Batch S7a (the exports map, RM-11) stays retired by Ryan's frame-mode decision (80054f4): the app reaches the CLI as a child process over frames, and CP-19's inventory rides the hello frame's verbs and features.

### 10.7 Corrections from the second refutation pass (binding on the batch specs)

Thirteen rows lost their refute stage to a schema cap in the first pass and were refuted afterwards (26 agents, none failed). Anchor slips were fixed in place above; these are the material corrections an implementer must apply.

- **RM-39.** Gate the placed-copy snapshot on the entry's inspection: a top-level symlink at a ledger path is still a row (`local-skills.ts:88-94`, `ls.ts:131-135`) and `snapshotSkillDirectory` would read THROUGH the link, which `spec:345` forbids; return no health for `inspection.kind === 'rejected'` (or mirror sync's `inspect(path, true)` gate at `sync.ts:250`). Drop `placement-missing` from the enum: a ledger placement whose folder is gone produces no row at all (`local-skills.ts:74`), so it is a MISSING ROW the S7g paragraph must name, not a health value. Split "the skill id is no longer in the clone" (`gone-from-repo`, from `snapshot.ids` at `ls.ts:125`) from `unknown` (unreadable clone). Use `spec:355`'s own vocabulary: `up-to-date`, not `ok`. `ls.test.ts:209` already pins "no git on `--local`"; do not write a runner-injection test for `showLocal` (it never receives a runner).
- **RM-08.** `src/__tests__/cli.test.ts:266` stubs `update` as `success(undefined)` under `CliVerbs`, so it must return an `UpdateReport`; wire the adapter through `read()` with a `.passthrough()` schema replacing the gap at `tauri/index.ts:84-85` (adapter track); CP-40 (same batch) deletes `COMMUNITY_URL` in `constants.ts` while `update.ts:7` keeps importing `RELEASE_PROBE_POLICY` from it.
- **RM-02.** The `allowed-tools` key is `schema.ts:115`; search's all-teams-failed gate is `search.ts:76`; per-row degradation is `skills.ts:34-36`; the malformed-grant shape is asserted at `schema.test.ts:25`.
- **EV-07.** The seam `ValidateResult` (`desktop/src/backend/types.ts:53`) is implemented by both backends, so the mock (`mock/index.ts:65`, pinned by a strict `toEqual` at `mock.test.ts:30`) changes too (adapter track). Warnings-only skills DO succeed (`validate.ts:50`, `hygiene.ts:95` HYG6), so the CLI half lights the warn row on ok:true; the error rows still wait for the adapter's ok:false value carry, so S7m depends on that adapter item. `validate.test.ts` is 55 lines: test (a) extends `:13-23` inside the describe, test (b) the top-level `it.each` at `:39-55`. Declare `let assessment: HygieneAssessment` at `validate.ts:39`.

Locals are `placed :91`, `changed :92`, `teams :93`; the stamp loop is `:335-350` with `writeStamp` at `:348`; `execute.ts` gate at `:28`, `isHookSync` at `:49-50`; `unreachable` is pushed at `sync.ts:151`.

### 10.8 Connected surfaces (Teddy, 2026-09-08 17:40 UTC): the adapter halves fold into M7

Teddy, in-thread: "The real app should only be showing real data. If the inbox does not work it should not be there, visible just yet." and, on when a surface returns: "Once it is covered by M7 implemented, they should show, since they should be connected." This section is binding over 10.5's first two bullets and over every "adapter track" reference above; the M7 document's own definition row (every SIMPLE row shipped with its app half on `desktop/src/backend/tauri/` and `mock/`; the ten `gap()` read models retire) already said the same thing, and the queue now implements it.

**The rule.** A batch is done when its surface shows real data end to end: the CLI half, the adapter mapping of the verb's result frame `value` into the seam DTO (every new key declared on the closed zod objects, every new field `?? null`), the mock where the seam changes, and the served-surfaces flag flipped for any read model the batch completes. The real app never renders a design constant, a sample value or a fabricated caption; an undelivered field is null and its slot renders empty; a surface whose read model is still a gap stays hidden from the sidebar. Everything stays pixel-neutral on the mock, so the 87 locked boards do not move. A surface reappears in the PR that first serves its read model from real data, with later batches filling fields that were null.

**Two new app-only batches, first in the queue.** S7af, adapter plumbing and the real-data rule (AD-01 to AD-10), and S7ag, the Rust child lifecycle (AD-11 to AD-13). Every other batch that is not MOOT or DONE depends on S7af and stacks on its branch until it merges; S7ad also stacks on S7ag because both edit `lib.rs`. The review's basic set (REPORT.md section 3) and its section-4 bugs are placed as AD rows: steps 1 and 12 (`status()`, `settings()`) ride S7k as AD-20 and AD-24; steps 3 and 4 (`library()`, `skill()`) ride S7f as AD-21 and AD-22; step 10's mapping rides S7g as AD-23; step 13 (`roster()`, `catalog()`) rides S7b as AD-25 and AD-26; bug 13's argv order rides S7d as AD-17; bugs 1 and 14 ride S7ad as AD-19 and AD-18; bugs 2 and 10 ride S7i as AD-14 and AD-16; bug 8 rides S7m as AD-15. The Inbox has no honest step today (nothing is derivable without IB-02, RM-02 and RM-30); it returns when S7r serves the feed. Onboarding stays unreachable until S7r's routing rule and a screen that calls `backend.setup` exist.

**Order (replaces 10.4's numbering; the queue's args carry it):** S7af, S7ag, S7f, S7g, S7ad, S7k, S7d, S7c, S7b, S7e, S7h, S7i, S7j, S7ae, S7l, S7m, S7n, S7o, S7p, S7q, S7r, S7s, S7t, S7u, S7v, S7w, S7x, S7y, S7aa, S7ab; skipped S7z (MOOT, A3), S7a (MOOT), S7ac (DONE); drafts S7t (PF-02 gated) and S7ab (Ryan-gated). Simulated: 30 batches, 151 rows placed once each, zero dependency violations.

**Gates.** Every batch with an app half runs the desktop battery (typecheck, lint, vitest, build, Playwright routes, then the 87-board fidelity spec with `TERUM_DESIGN_DIR`), serialised with the root battery under one lock; a Rust change adds `cargo check` per `.planning/research/2026-09-07-desktop-app-tauri-build.md`. Slower per batch than CLI-only PRs, and correct.

**What stays out of M7 (10.5 still applies to these):** the React screen fixes that are not data (the Skill error board's fabricated crumbs and diagnosis, the caption literals, `Analytics.tsx:10`'s provenance default, the `reports.tsx` literals, the `ErrorLine` wrapping, the disable Switch gating) remain shell hygiene with the design-strings closure, except the four sidebar `COUNTS` literals, which are fake data and die in S7af (AD-03); the `TZ` avatar and sidebar literals and the BootError rows stay with the design walk; S7a stays retired.

**AD rows (the delta an implementer applies; file:line from REPORT.md sections 3 and 4, verified 2026-09-08 against 898a795 / f8557c4):**

- **AD-01.** carry `value` beside `error` on an ok:false result frame (`desktop/src/backend/tauri/run.ts:72-74`); `status` fails-with-value when any team is unreadable (`status.ts:100`), so without this every status payload row lights only on healthy clones
- **AD-02.** the query lifecycle (report step 2): `providers.tsx:11` policy, `({signal})` on every read, no refetch storm on minimise/restore (measured +1 fetch per key per cycle; Settings, Teams = 10 node processes and 15 git children at N=3); a stale board is a previously true board and must not be presented as current
- **AD-03.** the served-surfaces declaration and the real-data rule: the adapter declares which read models it serves (a `surfaces` block beside `Capabilities`, `desktop/src/backend/types.ts:8`); `Sidebar.tsx:14` omits a nav group whose model is a gap (Inbox first) and `Shell.tsx:15` already queries capabilities; the mock declares every surface so the 87 locked boards do not move; delete the four hard-coded sidebar `COUNTS` literals (`InboxScreen.tsx:67`, `ShareScreen.tsx:32`, `LibraryScreen.tsx:18`, `MarketplaceScreen.tsx:66`) so a count comes only from `status` or is omitted (`Sidebar.tsx:8` draws the span only when present)
- **AD-04.** report bug 12: `read()` (`tauri/index.ts:47-53`) collects print frames and appends them to a failing `Result.error`, as `run.ts:57` already does for stderr
- **AD-05.** report bug 15: one `useEffect` in `providers.tsx` does `backend.subscribe(source => queryClient.invalidateQueries(...))` so `ChangeSource` (`tauri/index.ts:39-44,91`) has a consumer and counts refresh after install, uninstall and sync

- **AD-09.** report bug 11: `(args.ref || args.cwd)` for validate (`tauri/index.ts:83`); today `{ref:'', cwd}` sends `['validate','']` and emits 47 findings over `.git/**`
